# SaaS reconciliation — the eight source audits

Raw per-dimension findings behind `reconciliation.md`. Each audit was adversarially verified by a second agent; `verified` carries those verdicts. **A finding marked REFUTED must not be acted on.**

## tenancy

**Verdict.** The founder's "deviated" claim is HALF TRUE, and the halves are cleanly separable. The DATA MODEL is genuinely multi-tenant and always was: `org_id` is on every event envelope, every one of the 12 `kernel.*` tables, and every fan-out key since Wave 0; the hierarchy (org → N branches → N devices, org → N users) matches the SaaS-correct shape and matches the stated billing unit (`restaurant-os.md:108`, PKR 8,000/branch/month). Commit 8659587's claims check out: `kernel.orgs`/`branches`/`users` exist (`drizzle/0010`, `0011`), four provisioning commands are declared in `services/sync-gateway/package.json:11-14`, and the `catalog.runDayEnd` cross-tenant write is really fixed (`catalog-router.ts:271-273` now calls `runDueForOrg`). What deviated is the PRODUCT AROUND the model. There is no way to become a tenant except shell access to the service host and `DATABASE_URL`; `ops/README.md:1-4` is titled "install RestOS in one restaurant" and `ops/id.sh` mints exactly one org id per deployment; `01-F60`'s enabled `(branch, channel)` set is a per-PROCESS env var (`server.ts:401-402,451`), so one API host structurally cannot serve two restaurants with different branch topologies — the isolation suite's own header says so at `tenant-isolation.test.ts:78-85`; all tenants' staged day-end edits live in one process-local `Map` (`server.ts:442`); the org lifecycle `suspended` is stored and reported and enforced by nothing (`services/api/src/tenancy.ts:36-38`); and billing/plans/quotas/metering are, per `specs/15-platform-admin.md:182`, "deliberately NOT specified anywhere in this corpus". On ENFORCEMENT the picture is inverted from what one would expect: the device plane (WebSocket) is structurally isolated and well-tested against real Postgres with two live tenants, while the cloud plane — the one a SaaS sells — rests on a convention repeated at 14 call sites, with the repo's own measurement showing the domain-level guard beneath it is unreachable and kills zero tests. There is zero Postgres RLS, zero per-tenant schemas, zero foreign keys, one DB role, one connection pool, and 31 hand-written `org_id = $` predicates a developer must remember.

### Findings

- **Cloud-plane data isolation is a per-call-site convention, not a structural guarantee. All 14 gated tRPC resolvers hand-pass `ctx.subject.org_id` into a port; nothing at boot, in the type system, or in CI prevents a new resolver from passing `input.org_id` instead. The boot gate that DOES exist (`assertEveryProcedureIsGated`, server.ts:133) checks only that a procedure passed through `can()`, never where its data org came from.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* 16 procedures total, enumerated by `grep -aon '^\s*\([a-zA-Z]*\): \(authorized("...")\|sessionProcedure\|publicProcedure\)' services/api/src/*.ts`: 1 public (router.ts:52 auth.login), 1 session-only (router.ts:87 whoami), 14 `authorized(...)` — catalog-router.ts:127,134,165,179,190,219,244,271; device-router.ts:40,76; summary-router.ts:83; router.ts:133,169,178. Org derivation measured at services/api/src/router.ts:89,143,171,180; catalog-router.ts:135,166,180,203,222,247,272; device-router.ts:41,79; summary-router.ts:86 — 14/14 read `ctx.subject.org_id`, 0/16 read org from request input. The authorization SCOPE is structural (trpc.ts:267-271 builds `{org_id: ctx.subject.org_id}` inside the middleware); the DATA scope is not.
- **The permission matrix's org arm — `01-F71` enforcement point (a), the defence-in-depth layer under the cloud plane — is unreachable from `services/api` and kills zero tests there. Cloud-plane isolation therefore rests on exactly ONE mechanism (point (b)), with no second lock that any host-level test can observe.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* packages/domain/src/permissions.ts:483 (`reportScope`), :508 (`can`), :575 (`canPayOut`) each hold `if (subject.org_id !== scope.org_id)`. packages/domain/src/__acceptance__/tenant-isolation-matrix.test.ts:17-28 records the measurement: with `can()`'s org arm deleted, and again with `reportScope`'s, **0 of 287 services/api tests fail**, because trpc.ts:269 builds every scope from the subject so `subject.org_id !== scope.org_id` is never true on that plane. Its own out-of-tree matrix (M-a1..M-a3, lines 43-47) kills 4/2/1 by calling the functions directly. Commit 8659587's message states the same finding.
- **The gateway's `/internal` surface — the only path by which `services/api` reads or writes any tenant's catalog, ledger, devices and directory — takes `org_id` from the REQUEST on all 8 routes, behind a single shared bearer (`PUBLISH_TOKEN`) that is identical for every tenant. Any holder of that one secret reads and writes every tenant, and the gateway has no person-level check at all.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* services/sync-gateway/src/publish-http.ts:244-258 (one `onRequest` hook, one `timingSafeEqual` against `deps.publishSecret`), routes at :260 (catalog/publish), :283 (catalog/published), :295 + :308 (org-events), :337 (tenancy), :354 (devices), :380 (devices/revoke), :411 (ledger/window). Org arrives via `OrgQuery` (:83) or a body field (:60,:76,:95,:107) on every one. services/sync-gateway/CLAUDE.md states the boundary outright: "This service authorizes services, never people … A holder of PUBLISH_TOKEN can POST this route directly and bypass the matrix entirely." The same secret is `SYNC_GATEWAY_TOKEN` in services/api (server.ts:428-436) and is one value in ops/env/cloud.env.example for the whole deployment.
- **There is no Postgres row-level security, no per-tenant schema, no per-tenant role, and no foreign keys anywhere. Isolation in storage is 31 hand-written `org_id = $` predicates across 7 files, plus 12 tables sharing one `kernel` schema on one connection pool.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* `grep -rain 'row level security|enable row|create policy|current_setting('` over services/, packages/, ops/, scripts/, specs/ returns ZERO hits. `grep -ac 'references(' services/sync-gateway/src/schema.ts` = 0 (deliberate — `01-F68` at specs/01-kernel-sync.md:162 forbids a ledger FK forever). One `pgSchema("kernel")` at schema.ts:35; 12 tables (schema.ts:38,64,70,98,137,191,217,274,297,369,408,462). Predicate count per file: gateway.ts 12, registry.ts 6, catalog.ts 5, tenancy.ts 4, day-ledger.ts 2, org-events.ts 1, quarantine-query.ts 1 = 31.
- **The device/WebSocket plane IS structurally isolated, and this is the strongest part of the system. Session org comes from a signed token verified against the registry, never from anything the device says afterwards; a mismatched envelope is quarantined verbatim rather than dropped; stored rows carry the SESSION's org, not the claimed one; and fan-out is keyed by a structured pair so `("ab","c")` and `("a","bc")` cannot collide.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* services/sync-gateway/src/auth.ts:126-131 (org/branch/device from JWT claims); gateway.ts:415-434 (claims must match hello identity; `readRegistryRow(db, claims.org_id, claims.device_id)`; unregistered/revoked/branch-mismatch all refuse); gateway.ts:449 (`orgId: claims.org_id` is the only org the session ever has); gateway.ts:782-799 (`envelope.org_id !== session.orgId → "org_mismatch"` quarantine); gateway.ts:891 (`insert into kernel.events … values (…, ${session.orgId}, ${session.branchId}, …)` — the claimed org is never what is stored); gateway.ts:284 (`branchKey = JSON.stringify([orgId, branchId])`). Two-tenant coverage against real Postgres: services/sync-gateway/src/__acceptance__/isolation-regression.test.ts:9-15 connects both tenants and asserts B receives zero `event_batch` from A's push.
- **The `runDayEnd` class of defect — an unscoped sweep over every org filtered only at the answer — is closed at the procedure but the SHAPE still ships in two places. `services/api/src/server.ts:506-515` runs the unscoped `runDue()` on an interval (sanctioned: no subject, platform's own schedule) and `services/jobs/src/index.ts:145-168` sweeps `everyOrg(db)`. The jobs sweep is read-only by design, so it carries no write hazard, but both are one careless edit away from the original defect and neither has a two-tenant assertion.** [MINOR]
  - *evidence:* services/api/src/catalog-router.ts:271-273 now calls `ctx.catalog.scheduler.runDueForOrg(ctx.subject.org_id)`; the header at :255-269 documents the original defect verbatim. The surviving unscoped caller is server.ts:506-515 with its justification at :491-495. services/jobs/src/index.ts:133-136 `everyOrg` = `select org_id from kernel.org_sequences`, consumed by `auditEveryOrg` at :145-168; the read-only constraint is a COMMENT at :128-131 ("the Auditor writes nothing, ever, and neither may its host"), not a mechanism. `grep -arn 'forEach|for (const org|runDue|orgs\b' services/jobs/src/*.ts` returns exactly one hit (index.ts:146).
- **`01-F60`'s enabled `(branch, channel)` set is per-PROCESS, not per-org, so one `services/api` host cannot serve two restaurants with different branch topologies. This is the single largest architectural blocker to shared-tenancy hosting, and the isolation suite is forced to declare a union of both tenants' branches to exist at all.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* services/api/src/server.ts:401-402 reads `ENABLED_BRANCHES`/`ENABLED_CHANNELS` from env; :451 puts them on the one host-wide `CatalogDeps`. catalog-router.ts:122-124: "The answer is the DEPLOYMENT's set rather than a per-org lookup … `00 §7`'s layer-2 config plane does not exist, so one process serves one set." services/api/src/__acceptance__/tenant-isolation.test.ts:78-85 states it as a limit it cannot assert against: "holding it per-process means one process cannot serve two restaurants with different branch topologies — but it is a documented shape, not a broken guard." ops/env/cloud.env.example: `ENABLED_BRANCHES=` with "⚠ ENABLED_BRANCHES must CONTAIN the till's RESTOS_BRANCH_ID."
- **All tenants' staged day-end catalog edits live in one process-local `Map` in the PRODUCTION composition root. A restart loses every tenant's pending edits, and `14-F28`'s "cancellable until they land" promise for a tenant whose owner never presses anything rests on one `setInterval` in one process.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* services/api/src/server.ts:442 `staged: createMemoryStagedEditStore(),` — inside `start()`, beside the two ports that WERE fixed to reach the gateway (`publisher`/`ledger` at :449-450). The comment at :497-503 admits it: "a pending day-end edit still does not survive a restart, and a host that never runs `start()` never lands one." `createMemoryStagedEditStore` is declared in services/api/src/catalog.ts:140-172 as a per-process `Map` keyed by org.
- **An org's lifecycle status `suspended` is storable, reportable and enforced by absolutely nothing — and no command can set it. A tenant that stops paying cannot be turned off through any surface this product has.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* packages/domain/src/tenancy.ts:182 `ORG_STATUSES = ["active", "suspended"]`; schema.ts:379 stores it. services/api/src/tenancy.ts:36-38: "**It gates nothing here**: no FR anywhere makes a lifecycle state refuse a request." services/sync-gateway/src/__acceptance__/tenancy-read-http.test.ts:148-152 asserts it is "reported, never enforced here". services/sync-gateway/src/create-org.ts:134-135 explicitly REFUSES `--status suspended`; `grep -rain suspended` over packages/services/apps returns 13 hits, all declarations, comments or tests — zero enforcement branches. package.json declares no suspend/unsuspend command (services/sync-gateway/package.json:8-16).
- **There is no tenant self-service, no billing, no metering, no plan and no quota anywhere in the code — and the corpus says this is a deliberate un-decision, not an oversight. Provisioning is 7 shell commands requiring both shell access on the service host and `DATABASE_URL`.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* specs/15-platform-admin.md:182: "**Self-service signup** — 15-F26 rules it out … **Related and deliberately NOT specified anywhere in this corpus: billing, subscriptions, plans, quotas and metering-to-invoice.**" apps/platform-admin/src/index.ts is `export {};` (2-line scaffold stub). The only provisioning surface is services/sync-gateway/package.json:10-16 — migrate, create-org, create-branch, create-owner, list-tenancy, provision-device, revoke-device. packages/domain/src/tenancy.ts:196 explicitly excludes "anything to do with plan, billing, quota or metering" from `OrgRecord`. restaurant-os.md:108 states the model (PKR 8,000/branch/month) that nothing implements.
- **The entire operator kit is written for ONE restaurant per deployment, and the runbook says so in its first sentence. This — not the schema — is the concrete shape of the founder's "deviated".** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* ops/README.md:1-4: "Everything needed to install RestOS **in one restaurant** and keep it running". ops/id.sh:3 "mint **this restaurant's** ids and secrets ONCE, into one file"; :26-32 refuses to re-run because "These ids are baked into every device token already minted". ops/env/cloud.env.example carries one `ORG_ID`, one `ENABLED_BRANCHES`, one `DEVICE_TOKEN_SECRET`, one `PUBLISH_TOKEN` for the whole host, and warns "The org id you provision with `create-org --org <id>` must equal every device's RESTOS_ORG_ID … NOTHING CHECKS THIS." ops/systemd/ ships one unit per process, not per tenant.
- **`kernel.events.id` is a GLOBAL primary key and the push-path dedupe SELECT carries no org predicate, so all tenants share one event-id namespace. An id already stored under org A, pushed by a device of org B, is quarantined as `id_content_divergence` and its lamport slot is filled — the org-B event is lost, permanently, by a row belonging to another tenant.** [MINOR]
  - *evidence:* services/sync-gateway/src/schema.ts:41 `id: text("id").primaryKey()` (org appears only in the two UNIQUE constraints at :51,:53, never in the PK). services/sync-gateway/src/gateway.ts:637-638 `select id, envelope from kernel.events where id in ${incomingIds}` — no `org_id` filter. gateway.ts:855-857 quarantines on that global hit and passes `stream`, which fills the origin's slot (gateway.ts:625-628). The same-content path at :809-815 is NOT exploitable: `sameContent` (gateway.ts:139-148) strips only `server_received_at`/`global_seq`, so `org_id` is compared and a cross-org match is impossible. Practical likelihood is negligible under UUIDv7; the structural point is that the ledger's primary key is not tenant-scoped.
- **`kernel.users.email` is unique case-folded GLOBALLY rather than per org, so one human cannot hold an account in two tenants. For a SaaS with multi-branch groups and vendor onboarding staff, this is a first-week constraint, and the migration itself files it as an unanswered question.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* services/sync-gateway/drizzle/0011_tenancy_users.sql: `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX "users_email_lower_uq" ON "kernel"."users" USING btree (lower("email"))` — no `org_id` in the index. Its own comment: "EMAIL IS UNIQUE CASE-FOLDED AND **GLOBALLY**, not per org … The residual is stated: one human wanting an account in two orgs needs two emails, which is `backoffice-catalog.md` Q3's open multi-org question and not this migration's to answer." services/api/src/router.ts:53 `ctx.store.findByEmail(input.email)` takes an email and nothing else.
- **`services/api/src/__acceptance__/tenant-isolation.test.ts` is a genuinely strong two-tenant register — 23 `it(` declarations generating 40+ cases, every leak assertion paired with a control — but it attacks ONE plane through IN-MEMORY stores. It never crosses the gateway's `/internal` boundary, never touches Postgres, and never exercises the real adapters that carry org over the wire.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* 1189 lines. Structure: §0 controls (5), §1 (b) 7 READS (test:726-734) × 5 ATTACKS (test:737-774: org in body, org+subject id, three org headers, foreign branch_id, role claim) = 35 generated cases + 2 controls, §2 (a) 5, §3 write sweep 3, §4 runDayEnd regression 6. Stores are memory by explicit design (header rule 1, lines 48-55: "a fixture that gives each tenant its own store proves nothing"). NOT attacked: the 8 `/internal` routes, real Postgres predicates, `catalog.enabled` (declared out of scope at :78-85), login/account-enumeration across orgs. Points (c) and (d) are covered elsewhere and measured (header :33-41), so the gap is the API↔gateway seam, which no two-tenant test crosses.
- **The isolation suite's own doc comment is stale by one: it says "Four axes" and "the SAME four attacks" while the `ATTACKS` array has five entries. Harmless to the assertions, but this repo treats a hand-copied count as a defect class, and this one is in the file that the whole isolation claim rests on.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* services/api/src/__acceptance__/tenant-isolation.test.ts:719 "Four axes, because those are the four a real caller controls" and :804 "the SAME four attacks"; the array at :737-774 has 5 entries (name fields at :743, :748, :753, :758, :764). Measured: `(s.match(/^    name: /gm)||[]).length === 5`.
- **Provisioning writes no event and records no actor, on any of the seven commands, so a SaaS has no audit trail of tenant creation, device admission or (via CLI) revocation — in an append-only product whose whole premise is an attributable ledger.** [MINOR]
  - *evidence:* services/sync-gateway/CLAUDE.md, tenancy-command bullet: "⚠ NONE OF THEM EMITS AN EVENT, AND `15-F4`/`15-F3` SAY THEY SHOULD … So a tenant created today has no ledger record and no attribution." Same for `provision-device` (`device.registered` still unemitted — services/api/CLAUDE.md, `14-F12` section) and for CLI revocation. The reasoning is sound (`OrgEvent.actor_user_id` could only be `null` from a shell), which is exactly why the fix is a console, not a flag. Partial closure exists on ONE path: `devices.revoke` through services/api DOES record an actor (device-router.ts:82-99).

### Keep

- **The org-scoped kernel: `org_id` on every envelope, every table, every fan-out key, and the device-plane enforcement around it**
  - *why:* This is the part that did NOT deviate. A SaaS rewrite changes nothing here. The device plane derives org from a signed credential verified against the registry, stores the session's org rather than the claimed one, quarantines mismatches as evidence, and keys fan-out structurally — and it is proven with two live tenants against real Postgres, not asserted.
  - *size:* services/sync-gateway/src/gateway.ts (1337 LOC), auth.ts (137), schema.ts (503); 12 kernel tables; isolation-regression.test.ts + law5-fanout + law6-quarantine + notice-outbox + relay-hub-uplink carry the measured mutants (org_mismatch arm → 4 kills; branchKey → 1 kill)
- **`packages/domain/src/tenancy.ts` — the four tenancy records with their reasoning**
  - *why:* The record shapes are SaaS-correct as written (org → branch → device, org → user, one `DisplayName` schema, `01-F26` role assignments with `branch_id: null` = org-wide). The refusals are the valuable part: no per-branch timezone, no ledger FK, no `closed` status, unknown-keys-stripped for forward compat. A rewrite adds fields (plan, billing contact, region); it does not reshape these.
  - *size:* 377 LOC, 4 exported Zod records + 3 closed sets, one drift tripwire against `permissions.ts`
- **`services/api/src/trpc.ts`'s `authorized()` middleware + `assertEveryProcedureIsGated`**
  - *why:* The authorization half of tenancy is already structural and already boot-enforced: the `can()` scope is built from `ctx.subject.org_id` inside the middleware and cannot be stated by a caller, the subject is re-read from the store per request (not from token claims), and a host carrying an ungated procedure refuses to start. This is the right seam to hang the missing DATA-scope enforcement off.
  - *size:* trpc.ts 285 LOC; router.ts:224-241 the boot gate; 14/16 procedures gated, 2 on enumerated exemption lists
- **`services/api/src/__acceptance__/tenant-isolation.test.ts` and `packages/domain/src/__acceptance__/tenant-isolation-matrix.test.ts`**
  - *why:* A two-tenant attack register with a control in every case, plus a separate out-of-tree mutation matrix for the guard the first file structurally cannot see. The measurement discipline (each enforcement point mutated alone, kill counts recorded, a negative control proving the suite isn't brittle) is exactly what a SaaS security posture needs and is expensive to recreate. Extend the READS/ATTACKS arrays rather than rewriting.
  - *size:* 1189 LOC / 23 `it(` / 40+ generated cases, and 10 tests + a 4-row mutation matrix (M-a1..M-a3 + M-neg)
- **The seven declared provisioning commands and their refusals**
  - *why:* `create-org`/`create-branch`/`create-owner`/`list-tenancy`/`provision-device`/`revoke-device`/`migrate` already encode the ordering rules that the deliberately-absent foreign keys cannot: a branch under an unnamed org is refused, a re-run that would rename is refused, `--unrevoke`/`--password` are refused by name, and the owner's initial secret is minted rather than accepted. A control plane wraps these; it does not replace their logic.
  - *size:* create-org 214 + create-branch 224 + create-owner 272 + list-tenancy 154 + provision-device 323 + revoke-device 240 + migrate 143 LOC, with a 6-row mutation matrix (T1..T6, control 358/358)
- **`01-F71` as a written, enumerated enforcement register with a mutation requirement attached**
  - *why:* specs/01-kernel-sync.md:177 names four enforcement points, requires each to carry a test that fails when that point alone is removed, and states in terms that "a suite exercising one tenant passes with all four deleted". That FR is the correct governance artifact for a SaaS. Adding a fifth point (a structural data-scope gate) is an amendment to this list, not a new document.
  - *size:* specs/01-kernel-sync.md:177 (one FR, five clauses) + the 01-F68/F69/F70 records at :159-166

### Redo

- **Cloud-plane data scoping — replace per-call-site `ctx.subject.org_id` passing with a mechanism that cannot be bypassed**
  - *why:* 14 resolvers each hand-pass the org into a port; nothing checks provenance and the domain guard beneath is measurably unreachable (0 kills of 287 tests). `01-F71` itself says the property "is not satisfied by a `where org_id = …` in a query. That is how one call site implements it." Today the product is exactly one careless resolver away from the `runDayEnd` defect returning.
  - *shape:* A tenant-scoped data context: ports take no `org_id` argument at all. `withSession` resolves the subject and constructs an org-bound port bundle (`ctx.data.published()`, `ctx.data.devices.list()`), so a resolver has no org parameter to get wrong. Enforce it at boot the way `assertEveryProcedureIsGated` already works — a second walk asserting no procedure reaches an unscoped port factory — plus Postgres RLS with `SET LOCAL app.org_id` per transaction as the layer under it (the `01-F68` FK ban forbids referential constraints, NOT row policies; those are orthogonal and the corpus has no ruling against RLS — grep for it returns nothing anywhere in specs/).
- **The `services/api` ↔ `services/sync-gateway` `/internal` trust boundary**
  - *why:* 8 routes, org from request, one shared secret across all tenants, no person-level check, and no two-tenant test crosses it. In single-tenant deployment this is a non-issue; in shared hosting it is the whole attack surface, and the gateway's own CLAUDE.md already flags that a `PUBLISH_TOKEN` holder bypasses the permission matrix entirely.
  - *shape:* A short-lived, org-bound service credential minted per request by `services/api` from the authenticated subject (org_id as a signed claim, not a query parameter), verified by the gateway against the same registry it already trusts for devices. Then extend the two-tenant register to run across the real seam — spawn both services, provision two orgs, and re-run §1's READS×ATTACKS matrix against real Postgres.
- **`01-F60`'s enabled `(branch, channel)` set — move from process env to per-org storage**
  - *why:* This is the single hard blocker to one host serving two restaurants, and the isolation suite is already contorted around it (`ENABLED` is a union of both fixtures' branches). It is also the visible tip of a larger absence: `00 §7`'s layer-2 config plane does not exist at all, so EVERY tenant-varying setting has nowhere to live.
  - *shape:* A `kernel.org_config` table (org_id, key, value), written by the gateway (`18 §4` one-writer rule), read by `catalog.enabled` for `ctx.subject.org_id`. Ship the layer-2 plane once, with the enabled set as its first tenant; `BUSINESS_DAY_CUTOVER_HOUR` (`01-F46`'s layer-2 knob) is the second and is already specified as configurable.
- **Staged catalog edits — durable and org-scoped**
  - *why:* `server.ts:442` puts every tenant's pending day-end edits in one process `Map`. A restart silently discards them for all tenants at once; a second API replica sees none of them; and `14-F28`'s cancellation window is only as durable as one process.
  - *shape:* A `kernel.staged_edits` table beside the catalog tables (already keyed `(org_id, version)`), with the day-end sweep moved out of `server.ts`'s `setInterval` into `services/jobs` as a BullMQ repeatable — which `18 §5` already mandates and `server.ts:502-503` already records as owed.
- **Tenant lifecycle — make `suspended` mean something, or delete the column**
  - *why:* A status that is stored, returned on the wire, and enforced nowhere is worse than no status: it reads to the next engineer as a protection that exists. There is also no command that can set it, so the state is currently unreachable in both directions.
  - *shape:* Founder ruling first (see open questions), then one enforcement point stated in an FR — most likely: `withSession` refuses a subject whose org is suspended, and the gateway refuses `hello` for a suspended org's devices with a distinct reason (NOT `purge_command` — a suspended tenant's data must survive reinstatement, unlike `01-F42`'s revoked device). Plus `suspend-org`/`reinstate-org` commands and a `config.changed` event, which `15-F25` already routes through.
- **Provisioning — from seven shell commands to an authenticated control plane**
  - *why:* Becoming a tenant currently requires SSH to the service host plus the raw `DATABASE_URL`. That is not a SaaS onboarding path, it caps the business at hand-provisioned pilots, and it is why none of the seven commands can write an attributable event (a shell has no authenticated user).
  - *shape:* `apps/platform-admin` (currently `export {};`) as `15-F1`'s role-scoped internal console, calling the existing command logic as library functions behind a vendor-staff session. That single change closes the audit gap too: an authenticated actor makes `15-F3`/`15-F4`'s `config.changed` and `device.registered` emissions legal for the first time. Self-service signup is a separate, larger decision (`15-F26` refuses it today).
- **Billing, metering and plan state**
  - *why:* `restaurant-os.md:108` fixes the model (PKR 8,000/branch/month + ≤5% own-channel take rate, admin-settable per restaurant) and `specs/15:182` says billing/subscriptions/plans/quotas are deliberately unspecified anywhere in the corpus. Nothing implements any of it: `metering.usage_recorded` is in the `01 §4` catalog with no producer, `15-F24` says metering is measurement only, and no FR makes a payment state gate a service.
  - *shape:* Deliberately NOT a design I should invent (commandment 2 — every one of these is an unwritten FR). What the architecture needs to reserve: the take-rate is per-restaurant and admin-settable, so it is a layer-2 config value that must land on the same plane as the enabled set; and branch count is the billing unit, so `kernel.branches` becomes a metered table. Both are consequences of decisions already made in restaurant-os.md §7, not new ones.

### Delete

- **`BOOTSTRAP_OWNER_EMAIL` / `BOOTSTRAP_OWNER_PASSWORD_HASH` / `BOOTSTRAP_ORG_ID` / `BOOTSTRAP_OWNER_NAME` and `createMemoryUserStore` as a shipping code path**
  - *why:* A single env-declared owner in a process-local Map is the single-tenant assumption in its purest form: one org, one user, no way to make a second of either, and a restart is a migration. It is already correctly quarantined (mutually exclusive with `DATABASE_URL`, boot-crashes if both are set — server.ts:266-279) and `15-F26` calls it a stopgap, but it is still a supported mode and still the thing `ops/README.md` walks an operator toward on a laptop. Under SaaS, `kernel.users` is the only source, and the fallback should be "nobody can log in" alone. Note `services/api/CLAUDE.md` records that NO acceptance test covers the memory-store seam mutant — its return would be caught by nothing in CI.
- **`ENABLED_BRANCHES` / `ENABLED_CHANNELS` as environment variables**
  - *why:* Not the concept — `01-F60`'s enabled set is real and the writer's completeness check depends on it. The delete is the DELIVERY MECHANISM: a per-process env var makes the deployment, not the tenant, the unit of catalog configuration, and it is the one thing structurally preventing two restaurants on one host. `catalog.enabled` becoming a per-org read is the same procedure with a different source.
- **`ops/id.sh`, `ops/env/*.example` and the `ops/README.md` "Day zero" flow as the provisioning story**
  - *why:* The whole kit exists to make four processes agree on one org id, one branch id and one device id per installation, and its own header says "nothing anywhere checks that they do". Under SaaS the org id is minted by the control plane and handed to devices at pairing (`01-F25`'s pairing code, already specified and already owed) — there is no file for a human to keep in sync, so the class of failure the kit is built to mitigate stops existing. Keep the device-side env contract and the systemd units; delete the one-restaurant identity ceremony.
- **The org status `suspended` value, IF the founder rules that suspension is not a product concept**
  - *why:* Delete-or-implement, not delete-or-leave. Right now it is a shipped comment promising a protection that does not exist, which AGENTS.md's own §15 lesson names as worse than no comment because it retires the assertion the next session would otherwise write. `15-F25`'s own precedent for `closed` applies to `suspended` too: a state whose only effect is a different word on a dashboard is not a state.
- **The `services/api` `setInterval` day-end sweep at server.ts:506-515**
  - *why:* Not the behaviour — `14-F28` needs the sweep for tenants whose owner never presses anything. Delete this HOST for it: an every-org sweep inside the request-serving process is the `runDayEnd` shape one refactor away from being reachable from a request again, it does not survive a second API replica (two hosts both sweeping every org), and `18 §5` already mandates BullMQ repeatables in `services/jobs`, where the Auditor's identical every-org pass already lives.

### Open questions

- DEPLOYMENT MODEL — the fork everything else hangs off: is the target one shared stack serving N restaurants, or one isolated stack per restaurant that the vendor operates? The code today is unambiguously the second (ops/README.md:1, ops/id.sh, per-process ENABLED_BRANCHES), and the second is a legitimate SaaS for a Pakistani-restaurant beachhead at PKR 8,000/branch/month — it just needs automated per-tenant provisioning rather than a shared control plane. Almost every item in the redo list is cheap under one answer and expensive under the other. Do not let this be decided implicitly by the first person who fixes ENABLED_BRANCHES.
- MULTI-ORG HUMANS — may one person hold accounts in two orgs? `kernel.users.email` is globally unique case-folded today and `UserStore.findByEmail` takes an email and nothing else, so the answer is currently no, recorded as an open question in the migration itself and in backoffice-catalog.md Q3. This blocks vendor onboarding staff, multi-branch groups under separate orgs, and any future org-switcher. Changing it later means changing the login lookup shape, so it is cheaper to rule now.
- TENANT SUSPENSION — should a non-paying or abusive tenant be refusable, and if so at which of the two planes? Refusing at the cloud plane only (back office locked, tills keep selling offline per commandment 4 and 01-F17) is the conservative reading and is probably right; refusing at the gateway too means a suspended restaurant's tills stop syncing but must NOT be purged (01-F42's purge is for revoked devices, and a suspended tenant may be reinstated). No FR anywhere makes a lifecycle state refuse a request, so any answer here is a spec change, and the wrong guess in the permissive direction is a tenant that cannot be turned off, while the wrong guess the other way stops a paying restaurant mid-service.
- ROW-LEVEL SECURITY — is Postgres RLS wanted as the layer under the application scoping? The corpus is SILENT: grep for RLS/row-level-security/create policy over specs/, restaurant-os.md and AGENTS.md returns nothing. `01-F68` forbids foreign keys from ledger tables forever, and that reasoning (a constraint that refuses ingest for an org with no registry row is refusing a sale a till already rang) does NOT extend to row policies — but the two are one keystroke apart in the English and a session will conflate them. Rule explicitly, because RLS is the only mechanism that makes the 31 hand-written `org_id = $` predicates unnecessary rather than merely audited.
- BILLING AND METERING OWNERSHIP — specs/15:182 states outright that billing, subscriptions, plans, quotas and metering-to-invoice are deliberately unspecified anywhere in the corpus and that none should be written until §9.4's invoicing handoff has an owner. restaurant-os.md §7 has already fixed the shape (per-branch subscription, ≤5% own-channel take rate, admin-settable per restaurant). Someone has to own the FR, or the SaaS has a price list and no way to charge it. Related sub-question with an architectural consequence: is the take rate a layer-2 per-org config value (in which case it lands on the same plane as the enabled set) or layer-1 platform state?
- SELF-SERVICE SIGNUP — `15-F26` rules it out on the internal-only framing and restaurant-os.md §7's done-for-you onboarding, explicitly "not on a judgement that it is wrong". If the founder's SaaS vision includes a public sign-up or a trial org, that is a business decision with a product surface attached and it changes the provisioning design (create-owner currently mints a password and prints it once, which has no self-service analogue). If it does not, the control-plane console in the redo list is the whole answer and is much smaller.
- FIRST-CLASS TENANT DELETION / EXPORT — `01-F68`/`01-F69` say records are never deleted, `01-F1` makes events permanent, and `15-F25` refuses a `closed` state on the grounds that closure is a PROCEDURE (export → revoke devices → suspend). No such procedure is implemented and doc 22's export/erasure is unbuilt. A SaaS needs an answer for a departing customer's data before it has one, not after.

### Adversarial verdicts

- **AMENDED** — Cloud-plane data isolation is a per-call-site convention, not a structural guarantee. All 14 gated tRPC resolvers hand-pass ctx.subject.org_id into a port; nothing at boot, in the type system, or in CI prevents a new resolver from passing input.org_id instead. The boot gate that DOES exist (assertEveryProcedureIsGated, server.ts:133) checks only that a procedure passed through can(), never where its data org came from.
  - Substance CONFIRMED, enumeration is off. Verified by re-running the grep: 16 procedures (router.ts:52 publicProcedure login, router.ts:87 sessionProcedure whoami, 14 authorized(...) at exactly the cited lines). services/api/src/server.ts:133 is `assertEveryProcedureIsGated(appRouter);` and its body (router.ts:224-243) filters on `meta?.authz === undefined` against two hand-maintained exemption Sets — it inspects no org derivation at all. Zero resolvers read org from input (the only `input.org_id` hits in services/api/src are inside the test fake-gateway). The two-tenant suite's §1 attacks a HARDCODED `READS` list (tenant-isolation.test.ts:725-733), so a new resolver is not automatically covered. CORRECTIONS: (a) it is 13 of the 14 authorized resolvers that pass `ctx.subject.org_id` into a port — `catalog.enabled` (catalog-router.ts:127) passes NO org and returns the host-wide `ctx.catalog.enabled`, which is finding 5's defect, not this one; (b) two of the 14 (`catalog.editMenuPrices` router.ts:169, `ops.voidAfterKot` router.ts:178) are B-2 echo FIXTURES that reach no port, they just spread the subject's org into their reply; (c) the 14 org-derivation sites listed include router.ts:89, which is `whoami` — a SESSION-only procedure, not one of the 14 authorized ones. So the cited 14/14 is two different sets of 14 conflated. Severity major is defensible for a tenancy audit but note the mitigation the finding omits: trpc.ts:266-271 makes the org unstatable at the middleware, and the §1 sweep already fires 35 attack cases across 5 axes.
- **AMENDED** — The permission matrix's org arm — 01-F71 enforcement point (a) — is unreachable from services/api and kills zero tests there. Cloud-plane isolation therefore rests on exactly ONE mechanism (point (b)), with no second lock that any host-level test can observe.
  - Every cited fact CONFIRMED: packages/domain/src/permissions.ts carries `if (subject.org_id !== scope.org_id)` in reportScope (:483), can (:508) and canPayOut (:575); tenant-isolation-matrix.test.ts:17-28 records 0/287 services/api kills for the first two mutants; its M-a1/M-a2/M-a3 rows (lines 43-47) record 4/2/1 direct kills with an M-neg operand-swap control at 0. I also confirmed the mechanism: the only production callers of the matrix in services/api are trpc.ts:267 and summary-router.ts:63, and BOTH build the scope from `subject.org_id`, so the arm is genuinely unreachable on that plane. SEVERITY INFLATED — this is not an unguarded lock. The arm has its own mutation-proven register in packages/domain that kills exactly the mutants the API suite survives, which is the defence-in-depth arrangement 01-F71 asks for and which both files document up front. The true residual is narrow: no HOST-level test can observe point (a), so a refactor that breaks (b) and (a) together on the API plane would be caught only by the API suite's (b) assertions. That is a documented shape, not a missing guard — closer to minor/informational than major.
- **CONFIRMED** — The gateway's /internal surface takes org_id from the REQUEST on all 8 routes, behind a single shared bearer (PUBLISH_TOKEN) identical for every tenant. Any holder of that one secret reads and writes every tenant, and the gateway has no person-level check at all.
  - Verified line by line. `app.addHook("onRequest")` at publish-http.ts:244 is the ONE gate for everything under /internal — fail-closed 503 with no PUBLISH_TOKEN, else a single `credentialMatches` (timingSafeEqual) against `deps.publishSecret`, with no per-org or per-person dimension. `grep app.(post|get)` returns exactly the 8 cited routes at 260, 283, 295, 308, 337, 354, 380, 411. Org arrives from the request on every one: CatalogPublishRequest.org_id (:60), OrgEventRequest.org_id (:76), OrgQuery (:83), DeviceRevokeRequest.org_id (:95), LedgerWindowQuery.org_id (:107). services/sync-gateway/CLAUDE.md:667-668 says verbatim "A holder of PUBLISH_TOKEN can POST this route directly and bypass the matrix entirely. This service authorizes services, never people". services/api/src/server.ts:428-436 requires SYNC_GATEWAY_TOKEN and its own error text calls it "One shared secret"; ops/env/cloud.env.example:38 says "It is the same string as SYNC_GATEWAY_TOKEN below" and :69 "must be byte-identical". Nothing to refute.
- **CONFIRMED** — There is no Postgres row-level security, no per-tenant schema, no per-tenant role, and no foreign keys anywhere. Isolation in storage is 31 hand-written org_id = $ predicates across 7 files, plus 12 tables sharing one kernel schema on one connection pool.
  - Re-measured independently, all four numbers land exactly. `grep -rain 'row level security|enable row|create policy|current_setting('` over services/, packages/, ops/, scripts/, specs/ returns ZERO. `grep -ac 'references(' services/sync-gateway/src/schema.ts` = 0, and the .sql migrations confirm it in prose (0010_tenancy_records.sql:9, 0011_tenancy_users.sql:18). One `pgSchema("kernel")` at schema.ts:35; the 12 `kernel.table(` declarations are at exactly 38, 64, 70, 98, 137, 191, 217, 274, 297, 369, 408, 462. Counting `org_id = ${` across services/sync-gateway/src/*.ts gives gateway 12, registry 6, catalog 5, tenancy 4, day-ledger 2, org-events 1, quarantine-query 1 = 31, matching the per-file split precisely. ONE FRAMING CAVEAT the finding already half-states: the FK absence is not an oversight but a hard spec prohibition — specs/01-kernel-sync.md:162 (01-F68) forbids a ledger FK to the org record forever, with a stated reason (a referential constraint would refuse ingest for orgs with no registry row, i.e. refuse a sale already rung). So the FK clause is evidence of design, not of weakness; the RLS/per-tenant-role clause stands on its own.
- **CONFIRMED** — 01-F60's enabled (branch, channel) set is per-PROCESS, not per-org, so one services/api host cannot serve two restaurants with different branch topologies. This is the single largest architectural blocker to shared-tenancy hosting, and the isolation suite is forced to declare a union of both tenants' branches to exist at all.
  - Confirmed, and the mechanism is stronger than the finding argues. server.ts:401-402 read ENABLED_BRANCHES/ENABLED_CHANNELS from the boot env; :451 puts them on the single host-wide `CatalogDeps` (`enabled: { branches: env.ENABLED_BRANCHES, channels: env.ENABLED_CHANNELS }`), and catalog-router.ts:122-124 states "The answer is the DEPLOYMENT's set rather than a per-org lookup … one process serves one set" verbatim. tenant-isolation.test.ts:78-85 carries the quoted limitation and declares one set covering both tenants. ops/env/cloud.env.example:79/89 carry the quoted warning. WHY THE UNION WORKAROUND DOES NOT RESCUE IT — I read assertSavable (catalog.ts:204-234): it iterates the FULL cross product of enabled.branches × enabled.channels and throws unless a price exists for every cell. So on a union host, tenant A cannot save ANY sellable item without pricing it for tenant B's branches. That is not a cosmetic limit; it is a hard refusal, which makes 'blocker' for shared-tenancy hosting the right severity.
- **CONFIRMED** — All tenants' staged day-end catalog edits live in one process-local Map in the PRODUCTION composition root. A restart loses every tenant's pending edits, and 14-F28's "cancellable until they land" promise rests on one setInterval in one process.
  - services/api/src/server.ts:442 is `staged: createMemoryStagedEditStore(),` inside start(), directly above the two real gateway ports at :449-450 — so the contrast the finding draws is exactly as described. The comment block at 490-503 says in the code's own words "a pending day-end edit still does not survive a restart, and a host that never runs start() never lands one" and "14-F28's promise to a tenant whose owner never presses anything now rests entirely on this line", referring to the `setInterval` sweep immediately below. `createMemoryStagedEditStore` is declared at catalog.ts:138 (finding said 140 — a two-line offset, immaterial) and is a `Map<string, Map<string, StagedEdit>>` keyed by org, whose own header calls it a "STUB, and named one … Process local, dies with the process." Nothing overstated.
- **AMENDED** — An org's lifecycle status `suspended` is storable, reportable and enforced by absolutely nothing — and no command can set it. A tenant that stops paying cannot be turned off through any surface this product has.
  - The enforcement half is CONFIRMED: packages/domain/src/tenancy.ts:182 declares ORG_STATUSES; schema.ts:379 stores it; services/api/src/tenancy.ts:35-38 says "It gates nothing here"; tenancy-read-http.test.ts:148 asserts it is "reported, never enforced here". I swept every `suspend` hit in packages/, services/, apps/ — 13 files, and every hit is a declaration, a comment, a schema column or a test fixture; there is no branch anywhere that reads a status and refuses. No UPDATE against kernel.orgs exists anywhere, and services/sync-gateway/package.json:5-16 declares 7 commands, none of them suspend/unsuspend. TWO CORRECTIONS. (1) create-org.ts does not 'explicitly REFUSE --status suspended' in code — it hardcodes `status: "active"` at :147 and the refusal lives only in the doc comment at :133-135; there is no argument parser branch rejecting the flag. (2) The last sentence is overstated: a non-paying tenant CAN be turned off today, just not through the status column — `pnpm -C services/sync-gateway revoke-device` and 14-F13's back-office device list evict every till within 30 s and block reads as well as writes (01-F48). 15-F25's own closure procedure is described as exactly that composition (export → revoke devices → suspend with a reason). So the real finding is 'the lifecycle column is decorative and the only working kill switch is per-device revocation', which is narrower than 'no surface exists'.
- **CONFIRMED** — There is no tenant self-service, no billing, no metering, no plan and no quota anywhere in the code — and the corpus says this is a deliberate un-decision. Provisioning is 7 shell commands requiring shell access on the service host and DATABASE_URL.
  - Every citation lands. specs/15-platform-admin.md:182 is the §9 open question 7 and contains verbatim "**Self-service signup** — 15-F26 rules it out" and "**Related and deliberately NOT specified anywhere in this corpus: billing, subscriptions, plans, quotas and metering-to-invoice.**" apps/platform-admin/src/index.ts is 2 lines and is `export {};` with a scaffold comment. services/sync-gateway/package.json:7-16 declares exactly the 7 named commands (migrate, create-org, create-branch, create-owner, list-tenancy, provision-device, revoke-device), all `tsx src/*.ts` entry points requiring host shell + DATABASE_URL. packages/domain/src/tenancy.ts:196 excludes "anything to do with plan, billing, quota or metering" from OrgRecord, citing 15-F5a/15-F24. restaurant-os.md:108 carries the PKR 8,000/branch/month line. This is a correctly-attributed gap-by-decision, not an invented one.
- **CONFIRMED** — The entire operator kit is written for ONE restaurant per deployment, and the runbook says so in its first sentence.
  - ops/README.md:3 reads "Everything needed to install RestOS in one restaurant and keep it running". ops/id.sh:3 reads "mint this restaurant's ids and secrets ONCE, into one file", and :26-33 is the refusal-to-rerun with the quoted reason ("These ids are baked into every device token already minted…"), with `--force` documented at :18 as orphaning every provisioned device. ops/env/cloud.env.example carries single-valued ORG_ID (:116), ENABLED_BRANCHES (:89), DEVICE_TOKEN_SECRET (:34) and PUBLISH_TOKEN (:42) for the whole host, and :113-114 carries the exact quoted warning ending "NOTHING CHECKS THIS." ops/systemd/ ships 6 units, one per process (api, backoffice, jobs, sync-gateway, backup service+timer) with no tenant dimension. The 'shape of the founder's deviated' framing is editorial, but every load-bearing fact is verbatim.
- **AMENDED** — kernel.users.email is unique case-folded GLOBALLY rather than per org, so one human cannot hold an account in two tenants.
  - Facts CONFIRMED exactly: 0011_tenancy_users.sql:71 is `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX "users_email_lower_uq" ON "kernel"."users" USING btree (lower("email"));` with no org_id term (the per-org index at :74 is a plain non-unique `users_org_idx`), and the quoted comment is at :47-52 verbatim including the backoffice-catalog.md Q3 residual. router.ts:53 is `await ctx.store.findByEmail(input.email)` and UserStore.findByEmail (users.ts:55) takes an email and nothing else. SEVERITY INFLATED / FRAMING WRONG. This is not an accident that a per-org index would fix: the migration states the actual constraint chain, which I verified — the login path has no org input, and AuthSubject's org comes FROM the resolved user record (01-F71 (b): the org is taken from the subject, never from the request). A per-org unique index would therefore admit two rows that one lookup cannot choose between, i.e. it would break enforcement point (b) to buy multi-org accounts. Making one human multi-org requires an org selector on login (a product decision, backoffice-catalog.md Q3), not an index change. It also has zero isolation consequence — no leak follows from it. Minor/moderate, and it belongs on the roadmap rather than in a defect register.
- **AMENDED** — tenant-isolation.test.ts is a genuinely strong two-tenant register but attacks ONE plane through IN-MEMORY stores. It never crosses the gateway's /internal boundary, never touches Postgres, and never exercises the real adapters that carry org over the wire.
  - The description of the FILE is accurate — 1189 lines, 23 `it(` declarations, the five describes at 668/776/857/937/1033 matching §0–§4, READS (7 surfaces) at :725-733 × ATTACKS (5 axes: org in body, org+subject id, three org headers, foreign branch_id, role claim) at :736-774 = 35 generated cases, all stores memory-backed by the header rule at :48-55, and the catalog.enabled out-of-scope note at :78-85. But the GAP CLAIM over-reports and that is what an architect would act on. The /internal routes are NOT unattacked: inside services/sync-gateway, against real Postgres over real HTTP, I found cross-org isolation assertions for /internal/tenancy (tenancy-read-http.test.ts:191-211, §C 'never returns another tenant's org record or branches' WITH a tenant-B control), /internal/org-events (catalog-publish-http.test.ts:303, 'appends and reads back in 01-F18 order, org-isolated', publishing a decoy row under a second org), /internal/devices (device-http.test.ts:147, 'returns this org's registry rows, and no other org's'), /internal/ledger/window (day-ledger-http.test.ts:356/377, queries under `${ORG}-other`), plus quarantine-query.test.ts:72 (two orgs reusing the SAME branch_id string) and catalog-transport.test.ts:339/416/446 (per-org versions, and 'another org was told' asserted empty). So 'real Postgres predicates' and '8 /internal routes' are covered on the gateway side. The genuine residual, which is narrower and worth stating precisely: NO test spans BOTH services with two tenants — services/api's real adapters (gateway-client.ts:349/364/389/393/422/453, each taking org_id as a parameter) are exercised only by catalog-gateway-seam.test.ts against a single-tenant fake gateway, so nothing proves tenant A's request through the real adapter cannot return B's rows from a real gateway. Severity of that residual is moderate, not major, given both ends are independently covered.

## provisioning

**Verdict.** The founder is right about the outcome and wrong about the cause: this is not an accident of implementation, it is a written decision that was faithfully executed. `specs/15-platform-admin.md:169` (15-F26) states outright "There is no self-service signup path, and that is a decision," and §9.7 (`specs/15-platform-admin.md:182`) files it as an unresolved business question. So the corpus SPECIFIES vendor-operated, done-for-you onboarding, and the code BUILT exactly that. Measured against a SaaS bar, every tenant record in the system — org, branch, owner, device — comes into existence through one of six `tsx` CLI commands declared in `services/sync-gateway/package.json` and run over SSH on the service host; there is no HTTP, tRPC or UI path to any of them (8 `/internal/*` routes exist at `services/sync-gateway/src/publish-http.ts:260-411` and not one creates an org, branch or user; the back office issues exactly 12 tRPC calls and none of them creates anything but a catalog entry). `apps/platform-admin` — the vendor console spec 15's 28 FRs describe — is 2 lines: `export {};`. There is zero signup, zero invite, zero email verification, zero password reset, and exactly one unauthenticated procedure in the entire cloud plane (`PUBLIC_PROCEDURES = new Set(["auth.login"])`, `services/api/src/router.ts:30`). What survives a SaaS rewrite is substantial and better than the surface suggests: the kernel is genuinely org-scoped, `kernel.orgs/branches/users` are real tables with a real writer, tenant isolation is a specified property with named enforcement points and 33 dedicated tests, `services/jobs` genuinely enumerates every org, and `create-owner` already refuses to let a vendor choose a password. What is fatally single-tenant is narrower and sharper than "everything": the API's `ENABLED_BRANCHES`/`ENABLED_CHANNELS` is ONE deployment-wide env list consulted on every tenant's catalog save; the device staff roster is three hardcoded humans with globally identical UUIDs seeded from env vars with no wire transport at all; and the commercial lever the business model rests on — suspension — is a string in a column that nothing reads and nothing writes.

### Findings

- **Every tenant/branch/user/device is created by a CLI command run with a shell on the service host. There is no HTTP, tRPC, or UI path to any of them, and no other writer exists.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* `services/sync-gateway/package.json` scripts: `create-org`, `create-branch`, `create-owner`, `list-tenancy`, `provision-device`, `revoke-device`, `migrate` — all `tsx src/<file>.ts`. Symbol-precise grep for the only SQL writers: `insert into kernel.orgs` at services/sync-gateway/src/tenancy.ts:82 (sole caller create-org.ts:153), `insert into kernel.branches` tenancy.ts:130 (sole caller create-branch.ts:146), `insert into kernel.users` tenancy.ts:195 (sole caller create-owner.ts:212), `insert into kernel.device_registry` registry.ts:99 (sole shipping caller provision-device.ts:239). The gateway's 8 HTTP routes are publish-http.ts:260,283,295,308,337,354,380,411 — publish/read catalog, append/read org-events, read tenancy, list devices, revoke device, read ledger window. None creates a tenant record. Each command reaches DATABASE_URL directly (create-org.ts:180-184).
- **There is no self-serve signup, no invite flow, no email verification, and no password reset anywhere in the codebase. Exactly one procedure in the entire cloud plane is reachable without a credential.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* `services/api/src/router.ts:30`: `export const PUBLIC_PROCEDURES: ReadonlySet<string> = new Set(["auth.login"]);` — the comment reads "Exactly one, and it is how you get a credential." `grep -arniE '(signup|invite|verify.?email|password.?reset|magic.?link|set-credential)'` across apps/ services/ packages/ returns 20 files; every hit is a comment or a UI string — the only substantive ones are apps/backoffice/src/components/auth-gate.tsx:17 ("OWED … password reset, lockout and rate limiting") and services/api/src/router.ts:48 (same). Zero implementations.
- **The API's enabled (branch, channel) set is ONE deployment-wide environment list, not per-org — so a single API process structurally cannot serve two tenants' catalog writes.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* `services/api/src/server.ts:401-402` reads `ENABLED_BRANCHES`/`ENABLED_CHANNELS` from env; server.ts:451 builds `enabled: { branches: env.ENABLED_BRANCHES, channels: env.ENABLED_CHANNELS }`. `services/api/src/catalog-router.ts:195` calls `assertSavable(input.entry, ctx.catalog.enabled)` — while every other line in the same file scopes on `ctx.subject.org_id` (lines 135, 166, 180, 203, 222, 247, 272). `services/api/src/catalog.ts:223-224` iterates `for (const branch_id of enabled.branches) for (const channel of enabled.channels)` and REFUSES any entry missing a price for any cell. So tenant B's owner cannot save a menu item without pricing it for tenant A's branch ids, and tenant A's branch ids are visible to tenant B via `catalog.enabled` (catalog-router.ts:127).
- **The device staff roster is three hardcoded people with globally identical UUIDs, seeded from environment variables, with NO transport in the sync protocol. 01-F28's "synced credential hashes" has no wire.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* `packages/device-config/src/dev-staff.ts:90-98`: `DEV_STAFF = [{user_id: "00000000-0000-7000-8000-000000000004", display_name: "Ayesha", role: "cashier"}, {…0005, "Bilal", cashier}, {…0006, "Hina", branch_manager}]`. PINs come from `DEV_STAFF_PIN_ENV` (dev-staff.ts:114-118) = `RESTOS_DEV_PIN`, `RESTOS_DEV_PIN_BILAL`, `RESTOS_DEV_PIN_HINA`. `grep -arn 'staff.apply|registry.apply'` across apps/services/packages excluding tests returns exactly ONE shipping writer: packages/device-config/src/dev-staff.ts:212. `grep -arn 'staff' packages/sync-protocol/src/*.ts` returns ZERO hits — there is no staff message type in the wire protocol. `grep -an 'staff' packages/sync-client/src/cloud-session.ts` returns one hit, a comment at line 697. ops/README.md's own limitations table: "You cannot add, rename or remove staff. There is no real staff roster yet."
- **`orgs.status` (15-F25's active|suspended lifecycle — the platform's only commercial lever) is a column nothing reads and nothing can write to `suspended`.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* `packages/domain/src/tenancy.ts:182`: `export const ORG_STATUSES = ["active", "suspended"] as const;`. `services/sync-gateway/src/create-org.ts:147` hardcodes `status: "active"` and its doc comment (lines 133-135) explains `--status` is deliberately not an argument. `grep -arn 'kernel.orgs'` in non-test code returns 10 hits, all in tenancy.ts (one INSERT, two SELECTs) and comments — there is no UPDATE statement on the table anywhere. `grep -arn 'suspended'` in non-test code returns 8 hits, every one a comment or a type declaration. No code path reads `org.status` to gate any service. 15-F7 ("Suspension: reversible; gates cloud services") has zero enforcement.
- **apps/platform-admin — the vendor control plane that specs/15 gives 28 FRs — is a 2-line stub. Of 28 FRs, 1 is satisfied (15-F27, explicitly, by the CLIs), 2 partially, 25 unbuilt.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* `apps/platform-admin/src/index.ts` in full: line 1 `// Scaffold stub — implementation arrives via plans/ tasks (24 §9). Spec: specs/15-platform-admin.md`, line 2 `export {};`. package.json has no `dev`/`start`/`build`; `test` and `build` are echo stubs. Whole app = 28 lines incl. package.json and CLAUDE.md. specs/15-platform-admin.md is 182 lines with 28 FR definition lines (15-F1..F27 plus 15-F5a). 15-F27 is satisfied by the six CLIs (the FR says so at spec line 173, calling the command "the SHAPE, not the destination"). 15-F26 partially: create-owner mints a credential but 15-F26's single-use expiring set-credential LINK is unbuilt (create-owner.ts prints a permanent password, acknowledged at services/sync-gateway/src/create-owner.ts:170). 15-F25 partially: the status enum exists, the transitions do not. Unbuilt entirely: 15-F1 (internal SSO+2FA), F2, F3 (audit), F4 (provisioning UI), F5/F5a/F6 (plans, flags, take-rate), F7 (suspension), F8-F10 (bulk import, recipe workbench, runbooks), F11-F14 (fleet health, alerting), F15-F18 (impersonation, support, diagnostics), F19-F22 (release channels, pins, forced-update windows, kill switch), F23-F24 (metering).
- **01-F25's back-office pairing code is SPECIFIED and entirely UNBUILT. A device is provisioned by an operator typing the same three ids into a shell command on the service host AND into an env file on the device, with no check that they match.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* specs/01-kernel-sync.md:58 (01-F25): "Registration is a one-time pairing via back office code". specs/14-backoffice.md:91 (14-F12): "Pairing via one-time codes (01-F25)". `grep -arniE 'pairing|pair_code|pairing_code'` across apps/services/packages returns 19 hits — every single one is either an unrelated use of the English word "pairing" (typography token pairings, ticket pairing) or a comment stating the feature does not exist: apps/manager/src/branch.ts:33 "Neither exists: the back office has no pairing screen"; packages/device-config/src/device-identity.ts:32 "`01-F25`'s pairing. Nothing here gives a device an identity". ops/README.md step 5: "Device admission needs shell access on this box; there is no pairing code in the back office yet."
- **The cloud login endpoint has no rate limiting, no lockout, no session revocation, and no password reset — while every email is globally unique across all tenants, making it an account enumerator across the whole fleet.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* `services/api/src/router.ts:52-62` — the entire login procedure is findByEmail + verifyPin + issueSessionToken. Its own doc comment (lines 47-50) names lockout, rate limiting, password reset, session rotation and revocation, and `audit.login` as OWED. `grep -arniE 'rate.?limit|lockout|throttl|attempts' services/api/src/*.ts` returns exactly one hit — router.ts:56, the comment admitting the absence. Email uniqueness is global not per-org: services/sync-gateway/CLAUDE.md records `users_email_lower_uq` UNIQUE on `lower(email)` across all orgs, and create-owner.ts:222 refuses with "emails are unique case-folded across ALL orgs". Session TTL is 12h (services/api/src/session.ts:26) with no revocation list.
- **A restaurant's first owner receives a permanent, never-expiring password minted on the vendor's terminal, and there is no way for them to change it, reset it, or for a second user to ever be created.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* `services/sync-gateway/src/create-owner.ts:145` `mintInitialSecret` = 24 random bytes base64url; :261 `process.stdout.write(outcome.initial_password)`. Its own doc block acknowledges the gap (services/sync-gateway/CLAUDE.md: "What this does NOT satisfy … 15-F26 specifies a single-use, expiring set-credential LINK. What ships is an initial PASSWORD — it does not expire, it is not single-use, nothing forces rotation"). A second `create-owner` for the same org is REFUSED (create-owner.ts:187-196, `orgHasOwner`), pointing at 14-F14's CRUD. `grep -arn '14-F14'` returns 17 non-test hits, all comments saying it is owed; `services/api/src/users-postgres.ts:170` — `setAssignments` THROWS with "this store cannot re-assign roles. The durable writer is 14-F14's user CRUD, which is …". The back office's complete tRPC surface is 12 calls (auth.login, session.whoami, catalog.{save,archive,published,pending,cancelPending,history,enabled}, devices.{list,revoke}, summary.nightly) — no user creation, no password change, no branch creation, no org settings.
- **Device identity is a manually-copied 3-tuple with no verification at either end, and on the manager phone the device CREDENTIAL is baked into the app bundle at build time — one Expo build per physical device.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* `packages/device-config/src/device-identity.ts:58-62` — `IDENTITY_ENV = {org_id: "RESTOS_ORG_ID", branch_id: "RESTOS_BRANCH_ID", device_id: "RESTOS_DEVICE_ID"}`; `resolveDeviceIdentity` (line 79) falls back per key to `DEV_IDENTITY` (line 45), three hardcoded UUIDs. Its own header, line 32: "an operator types the same three ids into `provision-device` on the service host and into this environment on the till, and a mismatch is still silent." `describeDeviceIdentity` (line 156) exists solely because "being wrong about it looks exactly like being right." On the phone: `apps/manager/src/branch.ts:79-83` declares `EXPO_PUBLIC_RESTOS_ORG_ID / _BRANCH_ID / _DEVICE_ID / _GATEWAY_URL / _DEVICE_TOKEN`; branch.ts:68 — babel-preset-expo substitutes these AT BUILD TIME, so the device token is inlined into the bundle. branch.ts:40: "A credential inlined into a bundle is not the shipped design."
- **There is zero billing, subscription, plan, quota or take-rate code, and the corpus deliberately specifies none — while restaurant-os.md §7 sells a PKR 8,000/branch/month subscription plus a 0-5% take-rate.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* restaurant-os.md:108-110 (Part I §7): "PKR 8,000 / branch / month base subscription … Own-channel take-rate up to 5% … admin-settable per restaurant." `grep -arliE '\b(billing|subscription|invoice|take_rate|take-rate|stripe)\b'` across all src returns 8 files; every hit is thermal-receipt "invoice" printing (packages/escpos/*) or a schema comment. specs/15-platform-admin.md:182 (§9.7): "Related and deliberately NOT specified anywhere in this corpus: billing, subscriptions, plans, quotas and metering-to-invoice … no FR anywhere makes a payment state gate a service, and none should be written until §9.4's invoicing handoff has an owner."
- **Bringing one restaurant into service requires roughly 25 discrete manual acts across three machines, of which at least 9 need a root/deploy shell on the service host, and the runbook itself states there is no error message for the most likely failure.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* ops/README.md §1-§8: (1) `bash ops/id.sh` on the cloud box mints org/branch/2×device UUIDs + 3 secrets into ops/ids.env; (2) cloud box setup; (3) `pnpm -C services/sync-gateway migrate`; (3b) `create-org`, `create-branch`, `create-owner`, `list-tenancy` — four commands, each `sudo -u restos DATABASE_URL=… pnpm -C services/sync-gateway …`; (4) copy systemd units, edit cloud.env (10 keys incl. ENABLED_BRANCHES which must contain the branch id), start 4 processes, read boot lines by hand; (5) `provision-device` × 2, copy each printed token to the right machine; (6) per Windows machine: install Node+pnpm, clone repo, `pnpm install`, `pnpm rebuild:native`, hand-fill 9-key counter.env / 8-key kitchen.env, place shell:startup shortcut, set BIOS restore-on-AC and Windows auto-logon; (7) author the menu in the browser; (8) train staff. ops/id.sh header: "Four processes have to agree on values that nothing anywhere checks … Get any of those wrong and ALL FOUR PROCESSES REPORT SUCCESS AND NO TILL EVER SEES A MENU. There is no error message anywhere in the product for this." ops/README.md: "Budget half a day for steps 1–7 and a full service for step 8."
- **ops/README.md's own limitations table is stale on the account model in the direction that matters — it says the owner account is in memory, which stopped being true when createPostgresUserStore landed.** [MINOR]
  - *evidence:* ops/README.md "What this cannot do yet" table row: "**The owner account is in memory** | It is re-created from env on every API restart." But `services/api/src/server.ts:266-283`: when `DATABASE_URL` is set, `resolveUserStore` builds `createPostgresUserStore(env.DATABASE_URL)` reading `kernel.users`; setting `DATABASE_URL` and `BOOTSTRAP_OWNER_*` together is a boot CRASH (server.ts:266-277). The same file's §3b (line ~99) and §4 correctly describe the persistent path. Two sections of one 654-line runbook disagree about where accounts live — and the stale one is in the table an operator reads to decide what the product can do.
- **14-F12's device list is org-wide, not per-branch as specified, and projects 1 of the 4 fields the FR names.** [MINOR]
  - *evidence:* specs/14-backoffice.md:91 (14-F12): "Device list per branch: class, app version, last-seen, sync lag." `services/api/src/device-router.ts:40` — `list: authorized("device.manage").query(...)` takes no branch_id (device-router.ts's own header records this as deliberate, because the service learns a device's branch only by reading the registry). `services/sync-gateway/src/publish-http.ts:354` — `GET /internal/devices?org_id=`. services/sync-gateway/CLAUDE.md records: "It projects what this table HAS, not what 14-F12 asks for … App version and last-seen are stored nowhere in this service and sync lag comes off a cursor this row does not carry."

### Keep

- **The tenancy directory tables and their writer/reader split — `kernel.orgs`, `kernel.branches`, `kernel.users` with a single writer service and a read-only second reader**
  - *why:* This is real multi-tenant storage with a correct 18 §4 ownership rule already enforced in code. `orgs(org_id, display_name, status, created_at)`, `branches(branch_id, org_id, display_name, branch_type, branch_class, created_at)`, `users(user_id, org_id, email, display_name, password_hash, assignments jsonb, grid_ordinal, created_at)` with `users_email_lower_uq`. A SaaS control plane writes to exactly these tables through exactly this port — the shape does not change, only who calls it. `createPostgresUserStore.setAssignments` already throws rather than becoming a second writer.
  - *size:* services/sync-gateway/src/tenancy.ts (269 LOC, 11 exports), services/api/src/users-postgres.ts, migrations 0010/0011
- **The six provisioning commands' business logic — ordering enforcement, idempotence semantics, and refusal messages — as the API layer of a future console**
  - *why:* Every rule a SaaS signup endpoint needs is already written and tested here, and it is written at the WRITER because 01-F68 forbids foreign keys forever. Ordering (branch under an unnamed org refused by name, create-branch.ts:126; owner in an unnamed org refused, create-owner.ts:178), idempotence (re-run same name = no-op; re-run different name = refusal pointing at 14-F2/14-F30, create-org.ts:164), no un-revocation (revoke-device refuses --restore/--unrevoke by name via strict parseArgs), no password ever an input (create-owner refuses --password/--password-hash/--pin by name). Rewrite the transport (argv → tRPC input), keep every one of these rules verbatim.
  - *size:* 1,969 LOC across create-org/create-branch/create-owner/list-tenancy/provision-device/revoke-device/tenancy/registry; 64 acceptance tests across 6 suites (tenancy-provisionable 11, provisionable 9, revocable 8, tenancy-read-http 12, device-http 13, device-auth 11), each with a published mutation matrix
- **Device admission and revocation as a mechanism: bound tokens with mandatory expiry, silent renewal PERSISTED device-side, mid-session eviction**
  - *why:* 01-F47/F48 are fully built and reached. Tokens carry iss/aud so a token minted for one deployment is inert in another (provision-device reads DEVICE_TOKEN_ISSUER/AUDIENCE because server.ts does). Renewal is persisted in sync-client, not the host — `persistRenewal` at packages/sync-client/src/cloud-session.ts:200, called at :462 and :487, and `:220` prefers the persisted token over the env-supplied one, so RESTOS_DEVICE_TOKEN is a bootstrap value only. Revocation reaches a running gateway's LIVE sessions within one sweep and is proved cross-process. A SaaS pairing-code flow needs a new front door onto exactly this machinery, not a new machinery.
  - *size:* services/sync-gateway/src/{auth.ts 137, registry.ts 273, revoke-device.ts 240}, packages/sync-client/src/cloud-session.ts renewal path
- **Tenant isolation as a specified, named, mutation-tested property (01-F71)**
  - *why:* specs/01-kernel-sync.md:177-182 names four enforcement points — (a) the permission matrix refuses cross-org before any action reasoning, (b) the cloud plane takes org from the authenticated subject never from the request, (c) the gateway QUARANTINES a mismatched envelope rather than dropping it, (d) fan-out keys on a structured (org,branch) pair not a concatenation — and requires a test that fails when each point alone is removed. This is the single most valuable SaaS asset in the repo and it already exists. Three dedicated suites, 33 test cases.
  - *size:* services/api/src/__acceptance__/tenant-isolation.test.ts (23 tests), packages/domain/src/__acceptance__/tenant-isolation-matrix.test.ts (10), services/sync-gateway/src/__acceptance__/tenancy-read-http.test.ts (12)
- **services/jobs — genuinely fleet-wide, already iterating every tenant**
  - *why:* `services/jobs/src/index.ts:134` — `select org_id from kernel.org_sequences order by org_id`, then `:146` `for (const org_id of await everyOrg(db)) { const report = await runAuditor({ db, org_id }); }` on a BullMQ repeatable. This is the one service in the repo whose loop is already the SaaS shape. Nothing about it assumes one restaurant.
  - *size:* services/jobs/src/index.ts, ~170 LOC + acceptance suite
- **The permission matrix and its two enforcement planes**
  - *why:* `packages/domain/src/permissions.ts` ships ROLES, PERMISSION_ACTIONS, can(), canPayOut, reportScope with 89 tests and 28/28 mutants killed, and it is REACHED on both planes: `apps/pos-electron/src/main/authorize.ts` runs every renderer-originated append through can()/canPayOut before the ledger is touched, and `services/api/src/trpc.ts` builds every procedure with authorized(<action>) with `assertEveryProcedureIsGated` refusing to boot a host carrying an ungated one (services/api/src/router.ts:228-239). Role-scoped multi-tenant authz is done.
  - *size:* packages/domain/src/permissions.ts + 89 tests; enforcement chain across 6 files
- **The refusal to let a vendor choose a customer's password**
  - *why:* 15-F26's strongest SaaS-correct property is already enforced in code and by name: create-owner mints a 192-bit secret itself, hashes it through domain's hashPin at 01-F61's Argon2id cost floor, prints it once on stdout alone, and `parseArgs({strict: true})` refuses `--password`, `--password-hash` and `--pin` BY NAME. The reasoning (services/sync-gateway/src/create-owner.ts:99-112) — argv reaches every ps on the host and the shell history — survives the rewrite unchanged.
  - *size:* services/sync-gateway/src/create-owner.ts:99-150
- **The kernel's org-scoping itself: org-scoped event store, per-org catalog versions, per-org publish, per-origin idempotent ingest**
  - *why:* `kernel.org_events` carries org_id and no branch stamp (01-F62); the catalog publish path is per-org (`ctx.subject.org_id` at catalog-router.ts:135,203,222,272); `publishCatalog` serializes per org on `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext('restos:catalog:' || org_id))`. The plumbing the founder says was built for one restaurant was in fact built multi-tenant throughout. This is why the gap is a control plane and not a rewrite.
  - *size:* services/sync-gateway/src/{gateway.ts, catalog.ts, org-events.ts}

### Redo

- **Tenant creation: org + branch + first owner**
  - *why:* Today it is three shell commands over SSH (create-org, create-branch, create-owner) requiring DATABASE_URL on the service host, emitting no event, carrying no actor, and printing a permanent password to the operator's terminal. A SaaS customer cannot be served without a vendor engineer.
  - *shape:* Keep tenancy.ts as the port verbatim. Put an authenticated control-plane surface in front of it — either apps/platform-admin (spec 15's answer, needs 15-F1 SSO+2FA + 15-F3 audit) or a public signup endpoint if the founder overrides 15-F26. Whichever it is, it must supply an ACTOR: the sole recorded reason none of these commands emits a `config.changed` event is that a shell has no authenticated user and `OrgEvent.actor_user_id` would be permanently null in an append-only store (create-org.ts:39-45). A console closes that in the same act. Replace the minted permanent password with 15-F26's single-use expiring set-credential link + a redemption screen behind 14-F1.
- **The API's enabled (branch, channel) set**
  - *why:* It is one deployment-wide env list (server.ts:401-402, 451) consulted on every tenant's catalog save (catalog-router.ts:195). Two tenants in one process is not a scaling limit, it is a correctness failure: tenant B cannot save any item without pricing it for tenant A's branches, and can read tenant A's branch ids through catalog.enabled.
  - *shape:* Derive the enabled set per org from `kernel.branches` (which already exists and is already written) plus a per-org channel enablement row — 15-F5 already specifies channel enablement as a per-org flag. Keep the single-declaration discipline that was just won: ONE authority, served by `catalog.enabled` and consumed by assertSavable, with no client-side fallback. This is the change that turns the API process from single-tenant to multi-tenant.
- **Staff identity on devices**
  - *why:* Three hardcoded humans (Ayesha/Bilal/Hina) with the SAME user_ids `…0004/0005/0006` in every deployment, seeded from RESTOS_DEV_PIN* env vars, with no message type for staff anywhere in packages/sync-protocol. 01-F28 specifies "PIN verification works on-device against synced credential hashes" and there is no sync. Every tenant's ledger attributes every sale to the same three UUIDs.
  - *shape:* A staff snapshot message in sync-protocol, produced from `kernel.users` per (org, branch), consumed by the existing `StaffRegistry.apply({kind:"snapshot", version, members})` — the device-side receiver is already built and correct (packages/sync-client/src/staff.ts). Then 14-F14's user CRUD in the back office as the writer. Delete packages/device-config/src/dev-staff.ts, which its own CLAUDE.md already instructs: "Delete the module when the transport lands."
- **Device provisioning in the field**
  - *why:* An operator runs provision-device on the service host, copies a printed JWT, and hand-types the same three UUIDs into an env file on the device — with no check at either end that they match, and the runbook stating there is no error message for the mismatch. On the manager phone the credential is baked into the Expo bundle at build time (apps/manager/src/branch.ts:68-83), meaning one app build per physical device.
  - *shape:* 01-F25/14-F12's one-time pairing code, which the corpus already specifies in one clause: owner opens the back office, presses Add device, gets a short code; the device asks for the code on first launch and receives its org/branch/device ids AND its token in one exchange, storing the token via the existing persistRenewal path (and expo-secure-store on RN, per 18 §8). This makes the three ids UNTYPEABLE, which removes the failure with no error message rather than documenting it.
- **Org suspension / reactivation**
  - *why:* `ORG_STATUSES = ["active","suspended"]` exists in packages/domain and in the schema, create-org hardcodes `active`, no UPDATE on kernel.orgs exists anywhere, and no code path reads org.status to gate anything. The business model's only enforcement lever is inert.
  - *shape:* A control-plane transition writing `config.changed` into the org's own ledger with a layer-1 actor (15-F25 specifies exactly this and introduces no new event type), plus enforcement at the two cloud gates: services/api refuses cloud-originated writes for a suspended org, services/sync-gateway gates the storefront/publish surface. 15-F7 is explicit that in-branch POS billing NEVER stops — so the gate goes on the cloud plane only, and the till keeps selling. Do not add a `closed` state; 15-F25 refuses it with a stated argument.
- **The cloud login endpoint**
  - *why:* No rate limiting, no lockout, no password reset, no session revocation, global email uniqueness making it a fleet-wide account enumerator, and a permanent vendor-minted initial password behind it. router.ts:52-62 is 10 lines and its own comment names five owed protections.
  - *shape:* Reuse what already exists one plane over: 01-F61's durable per-(device,user) lockout is built and tested in packages/sync-client for PINs.
