Add lockout + rate limiting keyed on (email, source), a set-credential redemption flow, forced rotation of the provisioned initial secret at first login, session revocation on user deactivation (findById already re-reads per request at 01-F27, so deactivation propagates the moment a deactivation surface exists), and the `audit.login` record — 01-F5 already has the subtype and nothing emits it on this plane.
- **apps/platform-admin**
  - *why:* 2 lines against 28 FRs. The vendor has no fleet view, no support tooling, no impersonation-with-consent, no release channels, no metering — and every provisioning act today is unattributed because the shell has no actor.
  - *shape:* Build the narrow slice first, in FR order of dependency: 15-F1 (internal SSO + 2FA, separate auth domain — 15 §8 requires internal tokens never valid on tenant APIs), 15-F3 (audit row per act), then 15-F4/F26/F27 as the console form over the tenancy port. That alone retires four of the six CLIs and closes the actor gap they all record. 15-F11's fleet dashboard needs a heartbeat pipeline that does not exist and should be scoped separately.

### Delete

- **packages/device-config/src/dev-staff.ts — DEV_STAFF, DEV_STAFF_PIN_ENV, seedDevStaff**
  - *why:* Three hardcoded people with globally-identical UUIDs and env-var PINs. Its own CLAUDE.md already says "Delete the module when the transport lands." Under SaaS it is not merely a stopgap, it is a cross-tenant attribution defect: every restaurant's ledger names the same three user_ids. Delete it together with RESTOS_DEV_PIN / RESTOS_DEV_PIN_BILAL / RESTOS_DEV_PIN_HINA and the .bat gates that check them.
- **`DEV_IDENTITY` and the per-key fallback in `resolveDeviceIdentity` (packages/device-config/src/device-identity.ts:45-83)**
  - *why:* A device that silently adopts three hardcoded UUIDs when unconfigured is the failure ops/id.sh's header calls out — four processes report success and no till ever sees a menu. 01-F65 already grants it exactly one exemption (the counter's documented no-environment `pnpm start`) and requireDeviceIdentity is the correct discipline everywhere else. Once pairing supplies identity, the seed has no remaining justification and its survival is a permanent trap. Keep `requireDeviceIdentity`; delete the seed and the resolver.
- **BOOTSTRAP_OWNER_EMAIL / BOOTSTRAP_OWNER_PASSWORD_HASH / BOOTSTRAP_ORG_ID / BOOTSTRAP_OWNER_NAME and `bootstrapUsers` (services/api/src/server.ts:179-207)**
  - *why:* An org's sole owner assembled from four env vars into a process-local Map that dies on restart. 15-F26 names it "a development seed … a stopgap standing in a provisioning step's place." It has no place in a multi-tenant deployment: the account exists in no kernel.users row, list-tenancy cannot report it, 14-F14 could never deactivate it, and 15-F3's audit would name an account the directory has never heard of. The mutual-exclusion crash at server.ts:266-277 is the right instinct; deleting the branch entirely is better. Note this also deletes BOOTSTRAP_ORG_ID, the three-ended join key with no error message that AGENTS.md and ops/id.sh both spend paragraphs warning about.
- **`ops/id.sh` as the provisioning entry point, and the hand-copied ids.env → cloud.env → counter.env → kitchen.env chain**
  - *why:* A bash script that mints a restaurant's org/branch/device UUIDs and three shared secrets into a mode-600 file, from which a human copies 9 values into a counter env file and 8 into a kitchen one. Its own header states the failure it exists to prevent has no error message anywhere in the product. Under SaaS the ids are minted server-side at signup and the device receives them over a pairing exchange; the file, the copying and the whole class of silent mismatch go away. (The secrets half — DEVICE_TOKEN_SECRET, PUBLISH_TOKEN, SESSION_SECRET — is ordinary deployment secret management and survives in some form.)
- **The four `services/*` scaffold stubs that will never be reached by a tenant-lifecycle path, if they are still empty at rewrite time**
  - *why:* apps/{owner,pos-rn,rider,storefront,waiter}/src and services/{foodpanda,intelligence,tax,whatsapp} are all `export {};` with echo-stub test/build scripts and no dev/start. They are not lifecycle work, but they inflate every count taken off the workspace and they hide which apps actually run. Not a lifecycle finding — flagged so the architect does not read `ls apps/` as capability.

### Open questions

- Does the founder override 15-F26? The spec RULES OUT self-service signup as a decision, not an omission (specs/15-platform-admin.md:169), and §9.7 files it as an unmade business decision with a product surface attached. restaurant-os.md §7 sells done-for-you onboarding — menu import, a live recipe-mapping session with the chef (15-F9), printer runbooks — as part of the PKR 8,000/month offer. 'Multi-tenant SaaS' and 'self-serve signup' are separable: the product can be fully multi-tenant with staffed onboarding through a vendor console. Which one is being asked for changes whether apps/platform-admin or a public signup flow is the first thing built, and they are different modules.
- One API process per tenant, or one API process for all tenants? Today ENABLED_BRANCHES makes it one-per-tenant by construction. Per-tenant processes would preserve every existing behaviour and defer the enabled-set rewrite, at the cost of an orchestration layer and per-tenant deploy. A shared process needs the per-org enabled set (see REDO) and a per-org channel enablement row. This decision governs the size of the API rewrite and nothing else in the repo depends on it.
- Does billing exist in the rewrite, and does a payment state gate service? specs/15 §9.7 is explicit that billing, subscriptions, plans, quotas and metering-to-invoice are deliberately unspecified anywhere in the corpus, and that 'no FR anywhere makes a payment state gate a service, and none should be written until §9.4's invoicing handoff has an owner.' 15-F7/15-F25 give a suspension lever with a stated constraint — in-branch POS billing NEVER stops, because 00 §5.1 is not a business lever. If non-payment must gate anything, someone must rule on exactly which cloud services it gates and write the FR first.
- Multi-org membership for one human. services/api/src/users.ts:25-29 records this as open (backoffice-catalog.md Q3: 'a vendor-onboarding team member legitimately touches several'), AuthSubject carries a single org_id, and the users table's email uniqueness is GLOBAL and case-folded precisely because findByEmail takes an email and nothing else. If a SaaS operator or a multi-branch owner needs one login across orgs, that index and that subject shape both change, and the change reaches every enforcement point 01-F71 names.
- Where does the provisioning ACTOR come from, and does provisioning become part of the append-only ledger? Every one of the six commands deliberately emits NO event, on one shared and ratified reason: a shell has no authenticated user, so OrgEvent.actor_user_id could only ever be null, permanently, in a store 01-F1 forbids correcting. 15-F4 says provisioning emits the org's first config.changed and 15-F3 audits every staff action with an actor. A console supplies the actor — but the decision to start writing device.registered / config.changed / user.changed retroactively-inconsistently (revocations already exist with no matching registrations, recorded at services/api/CLAUDE.md) needs a ruling on what the pre-console history means.
- 01-F25's pairing code is one clause in the corpus and nothing more. Building it means designing a code format, a TTL, a rate limit, a claim/refusal protocol and a back-office surface — all policy, all currently uninvented. The repo's own commandment 2 forbids filling that in from plausibility. Someone has to write the FR before anyone can build the thing that removes SSH from device provisioning, and that is the single highest-leverage spec change in this dimension.

### Adversarial verdicts

- **CONFIRMED** — Every tenant/branch/user/device is created by a CLI command run with a shell on the service host. There is no HTTP, tRPC, or UI path to any of them, and no other writer exists.
  - Re-derived independently. `grep -a 'insert into kernel'` across apps/services/packages returns 12 sites; of those the four tenancy tables have exactly one non-test writer each — tenancy.ts:82/130/195 and registry.ts:99 (the only other device_registry insert is __acceptance__/helpers.ts:150, a test). Callers: insertOrg←create-org.ts:153, insertBranch←create-branch.ts:146, insertUser←create-owner.ts:212, registerDevice←provision-device.ts:239, and nothing else outside tests. Route registrations across the WHOLE gateway (not just publish-http.ts) are the 8 `/internal/*` routes cited plus one websocket register in server.ts:79-80 — none creates a tenant record. The API's tRPC surface (17 procedures, enumerated below) has no create-org/branch/user procedure. package.json scripts match the seven named.
- **CONFIRMED** — There is no self-serve signup, no invite flow, no email verification, and no password reset anywhere in the codebase. Exactly one procedure in the entire cloud plane is reachable without a credential.
  - router.ts:30 is verbatim `PUBLIC_PROCEDURES = new Set(["auth.login"])` with the quoted comment. I ran a WIDER grep than the finding (adding forgot-password, change-password, email-verif, invitation) with -a: 22 files hit, and inspecting every substantive one shows only comments (auth-gate.tsx:17, router.ts:47-50, create-owner.ts:36/258 naming 15-F26's set-credential link as owed) or unrelated English ("an empty screen is an invitation to act", "would invite the next reader"). Zero implementations. Corroborated by specs/15 §9.7, which rules self-service signup out of scope rather than as built.
- **CONFIRMED** — The API's enabled (branch, channel) set is ONE deployment-wide environment list, not per-org — so a single API process structurally cannot serve two tenants' catalog writes.
  - server.ts reads ENABLED_BRANCHES/ENABLED_CHANNELS from process env and builds `enabled: { branches: env.ENABLED_BRANCHES, channels: env.ENABLED_CHANNELS }` on CatalogDeps; catalog-router.ts:195 passes `ctx.catalog.enabled` to assertSavable while every neighbouring line scopes on `ctx.subject.org_id`; catalog.ts:222-234 is the nested `for (branch_id of enabled.branches) for (channel of enabled.channels)` that throws RangeError on any missing cell. catalog-router.ts:127 returns `ctx.catalog.enabled` unfiltered by org. The code states the consequence itself at catalog-router.ts:122-124: "The answer is the DEPLOYMENT's set rather than a per-org lookup ... one process serves one set." No per-org override exists anywhere.
- **CONFIRMED** — The device staff roster is three hardcoded people with globally identical UUIDs, seeded from environment variables, with NO transport in the sync protocol. 01-F28's "synced credential hashes" has no wire.
  - DEV_STAFF at dev-staff.ts:90-98 is the three literal UUIDs/roles as quoted; DEV_STAFF_PIN_ENV:113-118 maps them to RESTOS_DEV_PIN / _BILAL / _HINA. The finding's own grep pattern was imprecise (`staff.apply` returns nothing) but the corrected one confirms it harder: `registry.apply` across all non-test code returns exactly ONE site, dev-staff.ts:212, and seedDevStaff's only callers are pos-electron/main/index.ts:629 and pass-kds/main/index.ts:219. I searched the PROPERTY rather than the word in the protocol — roster/pin_hash/user_id/member/people in sync-protocol/src/messages.ts: zero hits; the complete message vocabulary is hello, hello_ack, push, push_ack, catchup_request/response, catalog_request/response, snapshot, catalog_notice, quarantine_notice, purge_command. No roster message under any name. pass-kds/main/index.ts:202 says it in shipped code: "nothing populates `store.staff` on any device".
- **AMENDED** — `orgs.status` (15-F25's active|suspended lifecycle — the platform's only commercial lever) is a column nothing reads and nothing can write to `suspended`.
  - The write half is confirmed exactly: ORG_STATUSES at domain/tenancy.ts:182, create-org.ts hardcodes status "active" with its doc comment refusing a --status flag, no UPDATE on kernel.orgs anywhere (I grepped `update kernel.orgs` and `set status` — zero), and every `suspended` hit outside tests is a comment or a type. But the headline "a column nothing reads" is wrong: tenancy.ts:95 and :110 both SELECT it, tenancy.ts:120 maps it into the record, list-tenancy prints it, and services/api/src/tenancy.ts:35-44 carries it to clients as `OrgListing.status`. Corrected claim: orgs.status is read and displayed but nothing can write `suspended` and no code path reads it to GATE anything — 15-F7's suspension has zero enforcement, and services/api/src/tenancy.ts:36-38 declares that deliberate ("It gates nothing here: no FR anywhere makes a lifecycle state refuse a request ... inventing a suspension check would be inventing policy").
- **AMENDED** — apps/platform-admin — the vendor control plane that specs/15 gives 28 FRs — is a 2-line stub. Of 28 FRs, 1 is satisfied (15-F27, explicitly, by the CLIs), 2 partially, 25 unbuilt.
  - Substance confirmed: src/index.ts is exactly the 2 lines quoted; package.json has only echo stubs for test/build and no dev/start; specs/15-platform-admin.md is 182 lines with `grep -c '^- 15-F'` = 28. Three corrections. (a) The whole app is 18 lines, not 28 (package.json 10 + CLAUDE.md 6 + index.ts 2) — the finding overstated it. (b) The "SHAPE, not the destination" sentence is at spec line 120, not 173 (line 173 is §9's open questions). (c) The tally is off by one in the same direction: 15-F4's "create org → … → branches" leg is also partially satisfied by create-org/create-branch (15-F27 says so), so it reads nearer 1 satisfied / 3 partial / 24 unbuilt. The unbuilt list itself (F1-F3, F5-F22 incl. suspension, fleet health, impersonation, release channels, metering) checks out against the FR headings.
- **CONFIRMED** — 01-F25's back-office pairing code is SPECIFIED and entirely UNBUILT. A device is provisioned by an operator typing the same three ids into a shell command on the service host AND into an env file on the device, with no check that they match.
  - specs/01:58 and specs/14:91 read as quoted. I re-ran the grep with -a: 30 hits, every one either the typography sense (`27-F42` size/line-height "pairing", layout-gate axis pairings, ticket pairing) or a comment stating absence — manager/branch.ts:33 "Neither exists: the back office has no pairing screen", device-identity.ts:32 "an operator types the same three ids into `provision-device` ... and into this environment on the till, and a mismatch is still silent", provision-device.ts:53 "`01-F25`'s pairing code is owed in full", auth.ts:58. ops/README.md §5 states it in the runbook. No pairing-code mint, store, claim or redemption exists under any symbol name — I checked device_registry's columns and the protocol's message set for a claim path too.
- **AMENDED** — The cloud login endpoint has no rate limiting, no lockout, no session revocation, and no password reset — while every email is globally unique across all tenants, making it an account enumerator across the whole fleet.
  - The four absences are confirmed: router.ts:52-62 is findByEmail + verifyPin + issueSessionToken and nothing else; `grep -a` for rate.?limit|lockout|throttl|attempts in services/api/src returns only router.ts:47 and :56, both comments admitting the gap; session.ts:26 SESSION_TTL_MS = 12h and the file's own comment names "session rotation and revocation" as owed — there is no revocation list or logout procedure anywhere in services/api. Global email uniqueness confirmed at schema.ts:499, drizzle/0011:71, tenancy.ts:266, create-owner.ts:220-223. But "an account enumerator" overstates the public endpoint: router.ts:55-58 deliberately throws ONE identical UNAUTHORIZED for both "no such account" and "wrong password", with the reasoning written out. What remains is a TIMING oracle — verifyPin (Argon2id) runs only when a user row exists, and nothing rate-limits the probe — plus the explicit disclosure in create-owner.ts:220, which is on the vendor's own shell, not a reachable endpoint. Corrected claim: enumeration is a timing side-channel unblunted by the missing rate limiting, not a message-level enumerator.
- **AMENDED** — A restaurant's first owner receives a permanent, never-expiring password minted on the vendor's terminal, and there is no way for them to change it, reset it, or for a second user to ever be created.
  - All substantive claims hold: mintInitialSecret at create-owner.ts:148 is 24 random bytes base64url, printed to stdout at :261, with the shipped message at :255-259 saying "It does NOT expire and nothing forces a change on first login"; a second create-owner is refused at :185-196 via orgHasOwner, naming 14-F14's CRUD and "a lost password is a reset flow this product does not have yet"; users-postgres.ts:166-173 setAssignments throws exactly as quoted. The back office's client-side surface is exactly the 12 calls listed — I enumerated `trpc.*.*` in apps/backoffice/src and got that set verbatim, with no user, password, branch or org-settings call. Two corrections: (a) the API ROUTER exposes 17 procedures, not 12 (adds tenancy.directory, ops.voidAfterKot, catalog.editMenuPrices, catalog.runDayEnd, plus the B-2 echo fixtures) — 12 is what the back office calls, which is the load-bearing number but should be labelled as such; (b) there is one other credential path, server.ts:167-254's BOOTSTRAP_OWNER_EMAIL/_PASSWORD_HASH in-memory store, mutually exclusive with DATABASE_URL and a boot crash if both are set — it is a second SEED, not a second user, so the claim survives, but ops/README.md's limitations row "The owner account is in memory" is itself stale against ops/env/cloud.env.example, which sets DATABASE_URL and no BOOTSTRAP_* key.
- **CONFIRMED** — Device identity is a manually-copied 3-tuple with no verification at either end, and on the manager phone the device CREDENTIAL is baked into the app bundle at build time — one Expo build per physical device.
  - device-identity.ts:58-62 IDENTITY_ENV maps the three RESTOS_* keys; DEV_IDENTITY:45-49 is the three hardcoded UUIDs; resolveDeviceIdentity:79-84 falls back PER KEY to that seed (validation is only non-blank/non-padded, explicitly "not a UUID check"); the header at line 32 is verbatim as quoted, and describeDeviceIdentity exists for the stated reason. On the phone, branch.ts:79-84 declares the five EXPO_PUBLIC_RESTOS_* keys, branch.ts:66-70 explains babel-preset-expo substitutes member expressions AT BUILD TIME, and branch.ts:29-40 says "A credential inlined into a bundle is not the shipped design" and names expo-secure-store + 01-F25 as the closure. Since org/branch/device/token are all build-time constants, one build per physical device follows.
- **AMENDED** — There is zero billing, subscription, plan, quota or take-rate code, and the corpus deliberately specifies none — while restaurant-os.md §7 sells a PKR 8,000/branch/month subscription plus a 0-5% take-rate.
  - Facts confirmed and if anything understated: I widened the grep to include quota and metering and still got only 6 non-test files, every hit a comment or an unrelated fiscal-invoice reference (escpos/receipt-document.ts:41) — domain/tenancy.ts:196, schema.ts:354 and api/tenancy.ts:37 each say plan/billing/quota/metering are owned elsewhere and gate nothing. restaurant-os.md:108-110 reads as quoted; specs/15 §9.7 (line 182) is verbatim. Correction is to severity, not fact: this is not a blocker an architect can be dispatched to fix — the corpus forbids writing these FRs ("none should be written until §9.4's invoicing handoff has an owner"), so it is a founder/business decision with a spec PR in front of it, and filing it as an engineering blocker would invite exactly the commandment-2 invention the spec is guarding against.
- **CONFIRMED** — Bringing one restaurant into service requires roughly 25 discrete manual acts across three machines, of which at least 9 need a root/deploy shell on the service host, and the runbook itself states there is no error message for the most likely failure.
  - ops/README.md §1-§8 match the description step for step; ops/id.sh:5-11 is the quoted header verbatim ("ALL FOUR PROCESSES REPORT SUCCESS AND NO TILL EVER SEES A MENU. There is no error message anywhere in the product for this.") and §1 repeats it. Env key counts are exact: cloud.env.example 10 keys including ENABLED_BRANCHES, counter.env.example 9, kitchen.env.example 8. Shell-on-host acts: id.sh, migrate, create-org, create-branch, create-owner, list-tenancy, provision-device ×2, systemd cp/daemon-reload/enable — 9 before counting §2's box setup. The ~25 total is an UNDERCOUNT if anything: §6 alone is 8 acts (node+pnpm, clone, install, rebuild:native, env file, shortcut, BIOS, auto-logon) × 2 machines. One thing that partially mitigates the "nothing checks" clause without refuting it: ops/startup/*.bat now gate on the three identity keys being PRESENT (README §6 table) — presence, never agreement with what the gateway was told, which is the failure the header names.

## deployment

**Verdict.** The founder's claim is TRUE here and this dimension is the sharpest proof of it. There is no deployment artifact of any kind in the repo — zero Dockerfiles, zero compose files, zero Terraform/fly.toml/render.yaml/Helm/Procfile/vercel.json, verified by `find . -name node_modules -prune -o -type f` and independently by `git ls-files` (5 of 899 tracked files are YAML: one CI workflow, two conformance files, two pnpm files). What exists instead is `ops/` — 16 files whose own README opens with "Everything needed to install RestOS in **one restaurant**" (ops/README.md:3) and whose first script "mint[s] **this restaurant's** ids and secrets ONCE" (ops/id.sh:3) into a single ORG_ID, a single BRANCH_ID and exactly two device ids. The shipping topology is: one Linux box per restaurant running `git clone` at `/opt/restos`, four systemd units executing **TypeScript source through `tsx`** (every one of the 7 services has `"build": "echo 'no build yet — scaffold stub'"`; the gateway unit says so in a comment at restos-sync-gateway.service:22), plus two Windows machines running from a git checkout that recompile Electron on every launch, plus a local Postgres and Redis. No TLS anywhere, both services bind `0.0.0.0` in plain HTTP/WS. Updating is `git pull && pnpm install` by hand, per machine. The important nuance the architect needs: **the data plane is already pooled and the config plane is not.** Every kernel table is org-keyed, `services/jobs` already audits *every* org in the database (services/jobs/src/index.ts:133-146), and the most recent commit (8659587) landed real tenancy tables and four provisioning commands. But `ENABLED_BRANCHES`/`ENABLED_CHANNELS` are process environment variables folded into a single host-global `catalog.enabled` (services/api/src/server.ts:401-402,451), and the code states the consequence itself at services/api/src/catalog-router.ts:120-124: "one process serves one set." That commit's own OWED list names it: "ONE services/api PROCESS CANNOT SERVE TWO TENANTS with different branch topologies. A real multi-tenancy limit; it needs an architecture decision, not a patch." Ops maturity against the spec is near zero: doc 22 has 22 FRs and exactly 2 are implemented (22-F21, 22-F22), both explicitly labelled pilot interim; `docs/runbooks/` does not exist; there is zero OpenTelemetry, zero Sentry, zero health endpoints, zero device heartbeat, zero alerting; the vendor control plane `apps/platform-admin` that doc 15 gives 28 FRs is `export {};` at 2 LOC; and CI has no deploy job and runs only 3 of the repo's own 7 verify rails.

### Findings

- **Zero deployment artifacts of any kind exist in the repository — independently verified two ways.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* `find . -name node_modules -prune -o -name .git -prune -o -type f \( -iname 'Dockerfile*' -o -iname '*docker*' -o -iname '*.tf' -o -iname 'fly.toml' -o -iname 'render.yaml' -o -iname 'Procfile' -o -iname '*.nomad' -o -iname 'Chart.yaml' -o -iname 'vercel.json' \) -print` returns EMPTY. `git ls-files | grep -aE '\.ya?ml$'` returns exactly 5 of 899 tracked files: .github/workflows/ci.yml, conformance/01.yml, conformance/wave-0-scope.yml, pnpm-lock.yaml, pnpm-workspace.yaml. `git ls-files | grep -av '\.md$' | xargs grep -aln docker` matches only pnpm-lock.yaml (testcontainers). specs/00-platform-overview.md:117 says the chosen shape is "containerized deploy on a managed platform" — none of it exists.
- **Production runs interpreted TypeScript source from a git checkout. There is no build artifact for any service.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* All 7 services declare `"build": "echo 'no build yet — scaffold stub'"` (services/{api,sync-gateway,jobs,tax,whatsapp,foodpanda,intelligence}/package.json). Their `start` scripts are `tsx src/server.ts` / `tsx src/index.ts`. ops/systemd/restos-sync-gateway.service:22-23 states it: "tsx over source: there is no compiled artifact — `build` is an echo stub in every service." ops/README.md:66 installs by `sudo git clone <repo> /opt/restos`. There is no immutable, versioned, rollback-able deploy unit anywhere.
- **The back office rebuilds Next.js on every process restart, inside the systemd unit.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* ops/systemd/restos-backoffice.service:23 `ExecStartPre=/usr/bin/env pnpm -C apps/backoffice build`, with the comment at :18-21 "It costs a minute on every restart; on a load-shedding schedule that is the right trade." That is a correct trade for one box in Lahore and disqualifying for a fleet — restart time is coupled to compile time and to `node_modules` being present and intact on the host.
- **The cloud plane's per-tenant configuration is process-global environment, so one API process cannot serve two tenants. This is the single hardest blocker in this dimension.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* services/api/src/server.ts:401-402 reads `ENABLED_BRANCHES: list, ENABLED_CHANNELS: channels` from the process env; :451 folds them into `enabled: { branches, channels }` on one `CatalogDeps` shared by all requests. services/api/src/catalog-router.ts:127 `enabled: authorized(...).query(({ ctx }) => ctx.catalog.enabled)` returns it to every org; catalog.ts:204 `assertSavable(entry, enabled)` validates every org's save against it. The comment at catalog-router.ts:120-124 states it: "The answer is the DEPLOYMENT's set rather than a per-org lookup … one process serves one set." Commit 8659587's OWED list: "catalog.enabled IS HOST-GLOBAL … ONE services/api PROCESS CANNOT SERVE TWO TENANTS with different branch topologies."
- **The data plane, by contrast, is already pooled-capable and should not be rewritten.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* services/sync-gateway/src/schema.ts carries `org_id` on every ledger table (:42, :65, :73, :102, :140, :194, :220, :278, :301, :376, :413, :467) with org-first composite keys and indexes. services/jobs/src/index.ts:133-134 `select org_id from kernel.org_sequences order by org_id` and :146 `for (const org_id of await everyOrg(db))` — the Auditor already sweeps every tenant in one process. services/api scopes every read/write on `ctx.subject.org_id` (catalog-router.ts:135,166,180,203,222,247,272; device-router.ts:41,79). All 9 gateway `/internal/*` routes take org_id (publish-http.ts:260-411).
- **Per-restaurant identity and secrets are minted by a shell script into one file per install, and re-running it strands the install.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* ops/id.sh:3 "mint this restaurant's ids and secrets ONCE, into one file"; :44-47 generates exactly one ORG_ID, one BRANCH_ID, COUNTER_DEVICE_ID and KITCHEN_DEVICE_ID; :52-54 mints DEVICE_TOKEN_SECRET, PUBLISH_TOKEN, SESSION_SECRET. :17 "`--force` # overwrite (this ORPHANS every already-provisioned device)". :5-11 states the reason it exists: "Four processes have to agree on values that nothing anywhere checks … ALL FOUR PROCESSES REPORT SUCCESS AND NO TILL EVER SEES A MENU."
- **The BOOTSTRAP_ORG_ID join key has three ends, none checked, and one end is still unchecked after the August 2026 tenancy work.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* ops/id.sh:6-9 names the three: API `BOOTSTRAP_ORG_ID` == device `RESTOS_ORG_ID`; device `RESTOS_BRANCH_ID` ∈ API `ENABLED_BRANCHES`; provision-device `--device X` == device `RESTOS_DEVICE_ID`. ops/env/cloud.env.example:113-115 "NOTHING CHECKS THIS. Getting it wrong gives four healthy processes and no menu, with no error anywhere." packages/device-config/src/device-identity.ts:151-170 encodes the same warning in a runtime message rather than a check. plans/wave-1/running-the-stack.md §7 owed item 4: the API/back-office half is closed but "the set … is still not checked against the DEVICE's `branch_id`."
- **Doc 22 (backup/DR/retention/erasure/export/vendor-exit) is 2/22 implemented, and both implemented FRs are self-declared pilot interims.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* specs/22-operations-recovery.md defines 22 FRs (`grep -c '^- 22-F'` = 22, IDs 22-F1..22-F22). Only 22-F21 and 22-F22 are cited in code: ops/backup.sh:3,119,135,208-209,222 and ops/sqlite-backup.mjs:10,13, plus ops/systemd/restos-backup.service:2. 22-F22 itself says it "satisfies neither 22-F1, 22-F2 nor 22-F6." `docs/` does not exist at all, so 22-F5's `docs/runbooks/OWNERS` ("An unowned runbook fails CI") has no file and CI has no such check. `grep -arn 'governance\.' packages/domain/src services apps` returns zero — none of the six `governance.*` events specified in 22 §2 exist.
- **Real RPO is up to 24 hours against a specified ≤5 minutes, and the device half of the backup has no scheduler at all.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* specs/22:20 (22-F1) "RPO ≤ 5 min via continuous WAL archiving with point-in-time recovery; RTO ≤ 4 h." ops/backup.sh:208-210 "22-F1's RPO of 5 minutes DOES NOT HOLD here — the real RPO is however long it is between runs of this script. On a nightly timer that is up to 24 hours of orders." ops/systemd/restos-backup.timer:11 `OnCalendar=*-*-* 06:00:00`. restos-backup.service:19 runs `ops/backup.sh --cloud` only. ops/README.md:424-431: "ONLY THE CLOUD HALF IS AUTOMATED. THE TILL HALF IS A HUMAN … There is no scheduled task, no `.bat` and no timer anywhere in this kit that runs `--till`" — while the till holds the only copy of every unpushed sale (22-F21).
- **Backups are per-install shell scripts that discover local Electron userData directories — structurally unusable for a fleet.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* ops/backup.sh:85-107 `discover_data_dirs()` searches `$APPDATA`, `$HOME/.config`, `$HOME/Library/Application Support` two levels deep on the machine it runs on. :220 `pg_dump --format=custom … "$DATABASE_URL"` writes to a local `RESTOS_BACKUP_DIR`. :233-237 retention is `find -mtime +$KEEP_DAYS -delete`. ops/sqlite-backup.mjs:32 resolves better-sqlite3 through `apps/pos-electron/package.json`, i.e. it requires the app's checkout to be present. Nothing here can enumerate tenants, and nothing can run anywhere but on the machine being backed up.
- **Observability is entirely absent: zero OpenTelemetry, zero Sentry, zero health endpoints, zero device heartbeat, zero alerting — against explicit spec promises.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* `grep -arn 'opentelemetry|@sentry' --include=package.json` across apps/services/packages/root returns NOTHING; `grep -arln 'opentelemetry|Sentry|captureException' --include=*.ts(x)` returns NOTHING. specs/00-platform-overview.md:116 promises "OpenTelemetry traces/metrics; Sentry for errors; custom device heartbeat." Gateway HTTP surface is `/sync` (server.ts:81) plus 9 `/internal/*` routes (publish-http.ts:260-411) — no `/health`, `/healthz`, `/ready` anywhere; ops/README.md:585 "No health endpoint." No heartbeat table exists: services/sync-gateway/src/registry.ts:148-150 and services/api/src/devices.ts:34 both record app version/last-seen/sync-lag as "stored nowhere in this product." ops/README.md:584 and ops/systemd/restos-jobs.service:28-30: Auditor findings terminate in journald, read "weekly, by a human." restos-sync-gateway.service:34: "stdout is pino JSON; nothing in this product collects it."
- **The vendor control plane — the thing that makes a SaaS operable — is a 2-line stub, while its spec carries 28 FRs.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* apps/platform-admin/src/index.ts is `// Scaffold stub …` + `export {};` (2 LOC, 1 source file); package.json build is `echo 'no build yet — scaffold stub'`. specs/15-platform-admin.md defines 28 FRs (`grep -c '^- 15-F'` = 28) including 15-F11 fleet heartbeat dashboard, 15-F13 sync topology view, 15-F14 alerting, 15-F19..F21 rollout channels/version pinning/closed-hours forced updates, 15-F22 kill switches, 15-F23 per-org meters. Only 13 distinct 15-F IDs are cited anywhere in shipping code, and the provisioning ones land as shell commands, not a console: services/sync-gateway declares 7 CLI entry points (migrate, create-org, create-branch, create-owner, list-tenancy, provision-device, revoke-device).
- **Org suspension — the SaaS business lever — exists as a schema column and a type with zero enforcement and no writer.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* packages/domain/src/tenancy.ts:182 `export const ORG_STATUSES = ["active", "suspended"]`; services/sync-gateway/src/schema.ts:379 the column. `grep -arn 'status' services/sync-gateway/src/{gateway,auth}.ts services/api/src/trpc.ts` returns NOTHING — no admission path, no session path reads it. The only writer is the initial `insert into kernel.orgs` at services/sync-gateway/src/tenancy.ts:82-83; create-org.ts:134-135 explicitly refuses a `--status` flag. specs/15:34 (15-F7) requires suspension to gate cloud services and be reversible "within minutes." Today a suspended tenant's tills keep syncing and its owner keeps logging in.
- **Per-restaurant configuration is per-machine environment files because 00 §7's config plane does not exist and has no transport to a device.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* 21 distinct `RESTOS_*` keys are read by device code (`grep -arho 'RESTOS_[A-Z_]*' apps/pos-electron/src apps/pass-kds/src packages/device-config/src | sort -u`), including layer-2/3 settings 00 §7 assigns to the org and branch: RESTOS_STATION_ROUTES, RESTOS_AGING_THRESHOLDS, RESTOS_HARDWARE_TIER, RESTOS_PANEL_PPI, RESTOS_READY_SIGNAL_OWNER, RESTOS_SERVE_SIGNAL_OWNER, RESTOS_KOT_PRINTER, RESTOS_PRINTER. apps/pos-electron/src/main/station-routing.ts:44 and hardware-tier.ts:62 both state the reason: "layer 2 has no transport to a device." ops/env/counter.env.example (233 lines, 9 required keys) and kitchen.env.example (94 lines, 8 keys) are the actual configuration mechanism, typed by a technician on each machine.
- **CI has no deploy stage and runs only 3 of the repo's own 7 verify rails.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* .github/workflows/ci.yml is the only workflow: 2 jobs (`verify`, `fr-citation`), steps at :19-28 are docs:lint, typecheck, lint, test, build. No deploy job, no artifact upload, no environment, no image push, no migration step. Root package.json:`"verify": "pnpm docs:lint && pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm tokens:check && pnpm strings:check && pnpm seams:check && pnpm layout:check"` — 7 steps; CI runs 3 of them, so `tokens:check`, `strings:check`, `seams:check` and `layout:check` never gate a merge. (AGENTS.md's own correction of this list says six steps and omits `strings:check` — re-measured, it is seven.) `pnpm build` is meaningful for 3 of 17 workspaces; the other 14 echo.
- **Device fleet management does not exist: no installer, no signing, no auto-update, no rollout channels — and the startup scripts that stand in for it have never been executed.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* No `electron-builder` in any package.json (apps/pos-electron devDeps: @electron/rebuild, electron, electron-vite, vite, vitest, testing libs — no builder), despite specs/18:182 allowing it. apps/pos-electron `start` = `electron-vite build && electron out/main/index.js`, invoked by ops/startup/restos-counter.bat:169 `call pnpm -C apps\pos-electron start` inside a `:loop` — so each till recompiles from source at every launch and restart. ops/README.md:583 "No installer, no auto-update. Both Windows machines run from a git checkout. Updating is `git pull && pnpm install` and a restart, by hand, per machine." ops/README.md:290 and :623-643: "NONE OF THIS IS VERIFIED — there is no Windows machine on the box these files were written on"; the .bat gates "have been read against the code they describe and have never been executed."
- **No TLS anywhere; both cloud services bind 0.0.0.0 in plain HTTP/WS.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* services/sync-gateway/src/server.ts:227 `app.listen({ port: env.PORT, host: "0.0.0.0" })`; services/api/src/server.ts:518 the same. `grep -arn 'https\.createServer|node:tls|cert|ssl:' services/*/src` (non-test) returns NOTHING. ops/env/cloud.env.example:51-52 "Binds 0.0.0.0 in PLAIN HTTP/WS — there is no TLS in this product." ops/README.md:582 "Do not put port 8080 on the internet." Device tokens (JWT) and the back-office session bearer travel unencrypted unless an operator adds a reverse proxy the kit does not ship.
- **The runtime footprint per restaurant is 8 components across 3 machines, all manually configured, with a documented ~half-day install.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* plans/wave-1/running-the-stack.md and ops/README.md §2-§6: one Linux box (Postgres 16 + Redis 7 + 4 systemd units: restos-sync-gateway:8080, restos-api:3001, restos-backoffice:3000, restos-jobs) + 2 Windows machines (pos-electron counter, pass-kds kitchen). ops/README.md:24 "Budget half a day for steps 1–7 and a full service for step 8." Manual steps include `timedatectl set-timezone Asia/Karachi` (:64, because restos-backup.timer:8-10 depends on system TZ), `useradd`, `git clone`, `pnpm install`, a hand-run `migrate`, 3 tenancy commands, 2 provision-device commands, per-machine `rebuild:native`, and two BIOS/Windows settings (:229-233).
- **Redis is a hard boot dependency for the Auditor and fails silently if misconfigured.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* ops/systemd/restos-jobs.service:16-18 and ops/env/cloud.env.example:121-123: "REQUIRED. BullMQ's backend. ioredis retries a refused connection FOR EVER, so a worker with no Redis boots, prints its startup lines, and never runs a single audit pass — silently." In a pooled deployment this is the only thing checking money conservation across every tenant, and its failure mode is indistinguishable from health.

### Keep

- **The kernel Postgres schema — org-keyed on every table, with the ruling that no ledger table may carry a foreign key to the org registry**
  - *why:* This is the part that is already SaaS-shaped. Pooling many restaurants into one database needs no schema change: org_id is the leading key on events, watermarks, catalog versions/entries, org_events, quarantine, device_registry, orgs, branches and users. The no-FK ruling (commit 8659587) is correct and load-bearing — a constraint would refuse ingest for orgs whose events predate their registry row, i.e. refuse a sale a till has already rung (01-F17).
  - *size:* services/sync-gateway/src/schema.ts (~510 lines), 12 migrations, 12 kernel tables; org_id appears as a key/index column at schema.ts:42,51,53,55,65,73,77,102,124,140,171,194,200,220,244,247,278,286,301,315,320,376,413,434,467,501
- **services/jobs — the multi-org Auditor host on a BullMQ repeatable**
  - *why:* Already written for a fleet, not for one restaurant: one repeatable, one pass, one report per org per pass. It is the only correctness instrument in the product and it is the only process that already enumerates tenants. Under pooling it needs a concurrency/scale story, not a rewrite.
  - *size:* services/jobs/src/index.ts — everyOrg at :133-134 (`select org_id from kernel.org_sequences`), per-org loop at :146-165, repeatable named at :103
- **The 7 declared gateway CLI commands as BUSINESS LOGIC (not as an interface)**
  - *why:* migrate / create-org / create-branch / create-owner / list-tenancy / provision-device / revoke-device already encode the correct provisioning semantics — idempotent migration, refusal of a second owner, one-time password minted and never stored (15-F26), a token bound to one device id, revocation that evicts live sessions. These become the service layer under apps/platform-admin. Only the entry point (a shell on the service host) has to go.
  - *size:* services/sync-gateway/package.json scripts:2-9; create-org.ts, create-branch.ts, create-owner.ts, list-tenancy.ts, provision-device.ts, revoke-device.ts, migrate.ts — each with an __acceptance__ suite that spawns the DECLARED script
- **ops/sqlite-backup.mjs — the WAL-correct device backup, mutation-proved**
  - *why:* The device half of backup survives every topology, because 22-F21's premise is unchanged by pooling: until an event is pushed and acked the till holds the only copy. This script is the one piece of ops here that was measured rather than reasoned (M1 and M5 mutants both caught real defects; M3 SURVIVED and the file says so instead of implying otherwise). The restore procedure it documents — delete -wal/-shm before copying, count `events` rather than checking the file opens — is knowledge that cost two measurement rounds to acquire.
  - *size:* ops/sqlite-backup.mjs (82 lines) + ops/backup.sh:115-153 (backup_sqlite) + ops/README.md:441-520 (the two-shapes table and the restore procedure)
- **The env-contract documentation in ops/env/*.example and the boot-line contracts in the systemd units**
  - *why:* Not as a deployment mechanism, but as the only complete, honest catalogue of every knob and every silent failure mode — derived by grepping process.env across the tree rather than from a document (cloud.env.example:6-7). It is the correct input to a real per-org config schema, and the boot-line contracts (what each process must print, and what each variant means) translate directly into container healthchecks and readiness probes.
  - *size:* ops/env/cloud.env.example 138 lines, counter.env.example 233, kitchen.env.example 94; ops/systemd/*.service — 5 units carrying ordering, restart posture (Restart=always, StartLimitIntervalSec=0) and their reasons
- **The org-scoped request path in services/api and services/sync-gateway**
  - *why:* Every read and write already derives its scope from `ctx.subject.org_id` or an explicit org_id parameter, and the cross-tenant write found by the last commit (catalog.runDayEnd sweeping every org) was fixed rather than papered over. The isolation surface was attacked with 13 vectors, each with a control proving the other tenant's data was really present. That is the expensive part of multi-tenancy and it largely holds.
  - *size:* services/api/src/catalog-router.ts, device-router.ts, trpc.ts; services/sync-gateway/src/publish-http.ts (9 org-parameterised /internal routes); 01-F71

### Redo

- **The deploy unit itself**
  - *why:* There is no artifact. Production runs `tsx` over a git working copy, so 'what version is tenant X on' has no answer, rollback is `git checkout`, and a half-finished `git pull` is a broken restaurant. specs/00:117 already chose 'containerized deploy on a managed platform' and nothing was built toward it.
  - *shape:* Replace the 7 `echo` build stubs with real builds (tsc or esbuild to dist/), produce one immutable image per service tagged by commit, and deploy to a managed platform. The systemd units' operational knowledge (start ordering, restart posture, required boot lines) becomes container dependency ordering plus liveness/readiness probes. `pnpm -C services/sync-gateway migrate` becomes a pre-deploy job, not a step a human remembers.
- **Per-tenant configuration: ENABLED_BRANCHES / ENABLED_CHANNELS out of process env and into per-org rows**
  - *why:* This is the one hard blocker to pooling and the code names it as an architecture decision rather than a patch. Today one API process serves one branch/channel set to every caller, so two tenants with different branch topologies cannot share a process. It also currently crashes the API at boot on an unknown channel — a boot-crash config policy that would take down every tenant on the host.
  - *shape:* A `kernel.org_config` (or branch/channel projection over kernel.branches) read at request time keyed on `ctx.subject.org_id`, served by the same `catalog.enabled` procedure so the writer and the grid keep their single-declaration property (that identity was hard-won — the earlier NEXT_PUBLIC_ duplicate drifted silently). An unknown channel for one org becomes a refusal for that org, never a process crash.
- **Device and branch configuration: 21 RESTOS_* env vars per machine**
  - *why:* 00 §7's three config layers exist only on paper; the code states plainly that 'layer 2 has no transport to a device', so org- and branch-level settings (station routes, aging thresholds, signal ownership, hardware tier, printer targets) are typed by a technician into a .env file on each machine. That is not configurable per tenant from anywhere, it cannot be changed without walking to the machine, and it has no audit trail.
  - *shape:* config.changed events (already a legal 01-F62 org-scoped type) delivered over the existing sync protocol, resolved by packages/device-config at boot and on change. Devices keep only their identity and their cloud URL in the environment; everything else arrives from the tenant's record.
- **Backup and recovery**
  - *why:* 2 of 22 doc-22 FRs, RPO up to 24 h against a specified 5 minutes, the device half unscheduled entirely, and no drill has ever been run. `pg_dump` on a nightly systemd timer to a local directory does not scale past one box and does not meet the objective the spec sets for a single paying tenant, let alone a fleet.
  - *shape:* Managed Postgres with continuous WAL archiving and PITR (22-F1/F2), backup monitoring feeding a real alert surface (22-F6/22-N1), and quarterly restore drills verified by the existing Auditor refold (22-F8 — the instrument already exists in packages/auditor). The device-side backup stays, but as a scheduled job the till runs and reports on, not a command an operator remembers.
- **Tenant provisioning and device admission: shell commands on the service host → an audited control plane**
  - *why:* Onboarding a restaurant today requires SSH to the box: create-org, create-branch, create-owner, provision-device ×N, plus editing /etc/restos/cloud.env and restarting. None of it emits an event, so there is no audit trail of who created or revoked what — the commands say so themselves (a shell has no authenticated user and 01-F1 will not take a permanent null actor). An owner cannot add a till without vendor shell access.
  - *shape:* apps/platform-admin built for real: internal SSO + mandatory 2FA (15-F1), a typed confirmation naming the org for destructive acts (15-F2), an audit row per act (15-F3), org provisioning with plan and take-rate (15-F4/F5/F6). Separately, 01-F25's back-office pairing code so an owner adds a till from apps/backoffice. Once an authenticated actor exists, the provisioning events these commands correctly refuse to fake become writable.
- **Org lifecycle enforcement**
  - *why:* active|suspended is a column and a TypeScript union with zero readers in any enforcement path and no writer after insert. Suspension is the one lever a subscription business has, and 15-F7 specifies exactly how it must degrade (cloud services gated, sync still accepted, in-branch POS billing never stops).
  - *shape:* A status check at gateway hello and at API session issue, with the 15-F7 asymmetry preserved: refuse new cloud-originated work and the back office, keep accepting device pushes so a suspended restaurant never loses a sale. Transitions written as config.changed with the platform-admin actor.
- **Observability and fleet health**
  - *why:* Zero OTel, zero Sentry, zero health endpoints, zero heartbeat, zero alerting. 'Is it up?' is answered by `systemctl status` and by looking at the till. With N restaurants that is not a degraded answer, it is no answer. Note this needs no dependency process: @sentry/node, @sentry/nextjs and the OpenTelemetry SDK packages are already on specs/18 §14's allowlist.
  - *shape:* OTel traces/metrics + Sentry with the 22-F12 PII scrubbing rules; /health and /ready on both services; a real device heartbeat table (app version, last-seen, sync lag) so 14-F12's device list and 15-F11's fleet dashboard stop rendering ABSENT; Auditor findings routed to an on-call channel instead of journald.
- **CI/CD**
  - *why:* One workflow, no deploy stage, no artifact, no environment, and 4 of the repo's 7 verify rails never run on a merge. There is no path from a green build to a running tenant.
  - *shape:* Add build → image → migrate → deploy with per-environment gates; run all 7 rails (layout:check needs a display, so it needs a headed runner or an explicit documented exemption rather than silent absence); produce signed device builds via electron-builder and promote them through 15-F19's internal → dev-pilot → fleet channels with 15-F20 pinning and 15-F21 closed-hours windows.
- **TLS termination**
  - *why:* Both services bind 0.0.0.0 in plain HTTP/WS. Device JWTs and the back-office session bearer are on the wire in clear. Acceptable only while everything is on one restaurant's LAN — which is precisely the topology being abandoned.
  - *shape:* TLS at the platform edge for the API and back office; wss:// for the device sync endpoint, with the device's cloud URL becoming a platform hostname rather than ws://CLOUD_HOST:8080/sync.

### Delete

- **ops/id.sh**
  - *why:* A per-install secret minter is actively harmful under pooling. It mints ONE org, ONE branch and exactly TWO device ids per checkout (ops/id.sh:44-47) and its own --force path 'ORPHANS every already-provisioned device' (:17). In a pooled deployment, device token and publish secrets are platform secrets, and org/branch/device ids are minted by the control plane at provisioning time — which create-org/create-branch/provision-device already do when --org/--branch are omitted.
- **ops/startup/restos-counter.bat and restos-kitchen.bat**
  - *why:* 379 lines of Windows batch that have NEVER BEEN EXECUTED — the kit says so twice (ops/README.md:290, :623-643), and their gates were 'read against the code they describe' only. A retry loop wrapped around `pnpm start` (which recompiles Electron each pass) is not a fleet update mechanism, and it cannot distinguish a permanent configuration refusal from a crash because the app has no exit code beyond non-zero (README:270-277). Replace with a signed installer + auto-update channel.
- **The BOOTSTRAP_OWNER_EMAIL / _PASSWORD_HASH / BOOTSTRAP_ORG_ID trio**
  - *why:* Already named a stopgap by its own FR (15-F26) and mutually exclusive with DATABASE_URL — set both and the API refuses to boot (ops/env/cloud.env.example:102-107). It is a second answer to 'who may sign in', it creates an owner in a Map that dies with the process, and 14-F14's CRUD can never deactivate it. kernel.users + create-owner supersedes it; keeping it alive keeps a path where a restart silently changes which account exists.
- **The per-restaurant Linux box + 5 systemd units as the SHIPPING topology**
  - *why:* One box per restaurant running four `tsx` processes from a git checkout, with a local Postgres and Redis, is a per-install architecture by construction — it cannot be pooled, cannot be monitored centrally, cannot be updated without SSH, and multiplies the whole ops surface by the customer count. Keep the unit files as a dev/pilot fixture and as documentation of the boot-line and restart contracts; do not ship them.
- **ENABLED_CHANNELS as a boot-crashing process environment variable**
  - *why:* A closed set validated at boot, where an unknown value crashes the API by design (ops/env/cloud.env.example:81-84). That is the right posture for one restaurant — boot is the last moment an operator can fix it — and exactly wrong for a shared process, where one tenant's bad channel value would take down every other tenant on the host.
- **ops/README.md's 'Day zero' as an installation procedure**
  - *why:* Half a day of manual steps per restaurant — timedatectl, useradd, git clone, pnpm install, hand-run migrate, three tenancy commands, two provision commands, per-machine rebuild:native, two BIOS/Windows settings, and two documented traps with no error message. Its content is valuable as the record of every silent failure mode and the cash SOP; its shape is the deviation. The onboarding path becomes a control-plane wizard, and the operator-facing survivors are the training and cash-SOP sections.

### Open questions

- POOLED OR CELL-PER-TENANT? The data plane is already pooled (org-keyed schema, jobs sweeping every org) but the config plane forces one process per org. Two coherent answers exist and they cost very differently: (a) true pooling — move ENABLED_* to per-org rows and run one API/gateway fleet for all tenants; (b) cell-per-tenant — keep the host-global config and give each restaurant its own container set on shared infrastructure, which requires almost no application change but multiplies per-tenant cost and makes cross-tenant reporting hard. Do not let this be decided by whoever touches catalog-router.ts first.
- WHAT DOES 'SaaS' MEAN FOR SIGNUP? specs/15-F26 states outright there is NO self-service signup, and services/sync-gateway/src/create-org.ts:33-36 records rejecting a public form on that ground, calling it a decision rather than a gap (15 §9.7 leaves whether one should exist as a business question). If the founder means restaurants literally 'sign up', that is a spec change to doc 15 before any code. If he means vendor-onboarded multi-tenant, 15-F26 already holds and only the console is owed.
- DOES THE RESTAURANT STILL GET AN ON-PREM COMPONENT? Design law 5 (restaurant-os.md §2) makes LAN-first in-branch coordination the technical moat, and 00 §5.1 forbids any in-branch feature requiring WAN — but the LAN mesh is hosted by nothing today, so the till and the kitchen screen already talk through the cloud and the kitchen stops receiving orders when the internet drops (ops/README.md:579). A pure-cloud SaaS shape makes that permanent. Either the mesh gets a host (Wave-0 work, still owed) or design law 5 is amended. This is a product ruling, not an infrastructure one.
- WHAT RPO/RTO IS ACTUALLY BEING SOLD? 22-F1 says ≤5 min / ≤4 h; today it is up to 24 h and untested. That number appears in no contract, and 22-F5 requires a NAMED human owner of the backup runbook (in a docs/runbooks/OWNERS file that does not exist). Committing to 22-F1 is a managed-Postgres line item plus quarterly drill time; committing to something weaker is a written promise to tenants. Someone has to pick and sign.
- WHERE DOES THE DEVICE FLEET UPDATE FROM, AND WHO PAYS FOR THE FIRST INSTALL? Today it is a git checkout per machine and `git pull` by hand, with no installer and no signing. specs/15-F19..F21 specify rollout channels, per-org version pinning and forced updates only inside closed hours. Building that is a module. The interim — a technician visiting each restaurant — is exactly the per-install economics the SaaS model is meant to escape, and it interacts with the PKR 8,000/branch/month price and the 'done-for-you onboarding' line in restaurant-os.md §7.
- IS THE CURRENT ONE-RESTAURANT KIT STILL A SUPPORTED DEPLOYMENT MODE? Some pilots may already be running on it. If yes, ops/ has to be maintained alongside the SaaS path and the two configuration mechanisms must be kept from drifting (this repo has documented that exact drift three times). If no, say so and freeze ops/ — but note that ops/README.md, backup.sh and sqlite-backup.mjs currently hold operational knowledge recorded nowhere else, and freezing must not mean deleting.

### Adversarial verdicts

- **AMENDED** — Zero deployment artifacts of any kind exist in the repository — independently verified two ways.
  - The two greps reproduce exactly (the container/IaC find returns EMPTY; git ls-files gives the same 5 YAML files; the non-md docker grep hits only pnpm-lock.yaml), and specs/00-platform-overview.md:115 (not :117) carries the 'containerized deploy on a managed platform' phrase. But 'of any kind' is false: `git ls-files ops` returns 16 TRACKED deployment artifacts — six systemd units (restos-{sync-gateway,api,backoffice,jobs,backup}.service + restos-backup.timer), three env templates, two Windows startup launchers, ops/id.sh, ops/backup.sh, ops/sqlite-backup.mjs and a ~650-line ops/README.md runbook. The find command's own filename filter could not have seen any of them. Corrected claim: zero CONTAINERIZATION/IaC/PaaS artifacts exist; the shipped deployment mechanism is a hand-installed single-box systemd kit plus two Windows shortcuts. Severity is major, not blocker — a deploy path exists, it is just not the one 00 §3 names.
- **CONFIRMED** — Production runs interpreted TypeScript source from a git checkout. There is no build artifact for any service.
  - All 7 services declare `"build": "echo 'no build yet — scaffold stub'"` verbatim. restos-sync-gateway.service:22-23 reads exactly as quoted ('tsx over source: there is no compiled artifact — `build` is an echo stub in every service.' / ExecStart=... pnpm -C services/sync-gateway start), and ops/README.md:65 is `sudo git clone <repo> /opt/restos`. One correction that does not touch the claim: only 3 of the 7 (api, sync-gateway, jobs) have a `start` script at all — tax, whatsapp, foodpanda and intelligence have neither `dev` nor `start` and cannot be run as processes.
- **CONFIRMED** — The back office rebuilds Next.js on every process restart, inside the systemd unit.
  - ops/systemd/restos-backoffice.service:23 is `ExecStartPre=/usr/bin/env pnpm -C apps/backoffice build`, and :18-21 carries the quoted rationale word for word including 'It costs a minute on every restart; on a load-shedding schedule that is the right trade'. :38-39 confirms the coupling to a writable checkout (`ReadWritePaths=/opt/restos`, 'The Next build writes apps/backoffice/.next'). Restart time is coupled to compile time and to node_modules on the host, as claimed.
- **CONFIRMED** — The cloud plane's per-tenant configuration is process-global environment, so one API process cannot serve two tenants. This is the single hardest blocker in this dimension.
  - Every line number checks out: services/api/src/server.ts:401-402 (`ENABLED_BRANCHES: list, ENABLED_CHANNELS: channels`), :451 (`enabled: { branches: env.ENABLED_BRANCHES, channels: env.ENABLED_CHANNELS }` on one CatalogDeps built once in the composition root), catalog-router.ts:127 (`enabled: authorized(...).query(({ ctx }) => ctx.catalog.enabled)`) with the quoted comment at :122-124 verbatim. assertSavable is declared at catalog.ts:204 and iterates `enabled.branches × enabled.channels` at :223-224 for every org. I searched for the property, not the symbol — no per-org enabled lookup exists anywhere (`enabledFor|orgEnabled|branchesFor` return nothing; the only org-keyed tenancy reads are kernel.orgs/branches, which no catalog path consults). Commit 8659587's message lines 97-99 state the same conclusion in the repo's own words.
- **AMENDED** — Per-restaurant identity and secrets are minted by a shell script into one file per install, and re-running it strands the install.
  - The first half is exact — ops/id.sh:3, :44-47 (one ORG_ID, one BRANCH_ID, two device ids), :52-54 (three secrets), :17's --force comment, all verbatim. The second half is wrong: re-running the script does NOT strand anything. :25-32 is a guard that exits 1 without writing when ops/ids.env exists, printing the stranding explanation; only an explicit `--force` overwrites. Corrected claim: identity and secrets are minted per install by a shell script into one gitignored file, and the destructive path is guarded behind an explicit --force flag. The real deployment consequence is the one in finding 4 — one ids.env per install means one restaurant per deployment — so severity is major rather than blocker on its own.
- **AMENDED** — The BOOTSTRAP_ORG_ID join key has three ends, none checked, and one end is still unchecked after the August 2026 tenancy work.
  - 'None checked' is refuted on the third end. services/sync-gateway/src/gateway.ts:415 refuses the hello outright when `claims.device_id !== message.device_id || claims.branch_id !== message.branch_id` ('token claims do not match hello identity (01-F27)'), :417-421 refuses an unregistered (org, device) pair, and :430 refuses when `registry.branch_id !== claims.branch_id`. So pasting device X's token onto a machine whose RESTOS_DEVICE_ID is Y is a loud AuthRejectedError, not a silent success — the id.sh:6-9 end that IS enforced. The other two ends are confirmed unchecked exactly as claimed: cloud.env.example:113-115 ('NOTHING CHECKS THIS'), packages/device-config/src/device-identity.ts:156-173 describeDeviceIdentity encodes the warning as a boot MESSAGE with no comparison, and running-the-stack.md §7 owed item 4 says the agreed set 'is still not checked against the DEVICE's branch_id'. Corrected claim: two of the three ends (API BOOTSTRAP_ORG_ID vs device RESTOS_ORG_ID; device RESTOS_BRANCH_ID vs API ENABLED_BRANCHES) are unchecked and fail silently; the device-token end is checked and refuses loudly at the gateway.
- **CONFIRMED** — Doc 22 (backup/DR/retention/erasure/export/vendor-exit) is 2/22 implemented, and both implemented FRs are self-declared pilot interims.
  - `grep -c '^- 22-F'` = 22. A repo-wide `grep -arn '22-F[0-9]*'` over ops/services/packages/apps returns only 22-F21, 22-F22 as implementations plus 22-F1/22-F2 cited negatively at backup.sh:208-209 ('this is a pre-22-F2 interim'; '22-F1's RPO of 5 minutes DOES NOT HOLD here'). `docs/` does not exist (ls: No such file or directory), so 22-F5's docs/runbooks/OWNERS has no file and CI (the only workflow) has no such check. I searched the governance events by PROPERTY not symbol — `erasure|legal_hold|export_generated|restore_drill` across packages/services/apps hits only comments in customer-file schemas and registry.ts:881-882 saying doc 22 owns erasure and nothing here designs one. Zero producers, zero schemas.
- **CONFIRMED** — Real RPO is up to 24 hours against a specified ≤5 minutes, and the device half of the backup has no scheduler at all.
  - specs/22:20 (22-F1) is verbatim 'RPO ≤ 5 min via continuous WAL archiving ... RTO ≤ 4 h'. ops/backup.sh:208-210 and the runtime warning at :222 state the 24-hour reality in the repo's own words. restos-backup.timer:11 is `OnCalendar=*-*-* 06:00:00` and restos-backup.service:19 is `ExecStart=/opt/restos/ops/backup.sh --cloud` — --cloud only. ops/README.md:424-431 reads exactly as quoted, including 'There is no scheduled task, no .bat and no timer anywhere in this kit that runs --till' and the reason (the till is Windows, the unit sets ProtectHome=true). I checked for a till scheduler under other names — no .bat, no schtasks, no Task Scheduler XML is tracked anywhere in the repo.
- **CONFIRMED** — Backups are per-install shell scripts that discover local Electron userData directories — structurally unusable for a fleet.
  - ops/backup.sh:85-107 is `discover_data_dirs()` iterating `$APPDATA`, `$HOME/.config`, `$HOME/Library/Application Support` with `find -maxdepth 2 -name device.db` — machine-local by construction (RESTOS_TILL_DATA_DIR only overrides with another local path). :220 is the `pg_dump --format=custom --no-owner --no-privileges --file="$DUMP" "$DATABASE_URL"` writing into local RESTOS_BACKUP_DIR; :233-237 is `find ... -mtime +$KEEP_DAYS -delete/-exec rm -rf`. ops/sqlite-backup.mjs:31-32 resolves better-sqlite3 through `createRequire(resolve(repoRoot, 'apps/pos-electron/package.json'))`, so it requires the app checkout present. Nothing enumerates tenants; no off-host or object-storage target exists.
- **CONFIRMED** — Observability is entirely absent: zero OpenTelemetry, zero Sentry, zero health endpoints, zero device heartbeat, zero alerting — against explicit spec promises.
  - No package.json anywhere (outside node_modules) names opentelemetry or @sentry; no source file imports Sentry/captureException/prom-client. specs/00:112 promises 'OpenTelemetry traces/metrics; Sentry for errors; custom device heartbeat'. No /health, /healthz, /ready, /livez route exists in either service; the gateway surface is /sync (server.ts:81) plus 8 /internal routes at publish-http.ts:260-411 (the finding says 9 — the ninth match is an app.register, not a route; immaterial). registry.ts:148-152 and services/api/src/devices.ts:28-38 both say app version/last-seen/sync-lag are 'stored nowhere in this product' and 'no heartbeat table exists'. restos-jobs.service:28-30 and README:584-585 confirm findings terminate in journald, read weekly by a human; restos-sync-gateway.service:34 confirms nothing collects the pino JSON. One nuance that does not rescue anything: packages/sync-protocol has a wire-level ping/pong session keepalive (messages.ts:275), but it is per-socket, persisted nowhere and surfaced nowhere — it is not the fleet heartbeat 00 §3 and 15-F11 mean.
- **CONFIRMED** — The vendor control plane — the thing that makes a SaaS operable — is a 2-line stub, while its spec carries 28 FRs.
  - apps/platform-admin/src/index.ts is literally two lines ('// Scaffold stub — implementation arrives via plans/ tasks (24 §9). Spec: specs/15-platform-admin.md' + 'export {};') and is the app's only source file; its build is the echo stub. `grep -c '^- 15-F'` = 28. A repo-wide symbol sweep of shipping src returns 13 distinct 15-F ids (F1, F3, F4, F5, F7, F8, F10, F11, F14, F24, F25, F26, F27) and most are comment citations naming what is owed, not implementations — so 13 is an upper bound. services/sync-gateway/package.json declares exactly the 7 named CLI entry points (migrate, create-org, create-branch, create-owner, list-tenancy, provision-device, revoke-device).
- **CONFIRMED** — Org suspension — the SaaS business lever — exists as a schema column and a type with zero enforcement and no writer.
  - packages/domain/src/tenancy.ts:182 is `export const ORG_STATUSES = ["active", "suspended"] as const;`; schema.ts:379 is the `status: text("status").notNull()` column with the 15-F25 comment. I searched by property rather than one symbol: ORG_STATUSES/OrgStatus have NO importers outside packages/domain (only its own schema at :203 and the barrel re-export at index.ts:140/143 — and per the repo's own Rule A, a barrel re-export is not a use). No admission path (gateway.ts handleHello) or session path (api/src/trpc.ts) reads a status at all; readOrg's only non-CLI caller is publish-http.ts:343, a read for the tenancy listing, not a gate. The only writer is `insert into kernel.orgs` at tenancy.ts:82, and create-org.ts:133-135 explicitly refuses a --status flag. specs/15:34 (15-F7) requires reversible gating within minutes.
- **CONFIRMED** — Per-restaurant configuration is per-machine environment files because 00 §7's config plane does not exist and has no transport to a device.
  - Re-measured: the device-side sweep returns 24 distinct RESTOS_* keys (the finding says 21 — understated, which strengthens rather than weakens it), and every layer-2/3 key named is present: RESTOS_STATION_ROUTES, RESTOS_AGING_THRESHOLDS, RESTOS_HARDWARE_TIER, RESTOS_PANEL_PPI, RESTOS_READY_SIGNAL_OWNER, RESTOS_SERVE_SIGNAL_OWNER, RESTOS_KOT_PRINTER, RESTOS_PRINTER. station-routing.ts:44 and hardware-tier.ts:62 both state 'layer 2 has no transport to a device — config.changed is org-scoped under 01-F62 and no device folds it' in the code's own words. ops/env/counter.env.example is 233 lines and kitchen.env.example 94, typed per machine per ops/README.md:226-229.
- **CONFIRMED** — CI has no deploy stage and runs only 3 of the repo's own 7 verify rails.
  - .github/workflows contains only ci.yml; its two jobs are `verify` (steps at :19-28 = docs:lint, typecheck, lint, test, build) and `fr-citation`. No deploy job, no artifact upload, no environment, no image push, no migration step. Root package.json:17 `verify` is seven steps (docs:lint, typecheck, lint, tokens:check, strings:check, seams:check, layout:check), so tokens/strings/seams/layout never gate a merge — and the finding's re-measurement against AGENTS.md's six-step list is right, strings:check is the omitted one. One denominator correction that does not affect the claim: `pnpm build` is meaningful for 3 of 26 workspaces (backoffice next build, pos-electron and pass-kds electron-vite build), not 3 of 17 — the other 23 echo.
- **CONFIRMED** — Device fleet management does not exist: no installer, no signing, no auto-update, no rollout channels — and the startup scripts that stand in for it have never been executed.
  - I searched the property, not one symbol: electron-builder, electron-forge, @electron/packager, electron-updater and update-electron-app appear in NO package.json in the repo; apps/pos-electron devDeps are exactly as listed (@electron/rebuild, electron, electron-vite, vite, vitest, testing libs). specs/18:182 does allow electron-builder. pos-electron `start` is `electron-vite build && electron out/main/index.js`, called at ops/startup/restos-counter.bat:170 inside the `:loop` at :168 — so every launch recompiles. ops/README.md:583 is the quoted 'No installer, no auto-update' row, and :290 ('NONE OF THIS IS VERIFIED — there is no Windows machine on the box these files were written on') and :621-643 ('read against the code they describe and have never been executed') are verbatim.
- **CONFIRMED** — No TLS anywhere; both cloud services bind 0.0.0.0 in plain HTTP/WS.
  - services/sync-gateway/src/server.ts:227 and services/api/src/server.ts:518 are both `await app.listen({ port: env.PORT, host: "0.0.0.0" })`. A property search across services/*/src for https.createServer, node:tls, cert and ssl (non-test) returns nothing — no fastify `https:` option, no cert path, no proxy config shipped. ops/env/cloud.env.example:51-52 says 'Binds 0.0.0.0 in PLAIN HTTP/WS — there is no TLS in this product' and ops/README.md:582 says 'Do not put port 8080 on the internet.' Device JWTs and the back-office bearer therefore travel in clear unless an operator adds a proxy this kit does not ship.
- **CONFIRMED** — The runtime footprint per restaurant is 8 components across 3 machines, all manually configured, with a documented ~half-day install.
  - ops/README.md:23 is 'Budget half a day for steps 1–7 and a full service for step 8.' §2 (:60-70) specifies one Linux box with Postgres 16, Redis 7, and the four systemd units exist as tracked files (sync-gateway:8080, api:3001, backoffice:3000, jobs) — six cloud components plus two Windows apps across three machines. Every manual step named is present: `sudo timedatectl set-timezone Asia/Karachi` at :64 (required because restos-backup.timer:8-10 depends on system TZ), useradd, git clone, pnpm install at :65-67, and the two BIOS/Windows settings at :229-233. Severity major is right — this is descriptive of scale, not a defect.
- **CONFIRMED** — Redis is a hard boot dependency for the Auditor and fails silently if misconfigured.
  - ops/systemd/restos-jobs.service:16-18 and ops/env/cloud.env.example:121-123 carry the quoted text verbatim. It is borne out in code, not just comments: services/jobs/src/index.ts:191 makes REDIS_URL a hard-required env, and :235-236 construct `new IORedis(env.REDIS_URL, { maxRetriesPerRequest: null })` — infinite retry, so a worker pointed at a dead Redis boots, prints its startup lines (which are emitted at :216 BEFORE anything touches Redis) and runs no pass, with no health endpoint and no alerting (finding 10) to notice. Failure is genuinely indistinguishable from health.

## commercial

**Verdict.** The founder's complaint is not merely true here — this is the dimension where it is total. There is no commercial plane. A comment-stripped sweep of all 291 shipping (non-test) TypeScript files under `apps/`, `services/` and `packages/` returns **zero** occurrences of `subscription`, `billing`, `invoice`, `dunning`, `entitlement`, `take_rate`/`takeRate`, `plan_id`/`planId`, `feature_flag`/`featureFlag`, `trial`, `add_on`, `metering` and `usage_recorded`. The single hit in the entire commercial vocabulary is the string literal `"suspended"` inside a closed set at `packages/domain/src/tenancy.ts:182` that no shipping code writes and no shipping code reads to make a decision. The vendor's own host app, `apps/platform-admin/src/index.ts`, is 2 lines (`export {}`); `services/tax/src/index.ts` is 2 lines. The two surfaces the 5% take-rate is levied ON — storefront (doc 06) and WhatsApp (doc 07) — are 2-line stubs too, so the take-rate has no revenue base and its metering event has no producer. Worse than unbuilt: `metering.usage_recorded` (the one billing event in the whole corpus, `06-F22`) and `config.changed` (the vehicle `15-F5`/`F6`/`F25` use for plan, flags, take-rate and suspension) both have **no payload schema** in `packages/domain/src/registry.ts` — 34 branch-scoped + 7 audit schemas, neither of them present — so under `01-F4` they are *unemittable*, not merely unwired. And the corpus did this on purpose: `specs/15-platform-admin.md:182` states that "billing, subscriptions, plans, quotas and metering-to-invoice" are "deliberately NOT specified anywhere in this corpus" and that "no FR anywhere makes a payment state gate a service, and none should be written". So the gap is not drift from the spec — the spec **is** the gap, and code implemented it faithfully. The one genuinely good piece of news, and it is significant: the event ledger already carries everything a billing engine needs (required closed `channel` enum on `order.created`, snapshotted `unit_price_paisa` on `order.line_added`, `amount_paisa` + closed `method` + a `purpose` discriminator on `payment.recorded`, all integer paisa, all indexed by `org_id`), plus a running BullMQ repeatable host in `services/jobs`. Metering is the one part that is nearly free. Charging is 100% greenfield.

### Findings

- **There is zero commercial code in the shipping tree. A comment-stripped scan of all 291 non-test .ts/.tsx files under apps/, services/, packages/ finds 0 hits for subscription, billing, invoic, dunning, entitlement, take_rate, takeRate, plan_id, planId, feature_flag, featureFlag, trial, add_on, meterin, usage_recorded. The only commercial-vocabulary hit in the entire shipping source is one string literal.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* Comment-stripped node scan over `find apps services packages -name '*.ts' -o -name '*.tsx' | grep -v node_modules | grep -v __acceptance__ | grep -v '\.test\.'` = 291 files. Output: subscription 0, billing 0, invoic 0, dunning 0, entitlement 0, take_rate 0, takeRate 0, plan_id 0, planId 0, feature_flag 0, featureFlag 0, trial 0, add_on 0, meterin 0, usage_recorded 0, suspend 1 → `packages/domain/src/tenancy.ts:182: export const ORG_STATUSES = ["active", "suspended"] as const;`. (The 11 `addOn` hits are Electron native-addon paths at `apps/pos-electron/src/main/index.ts:93,292,500` and menu-modifier UI strings at `apps/backoffice/src/components/entry-editor.tsx:82-84` — unrelated.)
- **`metering.usage_recorded` — the single billing event the corpus defines — has no payload schema in packages/domain, which under 01-F4 makes it UNEMITTABLE, not merely unbuilt. Same class as the 05-F7 and 02-F8 gaps this repo has already paid for twice.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* Spec: `specs/06-storefront.md:66` (06-F22) defines `metering.usage_recorded { kind: 'own_channel_order', order_id, order_value_paisas, rate_bps, fee_paisas }`; reused by `specs/07-whatsapp-channel.md:39,63`; listed in the `01 §4` catalog at `specs/01-kernel-sync.md:188`. Code: `packages/domain/src/registry.ts` `payloadSchemas` holds exactly 34 keys (programmatically extracted) — order.created … customer.address_added — plus 7 `audit.*`; `metering.usage_recorded` is in neither. Grep for `usage_recorded` across apps/services/packages returns zero hits.
- **`config.changed` — the event 15-F5/F6/F25 route plan changes, feature-flag changes, take-rate changes and suspension through — is allowlisted by type at the gateway but has no payload schema and its body is explicitly NOT validated. A take-rate change today is either unemittable or unstructured JSON.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* Allowlisted: `services/sync-gateway/src/org-events.ts:32-38` `ORG_SCOPED_EVENT_TYPES = ["catalog.changed","device.registered","device.revoked","user.changed","config.changed"]`. Not validated: `services/sync-gateway/src/org-events.ts:79-80` — "The payload body is **not** validated here … `packages/domain` exports no per-type payload schema". Confirmed absent from `packages/domain/src/registry.ts` payloadSchemas (34 keys, no `config.changed`). Corroborated by a test comment at `services/sync-gateway/src/__acceptance__/catalog-pricing.test.ts:90` ("a `config.changed` payload schema — **none of which exists**").
- **The host that owns the entire commercial plane does not exist. specs/15 is 182 lines fully specifying plan control, take-rate, feature flags and metering; apps/platform-admin is a 2-line stub. services/tax, which owns the paid add-on, is also 2 lines.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* `apps/platform-admin/src/index.ts` = 2 LOC; `services/tax/src/index.ts` = 2 LOC (measured by `wc -l`). Compare `services/sync-gateway` 22,925 LOC, `services/api` 12,267 LOC, `apps/backoffice` 11,306 LOC. `plans/wave-1/wave-1-scope-reconciliation.md:98` already records: `| 15 Platform admin | 1+ | **none** | apps/platform-admin stub |`.
- **The take-rate has no revenue base built. The 5% is levied on storefront/WhatsApp/QR order value; both of those apps are 2-line stubs, so the only channels a shipping till can ring are counter/phone/foodpanda — and foodpanda is explicitly NOT own-channel.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* `apps/storefront/src/index.ts` = 2 LOC; `services/whatsapp/src/index.ts` = 2 LOC. `packages/domain/src/registry.ts:40` `ORDER_CHANNELS = ["counter","phone","storefront","whatsapp","foodpanda"]` — the enum exists, the producers do not. 06-F22's `rate_bps` is "read from the org's platform-admin metering config" (`specs/06-storefront.md:66`), which does not exist either.
- **There is no entitlement gate of any kind. Authorization is purely role-based: 25 actions × 4 roles, with no plan, add-on or payment-state dimension anywhere in the matrix.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* `packages/domain/src/permissions.ts:109-152` — programmatically extracted PERMISSION_ACTIONS = 25 entries (order.create … report.sales_view); ROLES = ["cashier","branch_manager","storekeeper","owner"]. Not one action names a plan, add-on, quota or subscription state. (AGENTS.md's "22"/"23" counts are stale; re-measured 25.)
- **The closest thing in the tree to a per-tenant feature flag is a process-wide environment variable, and the code says so out loud. This is the single-tenant artifact sitting at the exact seam where a per-org entitlement lookup would live.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* `services/api/src/catalog-router.ts:123-125`: "The answer is the DEPLOYMENT's set rather than a per-org lookup, and that is stated rather than implied: `00 §7`'s layer-2 config plane does not exist, so **one process serves one set**". Wired at `services/api/src/server.ts:401-402` (`ENABLED_BRANCHES`, `ENABLED_CHANNELS`) and consumed at `:451` (`enabled: { branches: env.ENABLED_BRANCHES, channels: env.ENABLED_CHANNELS }`). Adding a second tenant's branch requires editing an env file and restarting the API for every existing tenant.
