- **`00 §7` layer 1 (the vendor configuration layer that holds provisioning, feature flags/tier gates, take-rate % and rollout channels) has NO implementation. packages/device-config implements only layers 2 and 3, and every one of those keys is read from a device's own process environment.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* `specs/00-platform-overview.md:167` defines layer 1. `packages/device-config/src/` contains aging.ts, device-identity.ts, dev-staff.ts, lan-mesh.ts, panel-density.ts, quick-tags.ts, serve-signal.ts — all annotated `00 §7` layer 2 or layer 3 (e.g. `panel-density.ts:2` "layer 3 — panel_ppi", `serve-signal.ts:67` "layer 2", `aging.ts:75` "layer 2"). `packages/config/src/index.ts` (51 LOC total) is only `defineEnv`, an env-var parser. Multiple files record that even layer 2 has no delivery mechanism: `quick-tags.ts:17`, `aging.ts:23`, `serve-signal.ts:36` — "Layer 2 has no transport to a device".
- **The tax add-on's "off by default" is not a config key, not a build flag, and not a runtime gate — it is off because services/tax is empty. A paying add-on customer and a non-paying one are byte-identical in this product.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* Spec: `specs/16-tax-module.md:28` (16-F1 "Tax is off by default. Enabling any posture or the add-on is an explicit org action recorded as `config.changed`"), `:77` (16-F25 enablement gate on adapter certification), `:153` ("Layer 1 (platform admin): add-on enablement per org"). Code: `services/tax/src/index.ts` = 2 LOC. Zero `fiscal.*` payload schemas in `packages/domain/src/registry.ts` (the six `fiscal.*` types are in the `01 §4` catalog at `specs/01-kernel-sync.md:188` and have no schema). The escpos `FBR_INVOICE` block exists as a renderer block only (`packages/escpos/src/__acceptance__/render.test.ts:578`, `packages/escpos/src/receipt-document.ts:41`) with nothing to fill it.
- **`suspended` is a value no shipping code can write and no shipping code enforces. The only writer of kernel.orgs is an INSERT that hardcodes "active"; there is no UPDATE of kernel.orgs anywhere in the repo and no suspend command among the gateway's declared scripts.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* Sole writer: `services/sync-gateway/src/tenancy.ts:79-88` `insertOrg` (`insert into kernel.orgs … on conflict do nothing`). Status hardcoded: `services/sync-gateway/src/create-org.ts:146-147` ("`15-F25`: provisioning lands in `active`. Not a parameter") and `:133-135` explicitly refuses a `--status` flag. Grep for `update(orgs`/`orgs).set`/`update … kernel.orgs` across services returns zero. Declared scripts in `services/sync-gateway/package.json`: dev, start, migrate, create-org, create-branch, create-owner, list-tenancy, provision-device, revoke-device, test, build — no suspend. Enforcement explicitly disclaimed: `services/api/src/tenancy.ts:35-38` ("**It gates nothing here**: no FR anywhere makes a lifecycle state refuse a request"); asserted as such by `services/sync-gateway/src/__acceptance__/tenancy-read-http.test.ts:148-152`, which has to write the suspended row with raw SQL (`:97`) because no code path can produce one.
- **Vendor collection — how a Lahore restaurant actually pays RestOS — was never designed, anywhere. All 15 RAAST mentions in the corpus are customer→restaurant, and under 06-F15 the own-channel money lands in the RESTAURANT's own bank account, never in a vendor-controlled flow. So the 5% take-rate is an invoice a restaurant can simply decline to pay, not a deduction.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* 15 RAAST hits total (`grep -ain raast specs/*.md restaurant-os.md`): `specs/06-storefront.md` ×8, `specs/02-pos-app.md` ×3, `restaurant-os.md` ×2, `specs/09-rider-dispatch.md` ×1, `specs/01-kernel-sync.md` ×1 — every one customer-side. Decisive: `specs/06-storefront.md:49` (06-F15) "storefront shows **the branch's** configured account details, customer submits their transfer reference … the storefront never asserts payment success it cannot verify"; `:144` puts "RAAST/bank account details" at layer 2 (the org's). Grep for `restaurant pays|pays us|pay RestOS|collect from the restaurant|vendor invoice` across all specs + restaurant-os.md returns zero. `specs/15-platform-admin.md:179` (§9.4) names the hole and assigns no owner: "The export contract for meter-derived invoicing (handoff format to whatever billing process exists) — out of scope here, but it needs an owner at build time."
- **Non-payment automation is not merely unbuilt, it is spec-forbidden. specs/15 §9.7 states billing is deliberately unspecified and that no FR should make a payment state gate a service; and 00 §5.1 + 15-F7 guarantee in-branch POS never stops, so the only lever a SaaS would ever have is the cloud plane.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* `specs/15-platform-admin.md:182` — "**Related and deliberately NOT specified anywhere in this corpus: billing, subscriptions, plans, quotas and metering-to-invoice.** 15-F5a fixes plan *shape* and 15-F24 states plainly that metering is measurement only; **no FR anywhere makes a payment state gate a service, and none should be written** until §9.4's invoicing handoff has an owner." `specs/15-platform-admin.md:34` (15-F7) and `:101` (15-F25) — "**in-branch POS billing never stops** (00 §5.1 is not a business lever)". `specs/15-platform-admin.md:90` (15-F24) — "Metering is measurement only — invoicing and collections are outside this module."
- **15-F25's refusal of a `closed` tenant state is internally well-argued but was reasoned under a no-billing assumption, and the argument does not survive SaaS. Its own test — "two states differing in nothing an implementation can observe are one state" — is satisfied today only BECAUSE `suspended` observably does nothing.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* `specs/15-platform-admin.md:99-107` (15-F25) and the mirrored rationale at `packages/domain/src/tenancy.ts:173-181`. The refusal rests on closure being "indistinguishable … from a suspension that is never lifted plus the revocation of its devices" — but step 1 of the composed procedure (`22-F16` owner export) has zero code: grep for `22-F16|22-F17|export_generated|governance\.` across apps/services/packages returns nothing, and `governance.*` has no payload schema in `packages/domain/src/registry.ts`. So of the three composed acts, only device revocation ships (`services/sync-gateway/src/revoke-device.ts` + `14-F13` back-office list); export and suspension do not. Under routine non-payment the distinction that matters is not dashboard wording but *whether accrual stops, whether the data-retention clock starts, and whether reactivation requires settling arrears* — three things a lifecycle value is exactly the right place to carry, and none of which existed to be observed when the FR was written.
- **The last surviving single-tenant login path is the three BOOTSTRAP_* environment variables — one owner, one org, assembled at boot. It is now a fallback rather than the only path, but it is still shipping and it is the shape a SaaS signup must replace.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* `services/api/src/server.ts:180-203` reads `BOOTSTRAP_OWNER_EMAIL` / `BOOTSTRAP_OWNER_PASSWORD_HASH` / `BOOTSTRAP_ORG_ID` / `BOOTSTRAP_OWNER_NAME` into a process-local store; `:296` — "PROCESS (15-F26 calls this a stopgap). Set DATABASE_URL instead to read kernel.users." Named as a stopgap in the spec itself at `specs/15-platform-admin.md:113` (15-F26). Runbook confirms one org per deployment in practice: `plans/wave-1/running-the-stack.md:33-41`.
- **Self-service signup — the entry point of any SaaS funnel — is refused at spec level as a decision, not deferred as a gap. Nothing about trial, plan selection at signup, or a first-run wizard exists in specs or code.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* `specs/15-platform-admin.md:120` (15-F26) — "**There is no self-service signup path, and that is a decision.**"; `:182` (§9.7) makes it an open business question. Code: `trial` = 0 hits in the comment-stripped sweep of 291 shipping files. Provisioning is 4 shell commands on the service host (`plans/wave-1/running-the-stack.md:142-164`, §2b), each requiring database credentials.
- **ORG_STATUSES is exported through the domain barrel but has no consumer outside its own declaring file — the shape this repo's own seams:check Rule A is built to catch ("a barrel re-export is not a use").** [MINOR]
  - *evidence:* `packages/domain/src/tenancy.ts:182` declares it; `:203` uses it in `z.enum` inside the same file; `packages/domain/src/index.ts:140` re-exports it. Symbol grep across apps/services/packages returns no other consumer.
- **The ledger is genuinely metering-grade — this is the one part of the commercial plane that is nearly free. Channel, order value and settled money are all first-class, closed-set, integer-paisa, snapshotted and org-indexed.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* `packages/domain/src/registry.ts:205-212` — `order.created` requires `channel: z.enum(ORDER_CHANNELS)`, a CLOSED set including `storefront` and `whatsapp` (the exact own-channel take-rate base), and the comment states "Required, and deliberately so". `:285-291` — `order.line_added` carries integer `qty` and `unit_price_paisa` "snapshotted at line-add, never re-derived (01-F18)". `:497-531` — `payment.recorded` carries `amount_paisa`, a closed `method` enum, `shift_id`, and `purpose: "settles_order" | "repays_receivable"` (the DEC-MONEY-007 discriminator that prevents khata repayments double-counting revenue). Query substrate: `services/sync-gateway/src/schema.ts:38-58` — `kernel.events` keyed `(org_id, global_seq)` with an `(org_id, branch_id, global_seq)` index. Rate arithmetic already exists and is protected: `packages/domain/src/money.ts:110` `applyRateBps(amount, bps)` — a 0–5% take-rate is one call.
- **The base subscription unit (PKR 8,000 per branch per month) is countable today with no new schema — kernel.branches exists, is written by a declared command, and is read back by another.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* Migration `services/sync-gateway/drizzle/0010_tenancy_records.sql` creates `kernel.orgs` and `kernel.branches`; `packages/domain/src/tenancy.ts:245-265` defines `BranchRecord` with `branch_id`, `org_id`, `display_name`, `branch_type`, `branch_class`, `created_at`; writers/readers at `services/sync-gateway/src/create-branch.ts` and `list-tenancy.ts` (declared scripts in `services/sync-gateway/package.json`). Note `branch_class` distinguishes `production` from `training` (`packages/domain/src/tenancy.ts:222`), which a per-branch invoice must exclude.
- **A scheduled per-org job host exists and runs — the substrate a monthly metering rollup and an invoice-generation pass would need is already stood up on BullMQ repeatables per 18 §5.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* `services/jobs/src/index.ts` = 283 LOC, header documents BullMQ repeatables per-org, spawned by a declared `start` script and asserted by `services/jobs/src/__acceptance__/auditor-host.test.ts`. Chosen explicitly over `setInterval` because `18 §5` mandates BullMQ repeatables; `bullmq`/`ioredis` already on the `18 §14` allowlist.

### Keep

- **The event ledger as a billing substrate — required closed `channel` on `order.created`, snapshotted `unit_price_paisa`+`qty` on `order.line_added`, `amount_paisa`+closed `method`+`purpose` discriminator on `payment.recorded`, all integer paisa, all `(org_id, global_seq)`-indexed and append-only.**
  - *why:* This is the single hardest thing to retrofit into a billing system and it is already correct, tested and law-bound. A take-rate meter and an order-count meter are folds over data that already exists — no schema migration, no device change, no new event on the hot path. `purpose: settles_order|repays_receivable` in particular already prevents the khata double-count that would inflate every own-channel revenue figure.
  - *size:* packages/domain/src/registry.ts:205-212, :285-291, :497-531 (34 payload schemas total); services/sync-gateway/src/schema.ts:38-58
- **`packages/domain` money primitives — integer paisa branded types, BigInt fold accumulation, `applyRateBps`, `splitPaisa`, and the GritQL ban on raw money arithmetic.**
  - *why:* A 0–5% take-rate on order value is literally `applyRateBps(order_value_paisa, rate_bps)`; the function is written, mutation-tested and on a protected path. Every billing rounding bug this would otherwise ship is already designed out.
  - *size:* packages/domain/src/money.ts:105-118; packages/config/biome-plugins/no-raw-money-arithmetic.grit
- **The tenancy registry — kernel.orgs / kernel.branches / kernel.users tables plus four declared provisioning commands (create-org, create-branch, create-owner, list-tenancy) and provision-device/revoke-device.**
  - *why:* Gives a billing engine its subjects for free: `select count(*) from kernel.branches where org_id = ?` is the PKR 8,000/branch/month line item today, and `branch_class = 'training'` is already there to exclude non-billable branches. The ruling that no ledger table may carry a foreign key to the org registry is also right and must survive.
  - *size:* drizzle/0010_tenancy_records.sql, 0011_tenancy_users.sql; services/sync-gateway/src/tenancy.ts (≈300 LOC); packages/domain/src/tenancy.ts (377 LOC); 6 declared CLI scripts
- **`services/jobs` — a running per-org BullMQ repeatable host.**
  - *why:* Monthly meter rollup, invoice generation, and dunning retries all need exactly this and it already boots, sweeps per org, and has an acceptance suite that spawns the declared start script. Most SaaS rewrites at this stage do not have a scheduler.
  - *size:* services/jobs/src/index.ts, 283 LOC + __acceptance__/auditor-host.test.ts
- **The org-scoped event store (`kernel.org_events`) and its 01-F62 shape — no branch, no device, no lamport/global seq, cloud-stamped ordering authority.**
  - *why:* This is the correct home for plan changes, take-rate changes, add-on enablement and suspension transitions. The hard architectural question (a cloud-plane event has no legal branch envelope) is already answered and the table already exists; only the `config.changed` payload schema and an authenticated actor are missing.
  - *size:* services/sync-gateway/src/org-events.ts (≈120 LOC); drizzle/0009_org_events.sql

### Redo

- **`metering.usage_recorded` — give it a payload schema, and change WHERE it comes from.**
  - *why:* 06-F22 puts the producer inside the storefront service, which is a 2-line stub, so the metering event is blocked behind an entire unbuilt Wave-2 app. Metering the vendor's own revenue must not depend on the customer-facing channel shipping first. It also has no schema, so today it is unemittable under 01-F4.
  - *shape:* Add the `06-F22` payload to `packages/domain/src/registry.ts`. Then derive the meter in `services/jobs` as a per-org fold over `kernel.events` (`order.created.channel` ∈ {storefront, whatsapp} joined to settled `payment.recorded.amount_paisa` where `purpose = 'settles_order'`, minus `payment.refunded`), writing a `metering_rollup` row keyed `(org_id, billing_month)`. Keep 06-F22's per-order idempotency key so that when the storefront does ship, its emitted events supersede the derived ones without double-counting. Note the fold reads no ordering metadata, so law 1 is not at risk.
- **Layer-1 configuration — a real per-org store for plan, take-rate bps, add-on enablement and feature flags.**
  - *why:* `00 §7` layer 1 has no implementation at all, and the current stand-in (`ENABLED_BRANCHES`/`ENABLED_CHANNELS` as process env) is actively single-tenant: `services/api/src/catalog-router.ts:123` admits it serves "the DEPLOYMENT's set rather than a per-org lookup". Onboarding tenant #2 today means editing an env file and restarting the API for tenant #1.
  - *shape:* A `kernel.org_config` table (or `platform.org_plan`) holding `15-F5a`'s `{ base_subscription, take_rate_bps (0..500), add_ons[] }` plus flag rows, written only by the platform-admin plane, read through one authority (the same discipline that closed the `catalog.enabled` double-declaration). Every write emits `config.changed` into `kernel.org_events` with a real `actor_user_id`. `ENABLED_CHANNELS` becomes a per-org flag row and the env var is deleted.
- **`config.changed` — a payload schema and a validated writer.**
  - *why:* It is on the gateway's org-scoped allowlist but `services/sync-gateway/src/org-events.ts:79-80` states the body is not validated, and `packages/domain` has no schema. A plan or take-rate change would land as unvalidated JSON in an append-only store where it can never be corrected in place.
  - *shape:* A discriminated payload in `packages/domain/src/registry.ts` — `{ key, before, after, effective_from }` with a closed `key` set covering plan, take_rate_bps, add-on and lifecycle — and `appendOrgEvent` validating against it. `15-F6`'s "changes take effect from a stated date, never retroactively" means `effective_from` is required, not optional; a meter that reads the rate at fold time without it will silently re-price history.
- **An entitlement dimension in authorization, separate from the role matrix.**
  - *why:* `can()` is 25 actions × 4 roles and answers "is this person allowed". Nothing answers "has this tenant bought this". Bolting entitlement into `PERMISSION_ACTIONS` would corrupt Appendix A's transcription, which is one of the cleanest artifacts in the repo.
  - *shape:* A second, orthogonal gate — `entitled(org_id, capability)` — composed with `can()` at the same choke points that already exist and are already asserted (`services/api/src/trpc.ts` `authorized(...)`, `assertEveryProcedureIsGated` at boot, `apps/pos-electron/src/main/authorize.ts`). Those choke points are the real asset: the wiring is done, only the second predicate is missing. Capabilities, not features: `tax.fiscalize`, `channel.storefront`, `channel.whatsapp`.
- **Suspension — a writer, and enforcement that means something.**
  - *why:* `suspended` exists as a string nothing can write (only INSERT of `active` at `services/sync-gateway/src/create-org.ts:147`; no UPDATE of `kernel.orgs` anywhere) and nothing enforces (`services/api/src/tenancy.ts:35-38`). Under a SaaS with routine non-payment this is the primary automated lever and it is inert.
  - *shape:* An `updateOrgStatus` writer + a platform-admin action (not a shell command — a shell has no `actor_user_id`, which is exactly why the existing provisioning commands emit no events, per `15-F27`). Enforcement at the cloud plane only, honouring `15-F7`/`00 §5.1`: cloud-originated orders blocked, storefront down with an honest notice, sync still accepted, in-branch POS untouched. The FR is correct that the till must keep selling; that is not the same as the constraint being un-automatable.
- **15-F25's two-state lifecycle, revisited under billing.**
  - *why:* The refusal of `closed` was ruled when no payment state existed to observe, so "two states differing in nothing an implementation can observe are one state" was trivially satisfiable. With accrual, arrears, retention clocks and reactivation-on-settlement, `suspended-for-non-payment` and `closed` differ in things code can observe.
  - *shape:* Do not just add `closed`. Decide what each state means for (a) whether metering keeps accruing, (b) whether the 22-F13 retention clock starts, (c) what reactivation requires. If those answers differ, the states differ and the FR's own test authorises the amendment. Note that closure's first composed step — 22-F16 owner export — has zero code today, so the composed procedure the FR relies on is not actually available.
- **Vendor collection — design it, from zero.**
  - *why:* Nothing in the corpus addresses how a restaurant pays RestOS. All 15 RAAST references are customer→restaurant, and 06-F15 routes own-channel money into the restaurant's own account, so RestOS is not in the flow and cannot deduct. This is the largest unanalysed commercial risk, not a missing module.
  - *shape:* A founder-level design before any code: invoice-in-arrears versus prepay; bank transfer with reference matching (the same manual-verification pattern 06-F15 already accepts for customers, which at least means the reconciliation UX has precedent) versus a PSP; and what happens at 30/60/90 days given that 00 §5.1 forbids stopping the till. The take-rate specifically is an invoice a restaurant can decline to pay — model the collection risk before pricing on it.

### Delete

- **`ENABLED_BRANCHES` / `ENABLED_CHANNELS` as process-wide environment variables.**
  - *why:* They are the deployment-per-restaurant assumption made concrete at the exact seam where a per-tenant entitlement belongs, and the code documents this itself (`services/api/src/catalog-router.ts:123`: "one process serves one set"). Every new tenant currently requires editing an env file and restarting the API for all existing tenants. They must become per-org flag rows, not be carried forward. Note the deletion must MOVE the check, not drop it — the same trap that bit `02-F42`'s closed-channel check when `lib/env.ts` was deleted.
- **The `BOOTSTRAP_OWNER_EMAIL` / `BOOTSTRAP_OWNER_PASSWORD_HASH` / `BOOTSTRAP_ORG_ID` login path.**
  - *why:* One owner, one org, from environment variables, assembled into a process-local store (`services/api/src/server.ts:180-203`). Already named a stopgap by 15-F26 and superseded by `kernel.users`. Under a SaaS with real signup it is a permanent cross-tenant credential hazard on a shared process, and it is the last structural artifact of the one-restaurant install.
- **15-F26's "there is no self-service signup path, and that is a decision", and 15 §9.7's instruction that no FR making a payment state gate a service should be written.**
  - *why:* Both are coherent under done-for-you onboarding of a handful of pilots and are load-bearing blockers under SaaS. They are not stale prose — they are active spec-level prohibitions that will be cited by the next session as authority to refuse building signup and entitlement gating. Under commandment 9 (spec change before behavior change) they must be amended in `specs/15` before any commercial code is written, or the work is unbuildable by this repo's own rules.
- **Nothing else. There is no commercial code to delete.**
  - *why:* Measured: zero hits across 291 shipping non-test files for the entire commercial vocabulary. This dimension has no sunk cost, no wrong abstraction to unwind and no migration to write. That is the one mercy in the finding — the rewrite is pure greenfield on top of a ledger that is already the right shape.

### Open questions

- Who touches the money on an own-channel order? 06-F15 sends the customer's RAAST transfer to the RESTAURANT's own account, so RestOS cannot deduct a take-rate — it can only invoice one in arrears. Is the 5% collectible at all under that flow, or does the storefront need to route payment through a vendor-controlled PSP account (a fundamentally different product, different regulatory posture, and a rewrite of 06-F15)?
- How does a Lahore restaurant actually pay the PKR 8,000/branch/month? Bank transfer with manual reference matching, a PSP with cards, cash collected by the onboarding rep, or direct-debit mandate? Nothing in the corpus answers this and the collection mechanism determines whether dunning can be automated at all.
- Does non-payment gate anything? 00 §5.1 and 15-F7 guarantee in-branch POS billing never stops, and specs/15 §9.7 forbids writing an FR that makes a payment state gate a service. If the only lever is cloud services (storefront down, sync accepted, cloud orders blocked), is that enough commercial leverage — and if not, which of those two laws yields?
- Does metering keep accruing while an org is suspended? Nobody has ruled, and it changes the reactivation conversation (settle arrears vs. forgive the suspended period) and therefore the schema.
- Should 15-F25 gain a third lifecycle state now that a payment state will exist to observe? The FR's refusal rests on its own test — two states differing in nothing observable are one state — which billing may now falsify. Founder call, and it is an FR amendment either way.
- Self-service signup (specs/15 §9.7, 15-F26): yes or no? Everything downstream depends on it — trial semantics, plan selection at signup, first-run wizard, and whether the 4 shell provisioning commands are a stopgap or the permanent onboarding path.
- Trial: is there one? No spec, no FR, no code, zero hits. Length, what it includes, what happens at expiry, and whether an expired trial suspends (which requires the enforcement that 15 §9.7 currently forbids).
- Who owns specs/15 §9.4 — the export contract for meter-derived invoicing? The spec has flagged it as needing an owner at build time since Draft 1 and it still has none. Nothing downstream can be sequenced until it does.
- Is the take-rate billed on gross order value or net of refunds/rejections? 06-F22 specifies a linked reversal metering event, so the intent is net — but the fold must be built that way from day one, because an append-only ledger makes a wrong first answer permanent.
- Does the tax add-on's price and enablement follow the org or the branch? 16-F19/16-F25 make certification branch-status-dependent (SRB's dispensation list), so an org-level add-on flag may be the wrong grain for both entitlement and billing.

### Adversarial verdicts

- **CONFIRMED** — Zero commercial code in the shipping tree — comment-stripped scan of 291 non-test files returns 0 for subscription/billing/invoic/dunning/entitlement/take_rate/plan_id/feature_flag/trial/add_on/meterin/usage_recorded; only hit is ORG_STATUSES' "suspended".
  - Independently reproduced with my own block-and-line-comment stripper over the same file set: 291 files, every listed token 0, `suspend` = 1 at packages/domain/src/tenancy.ts (ORG_STATUSES, raw line 182). I added `quota` (0) and `tenant` (1, a string literal in services/api/src/tenancy.ts). A raw `grep -a` for the same tokens returns only comment and test hits (services/sync-gateway/src/schema.ts:354, packages/domain/src/tenancy.ts:196-197, services/api/src/tenancy.ts:37, escpos test files). No under-reporting: I searched the property (any commercial vocabulary anywhere), not one guessed symbol.
- **CONFIRMED** — metering.usage_recorded has no payload schema in packages/domain, so under 01-F4 it is unemittable, not merely unbuilt.
  - packages/domain/src/registry.ts `payloadSchemas` opens at :204 and its keys are exactly the 34 the finding names (order.created :205 … customer.address_added :888), plus 7 audit.* at :1020-1034; no metering.* key. `metering` appears in the tree only in comments (registry.ts:234, tenancy.ts:196, api/tenancy.ts:37, schema.ts:354) — `usage_recorded` = 0 hits stripped and 0 raw outside tests. Spec side verified verbatim: 06-F22 at specs/06-storefront.md:66, 07-F5 at specs/07-whatsapp-channel.md:39, 07-F19 at :63, catalog entry at specs/01-kernel-sync.md:188. 01-F4 (specs/01-kernel-sync.md:23) makes an unknown type a build-time and runtime error, and UnknownEventTypeError is real shipping code (registry.ts:8, thrown at the append path, mapped in sync-client/cloud-session.ts:93). Unemittable is the correct word.
- **CONFIRMED** — config.changed is allowlisted by type at the gateway but has no payload schema and its body is explicitly not validated — a take-rate change is unemittable or unstructured JSON.
  - services/sync-gateway/src/org-events.ts:32-38 is ORG_SCOPED_EVENT_TYPES with "config.changed" at :37; :79 is verbatim 'The payload body is **not** validated here … `packages/domain` exports no per-type payload schema'. Absent from registry.ts payloadSchemas (checked the full key list). catalog-pricing.test.ts:90 quote confirmed. One thing worth adding rather than correcting: no shipping code emits a config.changed at all — services/sync-gateway/src/create-org.ts:39-45 states outright 'IT EMITS NO EVENT, and 15-F4 says it should', deferring the ledger half to 15-F27's console. So the type is reachable but has neither a producer nor a shape.
- **AMENDED** — The host that owns the entire commercial plane does not exist — apps/platform-admin is a 2-line stub, services/tax is 2 lines.
  - Stubs confirmed by wc -l: apps/platform-admin/src/index.ts and services/tax/src/index.ts are both 2 lines ('// Scaffold stub … export {};'); specs/15 is 182 lines. But 'the ENTIRE commercial plane' overstates it: a real slice of doc 15 ships as gateway CLI — 15-F27's provisioning and tenancy directory (create-org, create-branch, create-owner, list-tenancy in services/sync-gateway/package.json scripts, with services/sync-gateway/src/create-org.ts and tenancy.ts behind them), plus 01-F68/F69 records. What has no implementation anywhere is the COMMERCIAL half specifically: plan control (15-F5a), take-rate (15-F6), feature/tier flags (15-F5), metering (15-F24). Also LOC comparators drift slightly: backoffice/src is 10,389 not 11,306; sync-gateway 22,901; api 12,239. Substance stands, wording narrowed.
- **AMENDED** — The take-rate has no revenue base built — storefront and whatsapp are 2-line stubs, so the only channels a shipping till can ring are counter/phone/foodpanda, and foodpanda is not own-channel.
  - The channel enumeration is WRONG and it matters. apps/pos-electron/src/renderer/Counter.tsx:326-342 ships ORDER_CHANNELS_AT_COUNTER with FOUR ids — counter ('In restaurant'), phone ('Call'), foodpanda, whatsapp — ruled in by the founder in August 2026 (:278-300 records `whatsapp` moving 'from declined to ruled-in'; only `storefront` stays out), the append is `channel: pendingChannel` at :1042, and dev prices exist for whatsapp (main/catalog.ts:133). restaurant-os.md:109 sets the take-rate on 'storefront/WhatsApp/QR order value', so a counter-rung WhatsApp order sits inside the revenue base and IS ringable today. (AGENTS.md's own 'Counter.tsx:119 hardcodes COUNTER_CHANNEL' is itself stale — do not re-derive from it.) What survives: apps/storefront and services/whatsapp are 2 LOC, OrdersSurface.tsx:46 has a cloud inbox for storefront/whatsapp with no producer, no metering event exists to price any of it, and 06-F22's rate_bps source (platform-admin metering config) does not exist. So the correct claim is 'no METERED revenue base and no automated own-channel producers', not 'no own-channel order a till can ring'.
- **CONFIRMED** — No entitlement gate of any kind — 25 actions × 4 roles, no plan, add-on or payment-state dimension.
  - packages/domain/src/permissions.ts:109-152 — I counted PERMISSION_ACTIONS by hand off the source: 25 entries (order.create … report.sales_view, including device.manage, availability.toggle, customer.record). ROLES at :45 is the 4 named. Not one action or cell names a plan, tier, add-on, quota or payment state; `quota`/`entitlement`/`plan_id` are 0 hits in the comment-stripped sweep. The only non-role dimensions in the matrix are canPayOut's threshold and reportScope's branch scoping — neither is commercial. AGENTS.md's 22/23 counts are indeed stale at 25.
- **CONFIRMED** — The closest thing to a per-tenant feature flag is a process-wide env var, and the code says so out loud.
  - services/api/src/catalog-router.ts:122-124 is verbatim 'The answer is the DEPLOYMENT's set rather than a per-org lookup … 00 §7's layer-2 config plane does not exist, so one process serves one set' (finding said :123-125 — one line of drift, same sentence). Wiring confirmed: server.ts:401-402 declares ENABLED_BRANCHES/ENABLED_CHANNELS in the boot env, :451 passes `enabled: { branches: env.ENABLED_BRANCHES, channels: env.ENABLED_CHANNELS }` into the context the gated `enabled` query returns. Since it is process env, a second tenant's branch does require an env edit and a restart of the shared API.
- **AMENDED** — 00 §7 layer 1 has no implementation; packages/device-config implements only layers 2 and 3, all read from a device's process environment.
  - The substance holds: specs/00-platform-overview.md:167 defines layer 1 (provisioning, feature flags/tier enablement, own-channel take-rate %, rollout channels), and a case-insensitive grep for 'layer 1' across apps/, services/ and packages/ .ts source returns ZERO hits, while device-config's modules are each annotated layer 2 or 3 (panel-density.ts:2 layer 3, serve-signal.ts:67 layer 2, aging.ts:75 layer 2) and each resolves an RESTOS_* env string. Two evidence corrections: packages/config/src/index.ts is 23 LOC, not 51 (51 is the whole src dir, index.ts + dsn.ts), and it exports `redactedDsn` as well as `defineEnv`. Also note device-identity.ts resolves org/branch/device ids — arguably a layer-1 provisioning value — and it too is read from env, which strengthens rather than weakens the finding.
- **CONFIRMED** — The tax add-on's 'off by default' is not a gate — it is off because services/tax is empty; a paying and a non-paying add-on customer are byte-identical.
  - services/tax/src/index.ts is the 2-line scaffold stub. No fiscal.* key exists in registry.ts payloadSchemas (full key list checked), so all six catalog types at specs/01-kernel-sync.md:188 are unemittable. Spec citations verified verbatim: 16-F1 at specs/16-tax-module.md:28, 16-F25 at :77, layer-1 add-on enablement at :153. I searched for the PROPERTY rather than the word 'tax': packages/escpos genuinely ships fiscal RENDERING (render.ts:105-112 `fiscal?: FiscalBlock`, encoder.ts:38/142/337 `fiscal_qr`, receipt-document.ts:39-42 explaining FISCAL_LOCKED blocks are injected at render), and the producer side is empty — the only `fiscal` mention in all of apps/pos-electron/src is a comment at job-store.ts:22. Nothing anywhere passes a FiscalBlock. Renderer-only with nothing to fill it is exactly right.
- **CONFIRMED** — `suspended` is a value no shipping code can write and none enforces — sole writer hardcodes 'active', no UPDATE of kernel.orgs, no suspend command.
  - services/sync-gateway/src/tenancy.ts:79-88 insertOrg is the only statement touching kernel.orgs other than two selects (:95, :110) — I grepped the whole services tree for kernel.orgs/update(orgs/orgs).set and found no UPDATE. create-org.ts:133-135 refuses a --status flag by name, :146 hardcodes status: 'active'. Declared scripts read straight from package.json: dev, start, migrate, create-org, create-branch, create-owner, list-tenancy, provision-device, revoke-device, test, build — no suspend. Enforcement disclaimed verbatim at services/api/src/tenancy.ts:35-38 ('It gates nothing here: no FR anywhere makes a lifecycle state refuse a request'), and tenancy-read-http.test.ts:97 does insert the suspended row with raw SQL because nothing else can produce one.
- **CONFIRMED** — Vendor collection was never designed — all 15 RAAST mentions are customer→restaurant, own-channel money lands in the restaurant's own account, so the take-rate is an invoice a restaurant can decline.
  - `grep -ain raast specs/*.md restaurant-os.md` gives exactly 15 hits with the claimed distribution (06 ×8, 02 ×3, restaurant-os ×2, 09 ×1, 01 ×1). 06-F15 at specs/06-storefront.md:49 is verbatim as quoted — the branch's configured account, customer submits a transfer reference, storefront never asserts payment it cannot verify — and :144 puts RAAST/bank account details at layer 2 (the org's). specs/15-platform-admin.md:179 (§9.4) is verbatim and assigns no owner. I also checked the other direction for a vendor-side money path (15-F6/15-F24 at :33/:95): both are metering-only, and 15-F24 says invoicing and collections are outside the module. No deduction mechanism exists anywhere.
- **CONFIRMED** — Non-payment automation is spec-forbidden: §9.7 says billing is deliberately unspecified and no FR should make a payment state gate a service; 00 §5.1 + 15-F7 guarantee in-branch POS never stops.
  - specs/15-platform-admin.md:182 (§9.7) is verbatim, including 'no FR anywhere makes a payment state gate a service, and none should be written until §9.4's invoicing handoff has an owner'. 'in-branch POS billing never stops (00 §5.1 is not a business lever)' appears at both :34 (15-F7) and :101 (15-F25), as cited. One citation drift: 15-F24's 'Metering is measurement only' is at :95, not :90 — same sentence, verbatim. The forbidding is real and mirrored in code comments (services/api/src/tenancy.ts:37, packages/domain/src/tenancy.ts:196-197).
- **CONFIRMED** — 15-F25's refusal of a `closed` state was reasoned under a no-billing assumption; its observability test is satisfied only because `suspended` observably does nothing.
  - Evidence checks out. 15-F25 at specs/15-platform-admin.md:99-107 with the composed-closure procedure and the 'two states differing in nothing an implementation can observe are one state' precedent; mirrored at packages/domain/src/tenancy.ts:173-182. Of the three composed acts, only device revocation ships: `grep -ain '22-F16|22-F17|export_generated|governance\.'` across apps/services/packages returns ZERO, `governance.*` has no payload schema in registry.ts, and suspension is unwritable per finding 10 (which I confirmed independently). Flagging one thing about its nature rather than its accuracy: this is an argument about a spec decision, not a defect in shipped code, and it depends on the SaaS premise the corpus deliberately declines (§9.7). Severity 'major' is the ceiling; do not let it be read as a code blocker.
- **AMENDED** — The last single-tenant login path is the three BOOTSTRAP_* env vars — now a fallback but still shipping.
  - The path is real and shipping: services/api/src/server.ts:180-207 assembles one owner from BOOTSTRAP_OWNER_EMAIL / _PASSWORD_HASH / BOOTSTRAP_ORG_ID / _NAME into a process-local store, and :294-296 reports it as 'BOOTSTRAP_OWNER_* development seed — ONE owner, IN MEMORY, and it DIES WITH THIS PROCESS (15-F26 calls this a stopgap)'. Two corrections. (1) It is NOT a fallback: server.ts:222 states 'DATABASE_URL and BOOTSTRAP_OWNER_* are MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE, and both set is a boot CRASH', and with neither set the report is 'NONE — nobody can log in', which is fail-closed. It is a mutually exclusive alternative mode, which is a slightly better posture than 'fallback' implies. (2) The spec citation is :108 (15-F26's opening bullet, which names the stopgap), not :113 (a blank line). Runbook single-org coupling at running-the-stack.md:32-41 confirmed verbatim.
- **CONFIRMED** — Self-service signup is refused at spec level as a decision, not deferred; no trial, plan selection or first-run wizard in specs or code.
  - specs/15-platform-admin.md:112 is verbatim 'There is no self-service signup path, and that is a decision' (finding cited :120 — line drift only; :108 is the 15-F26 header carrying the same clause), and :182 (§9.7) makes it an open business question naming 'a public sign-up, a trial org, a first-run wizard'. `trial` = 0 hits in my own comment-stripped sweep of the 291 shipping files; I also checked for the property under other names (signup/sign-up/onboarding wizard) and found no surface. Provisioning is the four shell commands at plans/wave-1/running-the-stack.md:142-166 (§2b), each requiring DATABASE_URL, as claimed.

## sync-kernel

**Verdict.** This is the best-engineered thing in the repo and it is not a science project: 17,163 lines of production code carrying 53,520 lines of tests, 1,721 tests all green as re-measured today (sync-client 743/66 files, gateway 358/53 on real Postgres via Testcontainers, domain 543/28, sync-protocol 77/8), a genuine build-vs-buy record (specs/19) that names verified vendor evidence rather than preference, and a convergence oracle that would embarrass most commercial sync engines (bijective envelope-id relabel including an order-reversing one, plus clock/sequence injection through Proxy-poisoned envelopes — packages/sync-client/src/__acceptance__/merge-invariance.test.ts). The founder's "deviated" framing is WRONG about this dimension in the specific way that matters: the kernel is multi-tenant to the bone and always has been — every envelope carries org_id/branch_id (packages/domain/src/envelope.ts:33-47), every gateway query is `where org_id = ${session.orgId}` with orgId taken from a verified token claim and never from the client (services/sync-gateway/src/gateway.ts:194,207,1131,449), and kernel.orgs/branches/users plus a create-org/create-branch/create-owner/list-tenancy command set already exist (services/sync-gateway/src/tenancy.ts). What is missing for SaaS is self-serve signup and billing, not tenancy. Three corrections to the corpus, all measured: (1) AGENTS.md's headline claim that the LAN mesh is "BUILT and hosted by nothing" is FALSE — the @unreached-owed marker is gone from mesh-session.ts, apps/pos-electron/src/main/mesh.ts:147 and apps/pass-kds/src/main/mesh.ts:141 both call createMeshSession, both are wired at boot (index.ts:69 / :39), and commits 3311918/45d0153 land it with mutation matrices; seams:check reports 21 owed markers and 240 modules, not AGENTS.md's 34 and 173. (2) The measured O(N^2) of specs/25 is FIXED — assignGlobalSeqTx is now sidecar bookkeeping only (device-store.ts:1128-1132) and I measured append cost FLAT at 6.4→5.8 ms/event from N=1,000 to N=32,000. (3) A NEW and larger cost has replaced it, named nowhere: device-store.ts:1191 calls refoldTx() unconditionally on every store open, which Zod-parses and re-folds the ENTIRE ledger, retentionDrop has ZERO production callers so nothing ever prunes, and I measured cold-open growing linearly to 959 ms at 32k events (~30 us/event, 720 bytes/event on disk) — extrapolating a busy branch's ~3M events/year to a 2 GB tablet at specs/25's own 5-10x factor gives an 8-15 minute till boot and ~2.2 GB of database. That is the live launch blocker, and it exists under every candidate architecture. On the fork itself: event sourcing and offline-first each earn their cost; peer-to-peer merge does not clearly earn its 1,591 production lines plus ~3,133 test lines plus the gateway relay path, because the deployment fact it exists for (only the counter has internet — 01-F13) is satisfied by a branch-local relay with a fixed authority, which is the Toast pattern specs/19 §4.2 itself cites. Election, split-brain tolerance and full peer merge are the expensive delta beyond that, and they buy resilience to a state (the counter is off) in which the restaurant cannot take money anyway.

### Findings

- **Unbounded cold-start refold: every store open re-reads, Zod-parses and re-folds the ENTIRE local event log, and nothing ever prunes it. This is the real operational risk and no document names it — specs/25 is entirely about the OLD quadratic, which is fixed.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* packages/sync-client/src/device-store.ts:1191 `refoldTx();` runs unconditionally at open → :1134 refoldTx → :801 recomputeFolds → :748 readAllParsed (reads ALL own+peer envelopes, parseEvent on each) → engine.rebuild. `retentionDrop` (the only pruning surface, device-store.ts:1316) has ZERO production callers: `grep -arn retentionDrop apps services packages --include='*.ts'` excluding tests returns only its own declaration/transaction and error strings in folds/merge.ts:685,692,1457. Measured by me on this box (AMD Ryzen 5 3600, file-backed SQLite, shipped pragmas, realistic order/line/confirm/payment mix): N=1000→99ms, 2000→105ms, 4000→154ms, 8000→273ms, 16000→508ms, 32000→959ms; ratio per doubling 1.06/1.47/1.77/1.86/1.89 → LINEAR at ~30us/event. DB size 22MB at 32k events = 720 bytes/event. 01-F14 specifies a 'rolling operational window (current business day + configurable N days)' (specs/01-kernel-sync.md:01-F14) and it is unimplemented. Extrapolation: ~1000 orders/day ≈ 8-10k events/day ≈ 3M events/year → ~90 s cold open HERE, ×5-10 for a 2 GB tablet per specs/25:76 → 8-15 minutes, and ~2.2 GB of database on a 2 GB device.
- **There is no LAN peer authentication. Any device on the shop Wi-Fi can join a branch mesh and inject events into the branch ledger.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* apps/pos-electron/src/main/mesh.ts:79 `const LAN_HELLO_PLACEHOLDER = "lan-member-unauthenticated"`, with the file's own comment at :65-77: "this is a wire-schema minimum, not a credential … mesh-session.ts's own hello arm 'inspects no token' … LAN peer authentication is an open gap and is reported, not invented." Same constant in apps/pass-kds/src/main/mesh.ts. Compare the cloud leg, which is fully authenticated (services/sync-gateway/src/auth.ts, claims {org_id, branch_id, device_id, hub_relay, expires_at}, iss/aud bound).
- **A compromised relay-authorized hub can fabricate events attributed to any registered branch peer. Accepted in Wave 0, but it is a different risk when you sell to strangers.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* KERNEL.md:97 — "Residual trust assumption (DEC-SYNC-009, accepted): a compromised relay-authorized hub can fabricate events attributed to any registered branch peer — the gateway's origin gate checks the origin's existence, not authorship; Wave 0 has no per-event origin signatures." Confirmed in code: services/sync-gateway/src/gateway.ts:150 `registryValid(envelope)` checks existence only.
- **AGENTS.md's central claim about this dimension — 'the LAN mesh is BUILT and hosted by nothing' — is stale and false. The mesh has two production hosts and passing acceptance suites.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* `grep -arn 'unreached-owed' packages/sync-client/src` (non-test) returns NO marker on mesh-session.ts; the only marker there is `@unreached-by-design` on REELECTION_BUDGET_MS at mesh-session.ts:39. Production callers: apps/pos-electron/src/main/mesh.ts:4,147 and apps/pass-kds/src/main/mesh.ts:4,141 both import and call createMeshSession; wired at apps/pos-electron/src/main/index.ts:69 and apps/pass-kds/src/main/index.ts:39 (`import { createLanMesh } from "./mesh"`). Acceptance suites: apps/pos-electron/src/main/__acceptance__/lan-mesh-host.test.ts, lan-mesh-wiring.test.ts; apps/pass-kds/src/main/__acceptance__/lan-mesh-join.test.ts, lan-mesh-wiring.test.ts. Landed in 3311918 (17/17 and 19/19 suites) and 45d0153. `pnpm seams:check` today: clean, 240 production modules (136 shipping), 621 value exports, 18 optional seams, 34 @unreached-by-design, 21 @unreached-owed across 14 files — AGENTS.md claims 173/93/432/10 and 34 owed across 19 files.
- **specs/25's measured O(N^2) re-fold is genuinely fixed, structurally rather than by optimisation. Sequence adoption now does zero fold work and the append fast path holds at scale.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* packages/sync-client/src/device-store.ts:1128-1132 — `assignGlobalSeqTx` is `adoptGlobalSeq(eventId, globalSeq)` and nothing else; the `recomputeFolds()` call that specs/25 §2 names as the defect is gone. Asserted at packages/sync-client/src/__acceptance__/merge-workcounter.test.ts:354 ('assignGlobalSeq over an entire ledger folds nothing, rebuilds nothing, and leaves the projection bit-identical', including a fully REVERSED cloud order). Measured by me: append cost per event 6.433ms @N=1000 → 6.546 @2000 → 8.764 @4000 → 6.502 @8000 → 5.763 @16000 → 5.756 @32000 — flat, not quadratic (specs/25 §3 B2 measured 289x degradation at N=1000 under the old engine).
- **Law 1 (per-fold merge semantics, no ordering metadata read by folds) is real, implemented and enforced by an oracle strong enough to kill the specific wrong implementations that pass naive convergence tests.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* packages/sync-client/src/folds/merge.ts:6-9 header states it; the file reads branch_created_at (a delivered field) and never global_seq/lamport_seq/device clock for a projected value. Enforcement: packages/sync-client/src/__acceptance__/merge-invariance.test.ts — bijective envelope-id relabel INCLUDING an order-reversing map (reversingIdMap), sequence and clock injection, applied consistently to supersedes/preds references, with the expected projection as the phi-image. specs/26 §8 records the binding lesson: 'a min-envelope-id tiebreak passes plain convergence — min-id = min-wall-clock via the UUIDv7 prefix. Only bijective-relabel invariance kills it.' merge.ts:1150-1153 carries a comment about exactly that latent hazard at the confirm anchor.
- **Law 2 (branch-consensus time stamped at append) and Law 3 (integer paisa accumulated in BigInt) are both implemented and enforced, not aspirational.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* Law 2: packages/domain/src/envelope.ts:33-47 declares three time fields with branch_created_at and time_basis INSIDE the envelope and an explicit rationale for why a fold applying its own offset would break 01-F34 silently; packages/sync-client/src/device-store.ts:382 `CREATE TABLE branch_time`, :1360 setBranchTimeOffset, :1384 branchTimeStatus; mesh-session.ts:528 sets the offset from the hub ping. Suites: branch-time.test.ts, branch-time-mesh.test.ts, time-invariance.test.ts. Law 3: packages/domain/src/money.ts:36-58 `totalPaisaOrNull` accumulates in BigInt and returns null (never throws, never truncates) past 2^53, with the non-associativity argument written out; packages/sync-client/src/folds/merge.ts has 13 BigInt sites, :1189-1223 accumulate pay/repaid/refund totals in BigInt. Suite: fold-money-guard.test.ts.
- **Multi-tenant isolation at the kernel/gateway layer is already correct and server-derived. This is the part of the SaaS story that does NOT need rewriting.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* services/sync-gateway/src/gateway.ts:449 `orgId: claims.org_id` — the tenant key comes from the verified token, never the client. Every ledger query filters on it: :194-198, :207-211, :1130-1133 (`where org_id = ${session.orgId} and branch_id = ${session.branchId}`), :889 insert carries org_id/branch_id. Fan-out is keyed per (org, branch): :284 `branchKey`. Tenancy directory: services/sync-gateway/src/tenancy.ts (writer for kernel.orgs/branches/users), migrations drizzle/0010_tenancy_records.sql, 0011_tenancy_users.sql. Dedicated suites: __acceptance__/isolation-regression.test.ts, tenancy-provisionable.test.ts, tenancy-read-http.test.ts.
- **Tenant ONBOARDING is a shell command on the service host, not a product surface. This is the actual SaaS gap in this dimension, and it is small.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* services/sync-gateway/package.json scripts: create-org, create-branch, create-owner, list-tenancy, provision-device, revoke-device — all `tsx src/*.ts` CLIs. services/sync-gateway/src/tenancy.ts:27-32 explains why they emit no event: 'a command on a service host has no authenticated user, so OrgEvent.actor_user_id could only ever be null, permanently, in an append-only store (01-F1)'. 01-F25's back-office pairing code is unbuilt, so a new customer's device still needs a human with shell access. No billing, plan or metering code exists against restaurant-os.md §7's 'PKR 8,000 / branch / month'.
- **mDNS discovery — half of 01-F12 — does not exist. The LAN mesh runs on a hand-typed peer list.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* packages/device-config/src/lan-mesh.ts:15-22: 'THE mDNS HALF IS NOT IMPLEMENTED … 18 §14 line 197 lists mDNS on Android as an OPEN registry item rather than an allowlisted dependency'. Config is LAN_MESH_ENV = { RESTOS_LAN_HOST, RESTOS_LAN_PORT, RESTOS_LAN_PEERS } at :35-39, a manual `{device_id, host, port}` directory. `grep -ril mdns` across apps/services/packages returns only comments and this file.
- **The headline performance contract — 00 §5's 'catch-up after 8h offline with ~500 orders < 60 s on 4G' — has never been measured end-to-end, and every number in specs/25 and specs/26 is fold CPU only.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* specs/26-merge-semantics.md:58 — 'The budget is END-TO-END, and every measurement in this document is CPU-only'; :79 — 'Do not claim the target is met until the complete path — compressed 4G transfer, batched SQLite persistence, zero-work sequence adoption, incremental projection — passes on the reference 2-3 GB Android tablet.' KERNEL.md:95 — 'physical wall-clock proof on a real 2 GB tablet (p95, plug-pull — deferred to the hardware rig, D3) … The kernel is verified in logic and simulation; it has never run on target hardware.' Note specs/26 §6.4's second bottleneck IS now closed (compression is wired: transport-ws.ts:285-286, :326; gateway.ts:499; server.ts:92-93 — KERNEL.md:81's 'not yet wired into live transport' is stale).
- **The event-type catalog in packages/domain has grown well past what specs/25 §17 and specs/26 §7 call a 'blocking gap', but three types in the 01 §4 catalog still have no payload schema and therefore cannot be emitted at all.** [MINOR]
  - *evidence:* `grep -aoE '^\s+"[a-z_]+\.[a-z_]+":' packages/domain/src/registry.ts | wc -l` → 41 payload schemas (specs/25:276 says 'Only 8 fold-consumed + 5 audit.* types exist'; specs/26:113 lists table.state_changed, availability.changed, shift.*, cash.*, void/comp/discount.recorded as having 'no payload schema at all' — all but one of those now exist). Still absent from registry.ts while present in the 01 §4 catalog: `table.state_changed`, `config.changed`, `user.changed`. 01-F4 makes emitting an unschema'd type a build-time and runtime error, so those three are unbuildable, not merely unbuilt.
- **Test-to-code ratio and suite quality are exceptional for this layer, and the review discipline is documented rather than claimed.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* Measured under */src, excluding node_modules: sync-client 7,781 prod / 26,220 test (66 files, 743 tests); sync-gateway 5,657 / 17,244 (53 files, 358 tests, real Postgres via Testcontainers); domain 3,183 / 8,720 (28 files, 543 tests); sync-protocol 542 / 1,336 (8 files, 77 tests). Kernel total 17,163 prod / 53,520 test = 3.1:1, 1,721 tests, all four suites REAL_EXIT=0 today. Whole repo is 204,174 lines under */src of which 60,948 non-test, so the kernel is ~28% of production code. KERNEL.md:85 records that 5 of 8 adversarial review passes each found a blocking durable-data-loss defect the green suite could not see. specs/25 §9 is an appendix of the team's own refuted claims — a document that argues against itself is a strong quality signal.
- **Peer-to-peer merge is the smallest and most separable of the three concerns by line count, but it is the reason the merge algebra has to be as elaborate as it is.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* Directly P2P-specific production code: packages/sync-client/src/mesh-session.ts 633, hub-election.ts 27, packages/device-config/src/lan-mesh.ts 191, apps/pos-electron/src/main/mesh.ts 209, apps/pass-kds/src/main/mesh.ts 197, plus the LAN half of transport-ws.ts (createWsLanTransport at :65, createWsCloudTransport at :223) ≈ 158 → ~1,415-1,591 LOC. Test surface: mesh-*/relay-*/hub-election/spike-scenarios/journey-j3 suites + builders = 3,133 lines in sync-client alone, plus 5 dedicated gateway suites (relay-crash-replay, relay-hub-uplink, relay-origin-registry, relay-fix-round, sec-f1-revoked-fanout) and 43 `relay` sites in gateway.ts touching auth.ts, registry.ts, schema.ts, provision-device.ts, errors.ts. The indirect cost is larger: supersedes-DAG head sets with materialized tombstones, contested-terminal line-state MVRs and divergent-attempt dispute rendering (merge.ts, 1,521 lines) exist because two devices can both write while partitioned FROM EACH OTHER, not merely from the cloud.
- **Device→server queueing — the part that survives a SaaS rewrite intact — is already isolated in one file and is only 706 lines.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* packages/sync-client/src/cloud-session.ts: :291 drainPush (outbox batch), :209 sendHello with resume point, :231 sendCatchup, :351 applyEvents → store.ingestPage (transactional page with per-item savepoint isolation, device-store.ts:1108), push_ack write-checkpoint watermark, DivergentDuplicateError passed rather than wedging the pull (specs/26 §6.4's load-bearing warning), :243-289 catalog request/reconcile, :200 persistRenewal for 01-F47 token rotation. The relay-specific parts (:182 relayAcked, :305 relayPushFor, :320 relayDrain, :189 suppressedOrigins) are ~90 lines and are the only P2P residue in this file.

### Keep

- **packages/domain — the shared vocabulary: envelope, event-type catalog + Zod payload schemas, branded integer money with BigInt totals, permission matrix, state machine, tenancy records.**
  - *why:* Plane-agnostic by construction and already the single declaration point both planes parse through (18 §2). Nothing about cloud-first invalidates any of it. The money contract (integer paisa, BigInt accumulation, splitPaisa/applyRateBps, the GritQL ban on raw money arithmetic) is correct independent of sync topology and is the kind of thing teams get wrong for years. The tenancy records (01-F68/F69/F70, 11-F20) are the SaaS directory model already written.
  - *size:* 3,183 prod LOC / 8,720 test LOC, 28 test files, 543 tests green. 41 event payload schemas in registry.ts (1,077 lines), permissions.ts 600, tenancy.ts 377, money.ts 185, business-day.ts 179.
- **packages/sync-protocol — the wire contract (hello/hello_ack, push/push_ack, event_batch, catchup_request/response, catalog_request/response/notice, quarantine_notice, purge_command) plus the zstd framing and its per-connection negotiation.**
  - *why:* This is exactly the device↔server protocol a cloud-first SaaS needs, and it is already versioned, additive-only under v:1, golden-fixture tested, and negotiated per connection so a new gateway can never send frames an old device cannot parse. The LAN leg reuses the same message set, so removing P2P removes zero protocol surface — it removes callers.
  - *size:* 542 prod LOC / 1,336 test LOC, 8 files, 77 tests green. PROTOCOL.md is a genuinely good human-readable spec. compression.ts is 78 lines and is WIRED (transport-ws.ts:285, gateway.ts:499, server.ts:92).
- **packages/sync-client/src/cloud-session.ts — the device→server outbox with a push_ack write-checkpoint, paged resumable catch-up, and a blocked-cursor status that stops-and-reports rather than skipping (DEC-SYNC-011).**
  - *why:* This IS the 'short offline buffer that queues to an authoritative server' the SaaS design needs. It already handles the hard parts: idempotent replay, contiguous-prefix cursor advance, passing a divergent duplicate instead of wedging the pull, crash-resume mid-page. Borrowed from PowerSync's write-checkpoint concept with attribution (specs/19 §5). Strip ~90 lines of relay logic and it is done.
  - *size:* 706 prod LOC. Suites: cloud-session.test.ts, cloud-ack-guard.test.ts, catchup-blocked-status.test.ts, crash-resume.test.ts, transport-batch-e2e.test.ts, transport-batch-page.test.ts.
- **services/sync-gateway's tenant isolation and admission: token issue/verify with org/branch/device claims + iss/aud binding, the unrevoked-registry-row veto, per-org global_seq assignment, org-scoped queries, quarantine pipeline with a durable notice outbox, and the provision-device/revoke-device command pair.**
  - *why:* Multi-tenant isolation is already server-derived and never client-declared, which is the single hardest thing to retrofit. 30 s fail-closed revocation eviction, purge_command on next contact, and a revoked device losing live sessions are all built and tested. A SaaS rewrite keeps this file-for-file and adds a signup surface on top.
  - *size:* gateway.ts 1,337, auth.ts 137, registry.ts 273, tenancy.ts 269, provision-device.ts 323, revoke-device.ts 240, migrate.ts 143, 12 drizzle migrations. 358 tests / 53 files green on real Postgres, including isolation-regression.test.ts, device-auth.test.ts, auth-eviction-latency.test.ts, sec-f1-revoked-fanout.test.ts.
- **The three standing laws as LAWS, and their oracles — especially merge-invariance.test.ts's bijective-relabel + injection harness.**
  - *why:* Law 3 (BigInt money) is correct under any architecture and is a hard-won lesson: a running double total lets delivery order decide a money outcome. Law 2 (branch-consensus time stamped at append) is what makes kitchen ages and ETAs consistent across devices with wrong clocks, which is a real Pakistani-hardware fact and survives cloud-first unchanged. Law 1 is the one whose necessity shrinks with P2P — but the ORACLE survives, because it is the only instrument in the repo that can distinguish a correct merge from a convergent-and-wrong one, and specs/26 §8 records that plain convergence testing blesses a min-envelope-id tiebreak.
  - *size:* merge-invariance.test.ts + merge-builders.ts, plus time-invariance/branch-time/branch-time-mesh and fold-money-guard suites; the shift_cash fold has its own invariance suite (shift-cash-invariance.test.ts).
- **specs/19 (build-vs-buy), specs/25 (the measured defect + refuted proposals + the F69 ablation), specs/26 (merge semantics), SYNC-ORDERING-PROBLEM.md.**
  - *why:* These are a decision record of a quality most companies never produce. specs/19 disqualifies six vendors with verified evidence and names Ditto as a documented exit ramp with a negotiation checklist. specs/25 §9 lists the team's own wrong claims so a reader can calibrate. Under a cloud-first rewrite these become the argument for WHY the rewrite is safe — they already contain the refutation of every naive alternative someone will propose in the first week.
  - *size:* specs/19 66 lines, specs/25 288, specs/26 137, SYNC-ORDERING-PROBLEM.md 202, KERNEL.md 97.
- **packages/auditor — the nightly read-only correctness legs (conservation, lamport gaps, audit chain, state legality, read-model diff against the REAL merge engine).**
  - *why:* 20 §4.2 calls it 'the single highest-value correctness artifact we build', and under SaaS it becomes MORE valuable, not less: it is the thing that catches a money anomaly across a fleet of tenants before a customer does. It refolds through the same engine rather than reimplementing folds (fold-engine.ts subpath exists precisely so the gateway can reach the folds without better-sqlite3).
  - *size:* 567 LOC; 10 gateway acceptance suites (auditor-conservation, auditor-lamport-gap, auditor-audit-chain, auditor-state-legality, auditor-readmodel-diff, auditor-purity, auditor-report-contract + 3 fix-round files). Now scheduled by services/jobs on a BullMQ repeatable.

### Redo

- **Device-side ledger retention and cold start. Today the device holds every event it has ever seen forever, and re-folds all of it on every launch.**
  - *why:* MEASURED: refoldTx() at device-store.ts:1191 runs unconditionally at open and costs ~30 us/event linearly (99ms@1k → 959ms@32k on a Ryzen 5 3600); retentionDrop has zero production callers so N grows without bound; 720 bytes/event on disk. Projected to a year of a busy branch (~3M events) that is 8-15 minutes of boot on the target 2 GB tablet and ~2.2 GB of database. 01-F14 specifies the rolling window and nobody built it. This is a launch blocker under EVERY architecture including the current one, and it is not what specs/25 is about.
  - *shape:* Two independent fixes, both already anticipated by the corpus. (a) A fold SNAPSHOT/checkpoint (specs/25 §7 option F) so cold open replays from the last snapshot rather than from genesis — the SQLite projection tables are already the materialised state, so this is 'trust the tables unless a generation marker says otherwise', not new machinery. (b) Actually implement 01-F14's rolling window: prune events below a global_seq watermark (specs/26 §7 row 3 already reserves global_seq as a prune watermark and it is the ONE device-side thing that still legitimately needs an ordering read). Under cloud-first this gets easier, not harder — the server holds the full ledger, so the device is pruning a cache and can always re-fetch.
- **LAN mesh: peer-to-peer merge with hub election → a branch-local relay with a FIXED authority.**
  - *why:* The requirement (01-F13: only the counter has internet; waiter tablets and the kitchen screen are LAN-only by design) is real and is not negotiable — apps/pass-kds/src/main/uplink.ts says the cloud being 'the ONLY path' means a branch whose internet drops has a kitchen that stops learning about new orders. But that requirement is satisfied by a designated local relay, which is the Toast pattern specs/19 §4.2 cites as the precedent. What the corpus built BEYOND Toast is deterministic election among peers, sub-10s re-election, split-brain tolerance, per-origin lamport contiguity, relay attestation, and full symmetric peer merge — and those buy resilience to 'the counter is off', a state in which the restaurant cannot take money anyway. They also force the merge algebra to handle device↔device concurrency (supersedes-DAG head sets, contested-terminal MVRs) that a single branch authority makes unreachable.
  - *shape:* Keep createWsLanTransport and the star topology. Delete hub-election.ts and mesh-session.ts's election/candidate/split-brain state machine; the counter is the branch server by configuration (it already is by 01-F13 priority — apps/pos-electron/src/main/mesh.ts:91 hardcodes counter_electron and argues correctly that it must not be a parameter). Tablets speak the SAME device→server protocol to it that they speak to the cloud; the relay forwards upward. Concurrent-write merge then only has to handle the WAN boundary (branch vs cloud) and genuine multi-till concurrency at one branch — which the money algebra already handles via 01-F31 attempt keys. Estimated deletion: ~660 prod LOC + most of 3,133 test LOC, plus a large simplification of merge.ts's table/line concurrency arms. DO NOT delete before ruling on the open question below about hub-down operation.
- **LAN peer authentication.**
  - *why:* apps/pos-electron/src/main/mesh.ts:79 ships the literal string 'lan-member-unauthenticated' and the file states plainly that mesh-session's hello arm inspects no token. In a single-restaurant install this is a shop-Wi-Fi risk; in a SaaS where you sell to thousands of strangers and the ledger is the tax/audit trail, an unauthenticated write path into a branch's ledger is not shippable.
  - *shape:* Under the branch-relay shape above this becomes trivial and is another argument for it: the relay is a server, so tablets present the same device token they'd present to the cloud (01-F47, already minted by provision-device, already verified by auth.ts), and the relay verifies it against a cached registry snapshot. Under the current peer-merge shape you need per-event origin signatures, which KERNEL.md:97 already names as the missing piece behind the DEC-SYNC-009 residual.
- **Tenant onboarding: shell commands on the service host → an authenticated self-serve surface.**
  - *why:* services/sync-gateway/package.json declares create-org/create-branch/create-owner/provision-device/revoke-device as tsx CLIs. tenancy.ts:27-32 explains they emit no event because a shell has no authenticated actor — which means a SaaS signup today leaves no attributable ledger record of who provisioned what. 01-F25's pairing code is unbuilt, so a new customer's till still needs a human with SSH.
  - *shape:* The logic is right and already validated through packages/domain records; what is missing is a caller with an actor. Put create-org/create-branch/create-owner behind services/api's authenticated tRPC (which already builds every procedure with authorized(<action>) and refuses at boot to host an ungated one — services/api/src/server.ts assertEveryProcedureIsGated), so OrgEvent.actor_user_id is a real user and 15-F4/15-F3's audit half closes. Then build 01-F25's pairing code so a device provisions itself from a code the owner reads off the back office, rather than from three UUIDs typed on both sides.
- **Event sourcing's centre of gravity: device-authoritative full replica → server-authoritative log with a device-side bounded projection.**
  - *why:* Event sourcing earns its cost on the SERVER — the append-only ledger IS the audit trail in a cash business, it is what makes the Auditor possible, and it is cheap in Postgres where you can prune to cold storage. It costs the most on the DEVICE, where it produces the unbounded refold above and forces every device to hold and re-derive the whole branch history. The device does not need to be the system of record; it needs to not lose a sale.
  - *shape:* Device keeps: an append-only OUTBOX (durable, plug-pull safe, 01-F2's persist-before-ack — device-store.ts already does this and it is the non-negotiable part), plus a materialised projection covering the current business day and a short tail. Server keeps: the full event log, global_seq, the merge, the Auditor. The device stops being a replica and becomes a cache with a durable write queue. Note this KEEPS 01-F2, 01-F17 and commandment 4 fully intact — a sale is still persisted locally before ack and still never blocked — while removing the O(history) work from the device entirely.

### Delete

- **packages/sync-client/src/hub-election.ts (27 LOC) and the election/candidate/split-brain half of mesh-session.ts, IF the branch-relay ruling goes that way.**
  - *why:* Deterministic election among peers, sub-10s re-election and split-brain tolerance exist so the branch keeps coordinating when the elected hub disappears. With a fixed branch authority (the counter, which 01-F13 already ranks first and which apps/pos-electron/src/main/mesh.ts:91 already hardcodes as non-parameterisable) there is nothing to elect. Also deletes HUB-ELECTION.md, hub-election.test.ts, mesh-suspect-clear.test.ts, journey-j3-hub-handover.test.ts and the handover arms of branch-time-mesh.test.ts.
- **The DEC-SYNC-009 hub-relay ATTESTATION machinery in the gateway — per-origin lamport contiguity tracking for relayed third-party events, relay_authorized grant composition, origin-registry veto on relayed pushes, and the relayed-cloud-ack path back over LAN.**
  - *why:* 43 `relay` sites in gateway.ts plus relay code in auth.ts, registry.ts, schema.ts, provision-device.ts, errors.ts, plus 5 dedicated gateway acceptance suites and relay-builders.ts/relay-*.test.ts in sync-client. Its whole purpose is 'a hub pushes events it did not author on behalf of a WAN-less peer', which carries the accepted forgery risk at KERNEL.md:97. Under a branch relay that is a plain proxy speaking each device's own authenticated session upward, the attestation problem disappears rather than being mitigated: every event is pushed under its own device's token.
- **packages/sync-client/src/folds/merge.ts's device↔device concurrency arms specifically — the supersedes-DAG head sets with materialized tombstones for concurrent table assignment, and the contested-terminal line-state MVR — IF peer merge goes.**
  - *why:* These exist because two devices partitioned FROM EACH OTHER can both assign the same table or both drive the same line to different terminal states, with no authority to arbitrate. specs/26 §7 is explicit that supersedes[] on order.table_assigned is 'the only thing that makes the table anchor converge at all'. With one branch authority these become ordinary last-writer-at-the-relay. DO NOT delete the money arms — 01-F31 unique-keyed attempt sums and the refund cap still protect against two tills settling one bill and against crash-retries, which survive any topology (and AGENTS.md records a real Rs 2,240 double-settlement measured on two tills).
- **packages/sync-client/src/transport-rn.ts's LAN half and the RN mesh path (183 LOC), and the mDNS work item on 18 §14's open registry.**
  - *why:* apps/waiter and apps/rider are stubs; there is no shipping React Native device. Building an Android multicast responder for a discovery mechanism whose consumers do not exist, on a mesh that may be downgraded to a relay, is speculative work against a requirement (01-F12's mDNS half) that a typed peer list already satisfies for every deployment that exists. The cloud half of transport-rn.ts stays — it is the phone's device→server transport.
- **Nothing else. In particular: do NOT delete the event log, the outbox, the folds, the merge-invariance oracle, the Auditor, or the branch-time layer.**
  - *why:* Every one of these is either required by the offline-first constraint (which is non-negotiable in Pakistan and is the product's actual moat against Foodpanda-tier competitors) or is the only instrument that can prove a merge is correct rather than merely convergent. The temptation in a 'we deviated, rewrite it as SaaS' moment is to throw out the kernel wholesale. On the evidence measured here that would discard the highest-quality 17k lines in the repo to solve a problem (tenancy) it does not have.

### Open questions

- THE DECIDING QUESTION FOR THE P2P FORK: how often does a real branch need devices to keep taking and cooking orders while the COUNTER specifically is off, rebooting, or dead? Hub election, split-brain tolerance and symmetric peer merge exist for exactly that state and for nothing else. If a counter-down branch is already a stopped restaurant (no cash drawer, no receipt printer, no card terminal), the branch-relay shape is strictly better and ~660 prod + ~3,133 test LOC come out. If a pilot shows tablets meaningfully carrying service through counter reboots or power events that hit the Windows box but not the tablets, the mesh earns its keep. I cannot measure this from the repo — it is a field fact.
- What fraction of the first 1,000 SaaS customers are T1 (one till, one branch)? AGENTS.md and 02-F40 say T1 is 'most deployments'. If T2/T3 multi-terminal branches are under ~15% of the addressable market, the entire LAN-mesh investment serves a minority tier and should be sequenced after signup/billing regardless of which shape it takes.
- Does tax/fiscalization (specs/16) or any Pakistani regulatory requirement demand a device-side immutable ledger, or is a server-side one sufficient? If the FBR-facing story requires the terminal itself to hold a tamper-evident chain, device-side event sourcing becomes non-negotiable and the 'shrink the device to a cache' recommendation is dead. Nobody in the corpus states this either way and it changes the answer.
- Is the local ledger's audit trail a SELLING feature to restaurant owners (cash-business trust: 'your staff cannot delete a sale') or an internal correctness mechanism? If it is a selling feature, the device-side chain has product value beyond correctness and should survive at full strength. restaurant-os.md pitches the platform on take-rate and breadth, not on tamper-evidence, but the kernel treats it as the spine (KERNEL.md:15).
- What retention does a branch actually need on-device? 01-F14 says 'current business day + configurable N days' and nobody has picked N. This single number decides whether the cold-start fix is a snapshot, a prune, or both — and whether a device that has been offline for a week can still serve a customer asking about yesterday's order.
- Under SaaS, is a branch-local relay a SUPPORTED, SHIPPED artifact (an installer, an update channel, a health surface) or is it the counter app moonlighting? The corpus assumes the latter. A SaaS vendor supporting thousands of branch-local relays it cannot SSH into is a real operational commitment that nobody has costed, and it is the same commitment either way — mesh or relay.
- Does the founder want the cloud to be able to REPLAY a tenant's ledger into a fresh device (disaster recovery, device replacement, dispute resolution)? The gateway already stores everything needed and catchup_request/response already pages it, but nothing exercises full-ledger restore, and specs/22 (backup/DR) has no implementation. This is table stakes for SaaS and is close to free given what exists.

### Adversarial verdicts

- **AMENDED** — Unbounded cold-start refold: every store open re-reads, Zod-parses and re-folds the ENTIRE local event log, and nothing ever prunes it. No document names it — specs/25 is entirely about the OLD quadratic.
  - MECHANISM CONFIRMED, 'no document names it' REFUTED. Confirmed: device-store.ts:1191 `refoldTx();` runs unconditionally in the factory body ('Self-heal on open (01-F6)'); :1134 refoldTx → :801 recomputeFolds → :748 readAllParsed, which is `[...allOwnEnvelopes.all(), ...allPeerEnvelopes.all()]` over `SELECT envelope FROM events` (:620) and `SELECT envelope FROM peer_events` (:632) — no LIMIT, no window, parseEvent on every row, then engine.rebuild. Confirmed: `grep -arn retentionDrop` over apps/services/packages returns only the type decl (:224), the tx (:1180), the impl (:1316,:1318), two comments, three error strings in folds/merge.ts, and tests — ZERO production callers, and it drops fold-table rows, never event rows. Confirmed: 01-F14's rolling window (specs/01-kernel-sync.md:39) is unimplemented. REFUTED sub-claim: three documents already name this. (a) specs/01-kernel-sync.md §5 'Compaction: devices prune the branch stream beyond the rolling window but always retain events referenced by open entities' — spec'd and unbuilt. (b) packages/sync-client/README.md:301-306 states it outright: '`retentionDrop` is session-durable-only … the *ledger* rows survive, so a reopen legitimately rebuilds the dropped keys from the full retained ledger. True durable pruning is the compaction task's `global_seq` prune-watermark (01 §5), **not yet built**.' (c) specs/25:117 option F is 'Snapshots/checkpoints … Bounds cold-start replay only' — considered and set aside, so specs/25 is not 'entirely about the old quadratic'. Severity: downgrade blocker → major. This is spec'd-and-owed scope with the gap written down in the package README, not a hidden defect; at the finding's own ~8-10k events/day it is ~300 ms at one day and only bites after months with no pruning, and the 3M-events/year extrapolation to a 2 GB tablet is unverified by me.
- **CONFIRMED** — There is no LAN peer authentication. Any device on the shop Wi-Fi can join a branch mesh and inject events into the branch ledger.
  - Verified end to end, and the mesh now genuinely ships (AGENTS.md's '@unreached-owed NO HOST RUNS THE LAN MESH YET' is stale — no such marker remains in mesh-session.ts/hub-election.ts/transport-ws.ts, and apps/pos-electron/src/main/index.ts:69 imports createLanMesh and wires stop/notifyAppended/reachability at :668,:678,:825). apps/pos-electron/src/main/mesh.ts:79 and apps/pass-kds/src/main/mesh.ts:77 both declare LAN_HELLO_PLACEHOLDER = 'lan-member-unauthenticated', used at :152/:146 as the hello token. packages/sync-client/src/mesh-session.ts:442 'case "hello"' checks only store.isRevokedPeer(from) then admitFollower(from) — the token field is never read (its own comment at :151 says the hello arm 'inspects no token'). :468 'case "push"' likewise gates only on isRevokedPeer and then handlePush → store ingest → fan-out to the branch. Transport listens on a real socket: transport-ws.ts:184 `new WebSocketServer({ port, host: listenHost })` with packages/device-config/src/lan-mesh.ts DEFAULT_LAN_LISTEN_HOST = '0.0.0.0' — deliberately, per its own comment. I searched for the property rather than a symbol (token/verify/auth/claims across transport-ws.ts and mesh-session.ts): no verification exists anywhere on the LAN leg. Contrast confirmed on the cloud leg (gateway.ts:438-452 relayAuthorized from signed claims). Blocker severity holds: this is an unauthenticated write path into the money ledger, live in two shipping Electron hosts.
- **AMENDED** — A compromised relay-authorized hub can fabricate events attributed to any registered branch peer. Evidence: KERNEL.md:97; services/sync-gateway/src/gateway.ts:150 `registryValid(envelope)` checks existence only.
  - SUBSTANCE CONFIRMED, CODE CITATION WRONG. KERNEL.md:97 is verbatim as quoted (DEC-SYNC-009, accepted; 'the gateway's origin gate checks the origin's existence, not authorship; Wave 0 has no per-event origin signatures'). But gateway.ts:150 `registryValid` is NOT the origin gate — it is `try { parseEvent(envelope); return true } catch { return false }`, a pure schema parse whose failure quarantines as 'schema_invalid' (:847-848).
