The real gate is the identity block at gateway.ts:777-798: org_mismatch / branch_mismatch / `envelope.device_id !== session.deviceId && !session.relayAuthorized` → device_mismatch, plus the per-batch `originRows` registry-existence lookup declared at :773-775 ('null = no row for (session org, claimed device)'). So a relay-authorized session may push envelopes attributed to any registered, same-org/same-branch device — the claim holds, on different lines. Severity 'major' is fair but note it is a ratified, documented residual (DEC-SYNC-009), not an undiscovered hole.
- **CONFIRMED** — Tenant ONBOARDING is a shell command on the service host, not a product surface; no billing/plan/metering code exists.
  - services/sync-gateway/package.json scripts confirmed verbatim: create-org, create-branch, create-owner, list-tenancy, provision-device, revoke-device — all `tsx src/*.ts`, alongside dev/start/migrate. services/sync-gateway/src/tenancy.ts carries the quoted rationale in its header block ('NOTHING HERE EMITS AN EVENT … a command on a service host has no authenticated user, so OrgEvent.actor_user_id could only ever be null, permanently, in an append-only store (01-F1)') — the finding's line range 27-32 is right to within a line or two. 01-F25's pairing code (specs/01-kernel-sync.md:58, 'Registration is a one-time pairing via back office code') is unbuilt: `grep -arn -i pairing` across apps/services/packages returns only typography/layout comments, and apps/backoffice/src/components/ holds device-list.tsx (the 14-F13 revoke surface) with no provisioning/pairing screen. Billing: searched the property, not one symbol (billing|subscription|metering|plan_|8,000) — every hit is a comment explicitly deferring it ('anything to do with plan, billing, quota or metering (15-F5a, 15-F24)' in packages/domain/src/tenancy.ts:196 and services/sync-gateway/src/schema.ts:354) against restaurant-os.md:108's 'PKR 8,000 / branch / month'. No implementation.
- **AMENDED** — mDNS discovery — half of 01-F12 — does not exist. The LAN mesh runs on a hand-typed peer list.
  - FACTS CONFIRMED, SEVERITY INFLATED. packages/device-config/src/lan-mesh.ts:14-21 says exactly what is quoted ('⚠ THE mDNS HALF IS NOT IMPLEMENTED … 18 §14 line 197 lists mDNS on Android … as an OPEN registry item rather than an allowlisted dependency'); LAN_MESH_ENV is at :34-38 (finding said 35-39) with RESTOS_LAN_HOST/PORT/PEERS and LanPeer = {device_id, host, port}. I re-ran the search for the property (mdns|bonjour|zeroconf|multicast|dns-sd across .ts/.tsx/.json, -a): every hit is a comment or a test header — no responder, no dependency. But 01-F12 itself reads 'discover each other on the LAN (mDNS; **manual IP fallback**)', so the shipped manual directory is the FR's own sanctioned mechanism, not a violation; and adding an mDNS package is gated by 18 §15 process, so it is not an implementer's omission. lan-mesh.ts:188 even emits an operator-facing message naming the gap. This is a documented, deliberately deferred half of a two-mechanism FR — minor/known-deferred, not major. The real residual risk (DHCP reassignment silently breaking the peer list) is an ops concern the finding does not actually state.
- **CONFIRMED** — The headline performance contract — 00 §5's 'catch-up after 8h offline with ~500 orders < 60 s on 4G' — has never been measured end-to-end; every number in specs/25 and specs/26 is fold CPU only.
  - Every citation checks out line-exact. specs/26-merge-semantics.md:58 is the quoted 'The budget is END-TO-END, and every measurement in this document is CPU-only' sentence; :79 is 'Do not claim the target is met until the complete path — compressed 4G transfer, batched SQLite persistence, zero-work sequence adoption, incremental projection — passes on the reference 2–3 GB Android tablet.' KERNEL.md:95 is 'Not yet done at all: physical wall-clock proof on a real 2 GB tablet (p95, plug-pull — deferred to the hardware rig, D3) … The kernel is verified in logic and simulation; it has never run on target hardware.' The staleness note is also right: compression IS wired (transport-ws.ts:285-286 swaps `codec = createFrameCodec(message.compression)` on a hello_ack the client advertised for, :325 records the advertisement, gateway.ts:499 emits the negotiated grant, server.ts:92-93 adopts it after sending the plain ack), so KERNEL.md:81's '(codec present … not yet wired into live transport)' is out of date. I also checked the OTHER bottleneck specs/26 §6.4 names — per-event `store.ingest()` on catch-up — and it too is closed: cloud-session.ts:353-362 now calls `store.ingestPage(items)` for the whole page in one transaction. So both transport bottlenecks are closed and the end-to-end wall-clock measurement remains the outstanding item, exactly as claimed. Severity major is defensible, with the caveat that this is self-documented deferred work (rig D3), not a discovered defect.

## client-surfaces

**Verdict.** The founder's read is correct and understated for this dimension. 82% of all app code (68,223 of 83,287 ts/tsx lines in `apps/`) is two Electron desktop apps that boot from a git checkout on the restaurant's own PC; the only "open a browser and log in" surface in the repo is `apps/backoffice` (10,503 LOC), which is the owner's price editor, not the product. There is no installer, no auto-updater and no code signing anywhere — a symbol-precise sweep of every `package.json` for `electron-builder`/`electron-updater`/`nsis`/`squirrel` returns zero hits — so the till is literally deployed as `C:\restos` + `pnpm -C apps\pos-electron start` in a `.bat` restart loop (`ops/startup/restos-counter.bat:189`), reading per-machine UUIDs and **staff PINs in plaintext** from `ops/env/counter.env`, against three hardcoded seed users you "cannot add, rename or remove" (`packages/device-config/src/dev-staff.ts:90`). That is single-tenant on-prem in its purest form. BUT the coupling is far shallower than the LOC suggests, and this is the load-bearing finding: only **3 of 21** files in `apps/pos-electron/src/main/` import `electron` or `node:*` at all (`index.ts`, `printer-link.ts`, `file-printer.ts` — 2,673 of 9,777 main LOC); the other 7,104 lines (read models, authorization, KOT orchestration, settlement guards, folds) are platform-free TypeScript. The SQLite dependency is already behind a 6-method port (`packages/sync-client/src/storage.ts`) with two drivers shipped. The renderer↔main seam is exactly **21 typed methods in one 79-line file** (`apps/pos-electron/src/preload/index.ts`). The tokens genuinely honour `specs/00:115` ("RN and web share logic and tokens, not pixels") — the RN app imports only `@restos/ui/tokens`, never a component. The three things that truly cannot cross into a browser are: raw TCP to a printer, a LAN WebSocket **server** (the branch hub), and physical panel millimetres (obtained by shelling out to PowerShell WMI / `xrandr`, `apps/pos-electron/src/main/index.ts:298-312`). Two of those three are the *offline-first architecture itself*, not incidental hardware, so the real question this dimension forces is whether commandment 4 survives SaaS — not whether Electron does.

### Findings

- **App inventory re-measured. Two Electron apps are 82% of all app code; six of ten apps are 2-line stubs (`export {};`). Orchestrator's numbers verified except backoffice, which measures 10,503 ts/tsx (10,641 incl. css/mjs), not 11,306.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* `find apps -name '*.ts' -o -name '*.tsx' | xargs cat | wc -l` per app: pos-electron 55,397 (test 35,318 / prod 20,079); pass-kds 12,826 (test 7,983 / prod 4,843); backoffice 10,503 (test 5,584 / prod 4,919); manager 3,746 (test 2,530 / prod 1,216); owner, platform-admin, pos-rn, rider, storefront, waiter = 2 each. Stub body verified identical in all six, e.g. `apps/pos-rn/src/index.ts:2` = `export {};`, `apps/pos-rn/package.json:8` `"build": "echo 'no build yet — scaffold stub'"`. Electron share = (55,397+12,826)/83,287 = 81.9%.
- **There is no packaged artifact. The product is not installed — it is a git checkout that rebuilds from source at every boot. No installer, no updater, no signing.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* `grep -arn "electron-builder|electron-updater|nsis|squirrel" --include='*.json' --include='*.yml'` outside node_modules: zero hits. `apps/pos-electron/package.json:11` `"start": "electron-vite build && electron out/main/index.js"`. `ops/startup/restos-counter.bat:28` `set "RESTOS_REPO=C:\restos"`, `:189` `call pnpm -C apps\pos-electron start`, plus a documented manual `pnpm rebuild:native` per checkout (`.bat:12`). `specs/18-engineering-handbook.md:182` lists `electron-builder` on the allowlist; it is not installed.
- **The Electron MAIN process is shallowly coupled: only 3 of 21 main modules import `electron` or `node:*`. 73% of main-process LOC is portable TypeScript that would move to a server unchanged.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* Per-file grep of `^import.*from "electron"|^import.*from "node:` across `apps/pos-electron/src/main/*.ts`: hits only in `index.ts` (5), `printer-link.ts` (2), `file-printer.ts` (3). Zero in `printing.ts` (1,883), `gateway.ts` (1,023), `authorize.ts` (768), `line-advance.ts` (529), `approval-record.ts` (309), `catalog.ts` (277), `station-routing.ts` (266), `settlement-guard.ts` (253), `aggregator-settlement.ts` (223), `settlement-closer.ts` (212), `hardware-tier.ts` (211), `mesh.ts` (209), `job-store.ts` (164), `zero-tender-guard.ts` (154), `line-removal-guard.ts` (132), `sync.ts` (130), `customer-phone.ts` (73), `window-options.ts` (288). Coupled = 2,673 LOC; free = 7,104 LOC of 9,777.
- **PRINTING: there is no USB or serial driver anywhere in the repo. All three shipped 'transports' reduce to a TCP socket or a filesystem write. `serialport`, `usb` and `ble-plx` — all named by `specs/18 §10`/`§14` — are absent from every package.json.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* `grep -arn '"serialport"|"usb"|ble-plx' --include=package.json apps/ packages/ services/` → zero hits. `apps/pos-electron/src/main/printer-link.ts:71-72` imports only `node:fs/promises` `writeFile` and `node:net` `createConnection`. `:428` states it: "**No `serialport` and no `usb` dependency** — both are on `18 §14`'s allowlist and neither is installed". `windows://ShareName` writes to `\\localhost\<share>` (the OS spooler); `device:///dev/usb/lp0` writes to a character device. `:62` concedes both are write-only, so `03-F40`'s paper sensor is unreachable and a paper-out reads as a printed ticket.
- **K-8 (a physical printer pass) has NEVER been done — verified independently of AGENTS.md, in 12 places across shipped code, specs and ops docs. Nothing in `packages/escpos` is evidence about paper.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* `apps/pos-electron/src/main/printer-link.ts:14` "**IT DOES NOT CLOSE K-8. NO PRINTER HAS BEEN ATTACHED TO ANY OF THIS.**"; `packages/escpos/src/index.ts:8` "K-8 is the physical pass — NO PRINTER HAS EVER BEEN ATTACHED"; `plans/wave-1/kot-printing.md:175` "| **K-8** | **Physical verification on real hardware — OWED, not done** |"; `ops/README.md:652`; `specs/03-kitchen-fulfillment.md:193,219,230`; `apps/pos-electron/src/main/__acceptance__/receipt-printing.test.ts:32`; `ops/env/counter.env.example` "NO PRINTER HAS EVER BEEN TESTED AGAINST THIS CODE (K-8)".
- **LOCAL SQLITE is already portable: `better-sqlite3` is constructed in exactly ONE file behind a 6-method `StorageAdapter` port, and a second driver (RN op-sqlite) already ships. A browser driver is a third driver, not a rewrite — but the port mandates SYNCHRONOUS statements because `01-F2` requires durable-before-ack, which no browser storage API offers on the main thread.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* `packages/sync-client/src/storage-node.ts:18` is the sole `import Database from "better-sqlite3"`; its header `:9` asserts "**This is the ONE module in the package that constructs `better-sqlite3`**, and that is an asserted property". Port = `packages/sync-client/src/storage.ts` (`prepare`, `exec`, `pragma`, `transaction`, `close` + statement `run/get/all`). Second driver: `storage-op-sqlite.ts` (207 LOC) + door `rn.ts`. Property 1 in `storage.ts:29`: "**Everything is SYNCHRONOUS.** `01-F2` / `00 §5.2`: confirmed means durably persisted *before* the UI acknowledges it… `append()` is a synchronous value-returning function all the way up to the renderer."
- **The LAN branch hub is a WebSocket SERVER hosted by the till — a browser cannot listen on a socket. AGENTS.md and ops/env/counter.env.example both still say nothing hosts the mesh; that is stale.** [MINOR]
  - *evidence:* `apps/pos-electron/src/main/mesh.ts:134,147` calls `createWsLanTransport(...)` and `createMeshSession(...)`; reached from production at `apps/pos-electron/src/main/index.ts:667` `const mesh = createLanMesh({ store, lan, onChanged: notifyRenderer });` — unconditionally, before the cloud uplink. Same pair at `apps/pass-kds/src/main/mesh.ts:128,141` / `index.ts:291`. `grep -an unreached-owed packages/sync-client/src/mesh-session.ts` → no hits (marker deleted). Contradicting stale docs: AGENTS.md "`mesh-session.ts` carries `@unreached-owed NO HOST RUNS THE LAN MESH YET`" and `ops/env/counter.env.example` "⚠ NOTHING HOSTS THE MESH TODAY".
- **AUTHORIZATION is enforced on the customer's own till, and the code says so in as many words. Commandment 8's "server-side authorization" is satisfied by an Electron main process running on premises — which under multi-tenant SaaS is a client, not a server.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* `apps/pos-electron/src/main/authorize.ts:28-30`: "`18 §9` gives the renderer no Node access and one typed IPC bridge, so on this app the MAIN process is what \"server-side\" means: it is the only side that holds the store, the session and the staff registry". Same sentence repeated at `apps/pos-electron/src/main/index.ts:671`. The two files importing the `@restos/domain` matrix in shipping code are `main/authorize.ts` and `services/api/src/trpc.ts` — i.e. one on-device, one in cloud.
- **The physical-millimetre design language requires shelling out to PowerShell WMI / xrandr for the panel's real size. A browser cannot supply this, and the documented fallback renders every touch target at ~45% of its ergonomic size on a 10.1" tablet with nothing looking broken.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* `apps/pos-electron/src/main/index.ts:1` `import { execFileSync } from "node:child_process"`; `:298-312` `screen.getPrimaryDisplay()` + `measurePhysicalWidthMm(process.platform, (cmd,args)=>execFileSync(...))`. Probes at `packages/device-config/src/panel-density.ts:119` (`Get-CimInstance … WmiMonitorBasicDisplayParams`) and `:127` (`xrandr --query`). `:17` "`screen.getPrimaryDisplay()` carries `size` … and **no physical size**". `:53` "on a ~10.1″ tablet (~224 PPI of real glass) assuming 15.6″ yields ~100 PPI, so `cssPxPerDp` renders **every `27-F8` target at about 45% of its ergonomic size** … and **nothing on the screen looks wrong**."
- **packages/ui DOES honour `specs/00:115` ("RN and web share logic and tokens, not pixels") — but the consequence is that the 22 components are an ELECTRON-TILL library, not a shared design system. The RN app imports zero components; the web app imports exactly one.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* 22 component files (`ls packages/ui/src/components/*.tsx` minus stories/tests = 22; `index.ts` is 22 export lines — AGENTS.md's "18 components" is stale). `grep -arn react-native packages/ui/src` → zero hits; primitives used are `<div>` (51), `<span>` (41), `<button>` (13), `<input>` (5). RN consumer `apps/manager` imports only tokens: `src/App.tsx:26` `import { color, space, typography } from "@restos/ui/tokens"`, `src/probe.ts:20`. Web consumer `apps/backoffice` imports tokens+fonts and ONE component: `src/components/owner-summary.tsx:52` `import { MoneyValue } from "@restos/ui"`. All 22 components are consumed only by `apps/pos-electron` and `apps/pass-kds` — both Electron.
- **The token manifest and the bundled typeface are genuinely platform-neutral and survive any runtime: unitless numbers in JSON, real woff2 bytes on disk.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* `packages/ui/src/tokens/tokens.json` groups `color/space/touch/typography/kds/money`; values are bare numbers — `space-1..8` = 4/8/12/16/24/32/48/64, `touch-counter` 76, `touch-keypad` 126, `touch-kitchen` 96, `touch-handheld` 64, `touch-floor` 48 (all dp), `kds-primary-arcmin` 30, `kds-reference-distance-mm` 1500. `grep -ac 'px"|rem"|var(--|calc(' packages/ui/src/tokens/*.ts` = 0 in every file. Fonts are real bytes: `packages/ui/src/fonts/ibm-plex-sans-latin-{400,500,600}-normal.woff2` (22.6/24.2/24.3 KB) + `plex-latin.ts` (95 KB base64) + `OFL.txt`.
- **The renderer↔main seam is 21 typed methods in one 79-line file, and the renderer itself has zero Node/Electron imports. This is the single most valuable structural asset for a SaaS port: it is already an API contract.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* `apps/pos-electron/src/preload/index.ts` (79 LOC) exposes exactly: deviceState, openOrders, kitchenQueue, menu, staff, cashState, alarms, acknowledgeAlarm, resendAlarm, quickTags, append, addLine, toggleAvailability, lookupCustomer, recordCustomer, escalationFor, escalate, denyEscalation, unlock, lock, onChanged. Its header `:9` "no generic `invoke`, no channel parameter, no escape hatch". Contract types in `apps/pos-electron/src/shared/ipc.ts` (1,147 LOC). `grep -arn 'require\(|node:|from "electron"' apps/pos-electron/src/renderer/*.tsx` → hits only in two test files, none in the 5,363 LOC of shipped renderer.
- **`packages/escpos` is reached by exactly ONE app and has one production spooler construction. It is real, but it is not load-bearing for anything except the Windows till.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* `grep -arn '@restos/escpos' --include=package.json` → `apps/pos-electron/package.json:19` and `packages/testing/package.json:12` only. `grep -arn 'createSpooler(' apps/ services/ --include='*.ts'` minus tests → exactly one: `apps/pos-electron/src/main/index.ts:1229`. Five non-test files import it, all under `apps/pos-electron/src/main/`. `apps/pass-kds` does not depend on it at all.
- **`packages/escpos` is 77% test/oracle code: 3,702 LOC of implementation carries 12,418 LOC of tests plus 1,887 LOC of oracle-surface files. Two declared capabilities are dead: Font B cannot be emitted at all, and `cols_font_b` has zero production readers.** [MINOR]
  - *evidence:* `find packages/escpos/src -name '*.ts' ! -path '*__acceptance__*' | xargs wc -l` = 3,702; `__acceptance__` total = 12,418 incl. 1,887 of `*oracle-surface*`. Largest impl files: `simulate.ts` 557, `document.ts` 538, `cash-documents.ts` 425, `spooler.ts` 367, `encoder.ts` 365. `packages/escpos/CLAUDE.md` (Font B row): "the encoder **cannot emit Font B at all** (no `ESC M` in `encoder.ts` … ) and **`cols_font_b` has zero production readers**".
- **The layout:check rail exists twice (once per Electron app), totals 4,480 LOC, and sweeps exactly 11 panels — verified by reading the PANELS array, not the docs.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* `apps/pos-electron/src/layout-gate/`: main.ts 2,057 + preload.ts 604 + mode-contract.ts 461 + probe.ts 451 = 3,573. `apps/pass-kds/src/layout-gate/`: main.ts 663 + preload.ts 244 = 907. PANELS labels at `apps/pos-electron/src/layout-gate/main.ts:119-254`: counter-1366, counter-1920, laptop-1280, tablet-10.1, netbook-1024, probe-below-floor (ships:false), tablet-11.6, laptop-12.5, laptop-13.3-hd, desktop-24, ultrawide-32 = 11. Run command: `package.json:13` `"layout:check": "electron-vite build && electron out/main/layout-gate.js"` — it needs an Electron binary.
- **THE POS/ANDROID GAP IS TOTAL AND SELF-CONTRADICTORY. `specs/00` and `specs/18` both specify an Android RN counter; it is 2 LOC. Meanwhile the Electron layout gate ships `tablet-10.1` (1366×768 @10.1") as `ships: true` — a panel class that only exists on Android tablets, for an app that cannot run on Android, because Electron has no Android target.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* `specs/00-platform-overview.md:15` row 02: "POS / counter app … | Windows (Electron), Android (RN) | 1". `specs/18-engineering-handbook.md:31` "pos-rn/ # Android counter (Expo)". Built: `apps/pos-rn/src/index.ts` = 2 lines, `export {};`. `specs/00:141` names the reference hardware as a "PKR ~25k Android tablet (2–3GB RAM)". `apps/pos-electron/src/layout-gate/main.ts:181` `{ label: "tablet-10.1", width: 1366, height: 768, diagonalIn: 10.1, ships: true }` — with a comment at `:160` calling it "the row the compact arrangement was built for … Pakistani restaurants already own". `apps/pos-electron/package.json` has no Android target and Electron ships none.
- **`apps/manager` is the only real RN app and it is read-only by construction: no PIN session, so it can author nothing. Its identity comes from the BUILD (`EXPO_PUBLIC_*`), which is single-tenant baked into a binary.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* `apps/manager/package.json` deps: expo 54, react-native 0.81.5, `@restos/sync-client` (imported via the `/rn` door: `src/branch.ts:63`, `src/home.ts:41`). `apps/manager/CLAUDE.md`: "Identity and the device credential come from `EXPO_PUBLIC_*`, i.e. from the BUILD: `01-F25`'s pairing screen does not exist and `provision-device` needs shell access on the gateway host, which a phone has not… And there is **no PIN session** here, so this device reads the branch and can author nothing". `packages/sync-client/src/rn.ts:28` "**Nothing in this repository executes `open()`**" — op-sqlite is a TurboModule with no rig.
- **Per-restaurant configuration is a hand-edited env file on each physical machine containing plaintext staff PINs, hand-copied UUIDs that nothing cross-checks, and three immutable hardcoded users. This is the single-tenant assumption made literal in ops.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* `ops/env/counter.env.example`: "One file per PHYSICAL machine"; `RESTOS_ORG_ID` "Must equal services/api's BOOTSTRAP_ORG_ID… WRONG VALUE: all four processes report success and this till never sees a menu. No error."; "⚠ THIS IS A DEV SEED BEING USED IN PRODUCTION, KNOWINGLY, BECAUSE THERE IS NO OTHER OPTION… These three keys seed three users — you cannot add, rename or remove them"; keys `RESTOS_DEV_PIN`, `RESTOS_DEV_PIN_BILAL`, `RESTOS_DEV_PIN_HINA`. Roster at `packages/device-config/src/dev-staff.ts:90-95` — Ayesha/Bilal (cashier), Hina (branch_manager), hardcoded UUIDs `00000000-0000-7000-8000-00000000000{4,5,6}`.
- **Only ~25% of the 51,415 LOC of app tests are tied to the Electron/IPC harness; the rest are logic tests that survive a runtime change.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* 119 test files under `apps/`; `grep -arl 'window.restos|ipcMain|from "electron"|BrowserWindow|preload'` matches 30 of them (25%); 41 touch happy-dom / @testing-library. The remaining ~48 are pure logic (folds, guards, schemas, alarm derivation).

### Keep

- **The token manifest + typed view + bundled IBM Plex woff2 (`packages/ui/src/tokens/`, `packages/ui/src/fonts/`)**
  - *why:* Genuinely runtime-neutral: unitless JSON, no px/rem/CSS-var anywhere, already proven to be the ONLY thing the RN app consumes. This is the piece that already implements `specs/00:115` correctly, and it is the piece a SaaS design system would need first. The colour-science oracles (contrast, palette ladder, non-text contrast) are real accessibility work that no rewrite should redo.
  - *size:* tokens 854 prod + 1,733 test; icons 681 + 1,424; fonts 148 + 119 + 71 KB of woff2. Total ~5,030 LOC + font bytes.
- **The pure business logic in `apps/pos-electron/src/main/` that imports neither `electron` nor `node:*`**
  - *why:* This is the actual product — read models, permission-to-event mapping, KOT fan-out and retry budget, settlement guards, line-state advance, aggregator settlement, customer lookup. It is platform-free TypeScript that moves to a server or a service worker unchanged. Measured, not assumed: 18 of 21 main modules have zero platform imports.
  - *size:* 7,104 LOC across 18 files: printing.ts 1,883, gateway.ts 1,023, authorize.ts 768, line-advance.ts 529, approval-record.ts 309, catalog.ts 277, station-routing.ts 266, settlement-guard.ts 253, aggregator-settlement.ts 223, settlement-closer.ts 212, hardware-tier.ts 211, mesh.ts 209, job-store.ts 164, zero-tender-guard.ts 154, line-removal-guard.ts 132, sync.ts 130, customer-phone.ts 73, window-options.ts 288
- **The IPC contract `apps/pos-electron/src/shared/ipc.ts` and the 21-method bridge**
  - *why:* It is already a closed, typed, auditable API surface with no escape hatch — precisely the shape a tRPC router or an HTTP contract needs. The 79-line preload is the only file that has to be replaced to change transport; everything above and below it is unaware of Electron. This is the cheapest port path in the repo.
  - *size:* ipc.ts 1,147 LOC + preload/index.ts 79 LOC = 1,226; 21 methods
- **`packages/escpos`'s pure half — encoder, document model, capability/column derivation, render, simulate**
  - *why:* A correct, heavily-oracled ESC/POS encoder with an allowlist walker, a QR decoder in the tests (jsqr, deliberately not the encoder library), a byte→page simulator with ONE interpretation, and per-document-type column floors. Bytes are deterministic from data, so this runs anywhere — including a cloud print service. There is nothing else like it available off the shelf for Pakistani 58/80 mm stock.
  - *size:* ~2,800 LOC of the 3,702 prod (encoder 365, document 538, document-parts 250, render 239, capability 177, min-columns 161, status 162, simulate 557, cash-documents 425, receipt-document 345), backed by 12,418 LOC of oracle tests
- **`packages/sync-client`'s `StorageAdapter` port and its two drivers**
  - *why:* The hardest part of making the device store portable is already done, deliberately and with an asserted single-construction property. Adding an OPFS/wa-sqlite driver is a third implementation of a 6-method interface, not an architectural change. The port's doc comment even enumerates exactly what a second driver must get right (savepoint nesting, `undefined` on no match, synchronous writes).
  - *size:* storage.ts 89, storage-node.ts 64, storage-op-sqlite.ts 207, rn.ts 76 = 436 LOC + a full contract suite (`storage-contract.ts` 398)
- **`apps/backoffice` (Next.js 16, App Router, tRPC + TanStack Query, server-answered auth gate)**
  - *why:* This is the only surface in the repo already built the way a SaaS is built: browser talks to its own origin, a Next rewrite proxies `/api/trpc`, `RESTOS_API_URL` is read at request time so a deployment repoints the backend without a rebuild, and the auth gate keys on the server's `whoami` answer rather than a stored token claim. It needs multi-tenancy, not a runtime change.
  - *size:* 10,503 ts/tsx (prod 4,919 / test 5,584); `next.config.ts` rewrite; `src/components/auth-gate.tsx` 248
- **The intent behind `specs/27` + `DEC-HW-001` (bring-your-own-hardware) and the panel sweep as a CONCEPT**
  - *why:* A SaaS POS must run on whatever glass a restaurant already owns — the founder ruled exactly this — and the corpus has already done the hard thinking: eleven real panel classes from 202 to 782 mm, a measured floor, a documented refusal to shrink touch targets to make a layout fit, and a `PanelHealth` strip that names degradation instead of hiding it. This reasoning is more valuable in SaaS than on-prem, and it is not written down anywhere else.
  - *size:* specs/27 (77 FRs), DEC-HW-001 in specs/DECISIONS.md:49, PANELS table at layout-gate/main.ts:77-254

### Redo

- **The POS client runtime — `apps/pos-electron`'s hosting shell**
  - *why:* A SaaS restaurant does not get a git checkout at `C:\restos`, a manual `pnpm rebuild:native`, and a `.bat` in shell:startup. The shell is also where every genuinely non-portable thing lives, so replacing it is the same act as decoupling from hardware.
  - *shape:* Split the app in two along the seam that already exists. (a) The 5,363-LOC React DOM renderer + `packages/ui`'s 22 components become a browser app / installable PWA — no rewrite of components, they are already div/span/button. (b) The 21 IPC methods become an HTTP/WS contract served either by a cloud API (if commandment 4 is relaxed) or by a small local edge process (if it is not). Drop `index.ts` (1,890), `window-options.ts` (288), `preload/index.ts` (79). Estimated ~2,257 LOC deleted, ~6,500 LOC re-hosted, renderer ~unchanged.
- **Printer reachability — the last hop from software to paper**
  - *why:* A browser cannot open a TCP socket to port 9100 and cannot write `/dev/usb/lp0`. WebUSB/WebSerial exist but require a class driver this repo does not have (there is no `usb` or `serialport` dependency at all), are Chromium-only, need a per-device user gesture, and cannot run unattended at 05:00 — which is the exact scenario `restos-counter.bat` was written for.
  - *shape:* Keep `render()` in the cloud (bytes are pure) and put the transport + durable spooler in a ~600-LOC signed edge agent (Node single-binary or a WASM-free service) that a restaurant installs once per branch, polls the cloud for jobs, and owns TCP-9100 / OS-spooler delivery. That agent is also the natural home for the LAN hub and the local store. Alternative if the founder will accept it: network printers only, and print via a cloud relay to `tcp://` over a per-branch tunnel — no agent, but it forbids the USB printers that are the actual Pakistani installed base.
- **Authorization placement**
  - *why:* `main/authorize.ts:28` states that the customer's own Electron main process IS "server-side". In multi-tenant SaaS the till is an untrusted client on someone else's premises; a permission verdict computed there is a client role claim, which commandment 8 forbids. Today the till also holds the authoritative append-only ledger.
  - *shape:* Move `can()`/`canPayOut`/`reportScope` enforcement to the cloud API for every write that can tolerate latency, and keep a device-side copy ONLY as an offline-degraded path whose events are re-validated on ingest at the gateway. The `WRITE_ACTIONS` event-type→action table (the one thing the matrix cannot know) moves with it. `services/api/src/trpc.ts` already has the correct shape.
- **The layout gate `layout:check`**
  - *why:* 4,480 LOC across two duplicated copies, each requiring an Electron binary and each importing that app's real `*_WINDOW_OPTIONS`. Under SaaS there is no BrowserWindow to import and no OS to ask for physical millimetres. Its own doc rejects Playwright because "in a headless browser you SET the viewport" — but in a browser-delivered product the app genuinely does get whatever viewport it gets, so setting it is now the correct model, not a blind spot.
  - *shape:* Port the sweep to Playwright/Chromium with the 11 panel rows kept as viewport+DPR pairs, and one copy shared across all surfaces instead of two. The pixel/clipping/composition/reachability checks survive intact. The physical-millimetre half (`27-F8` targets in mm, `27-F27` arcmin) does NOT survive without PPI — either accept CSS-px targets with a documented loss, or expose PPI through the edge agent for the branches that have one.
- **Device identity, provisioning and staff roster**
  - *why:* Three env vars per physical machine, hand-copied from `ops/ids.env`, that nothing cross-checks (`counter.env.example`: "WRONG VALUE: all four processes report success and this till never sees a menu. No error."), plus three immutable seed users with plaintext PINs. A restaurant that signs up online cannot do any of this, and `01-F25`'s pairing code was deliberately never built.
  - *shape:* Build `01-F25`'s pairing flow: owner signs up in the back office → generates a short-lived pairing code → the till (browser or agent) redeems it for an org/branch/device identity and a token, persisted locally. `packages/sync-client/src/cloud-session.ts:197` already persists silent renewals, so only the initial mint is missing. Staff roster must arrive over the catalog path like the menu does, not from env; `DEV_STAFF` is deleted.
- **The device store's synchronicity contract**
  - *why:* `storage.ts` property 1 makes every write synchronous because `01-F2` says confirmed = durably persisted before UI ack. Browser OPFS sync access handles are Worker-only; IndexedDB is async everywhere. So a browser POS either changes `01-F2` or moves the entire store into a Worker and makes the renderer's 21 calls async.
  - *shape:* Founder-level decision, but the implementable version is: store lives in a dedicated Worker over OPFS+wa-sqlite; the bridge becomes async; `01-F2` is restated as "acknowledged only after the Worker confirms the commit", which preserves the property and changes the signature. Cost is a real audit of every `append()` call site in the renderer.
- **`packages/ui`'s 22 components — decide their target, do not port them twice**
  - *why:* They are DOM-only today (`grep react-native` → zero hits) and consumed only by the two Electron apps. They are 100% reusable if the POS goes browser and 0% reusable if it goes React Native. The repo currently hedges by shipping an RN app that uses none of them.
  - *shape:* Pick one. If browser: keep all 22 unchanged and delete `apps/pos-rn` from the plan. If RN: the components are rewritten against RN primitives and only the tokens cross — budget ~4,100 LOC of rewrite plus the 3,501 LOC of happy-dom tests that go with them.

### Delete

- **`apps/owner`, `apps/platform-admin`, `apps/pos-rn`, `apps/rider`, `apps/storefront`, `apps/waiter`**
  - *why:* Six scaffold stubs, 2 LOC each (`export {};`), 12 LOC total. They cost nothing to keep but they actively mislead: `plans/` and `specs/18 §2` read as a covering set of apps, AGENTS.md counts them in the app inventory, and this audit's own brief listed them alongside real apps. Under SaaS at least three of them (owner, storefront, platform-admin) are web pages, not Expo apps, so the scaffolds encode the wrong runtime decision. Delete the packages; keep the specs.
- **`ops/startup/restos-counter.bat`, `restos-kitchen.bat`, `ops/env/*.env.example`**
  - *why:* A 200-line Windows batch restart loop that loads plaintext staff PINs and hand-copied UUIDs, gates on five env vars whose absence is silent, and rebuilds from a git checkout at every boot. Every failure mode it painstakingly documents is a symptom of on-prem-from-source deployment and disappears under SaaS. Keep the *reasoning* in the runbook (auto-logon, BIOS restore-on-AC, why refusals must not wait for a keypress — that last one is a real product insight for unattended edge devices); delete the artifacts.
- **`apps/pos-electron/src/main/file-printer.ts` and `RESTOS_PRINT_TO_FILE`**
  - *why:* 191 LOC of PDF simulator whose own docs say it is "not evidence" about any printer, that must never be set in service because it replaces the transport whose alarm is the only honest signal that no printer is attached, and whose first rendered document produced an unresolved layout finding nobody can settle without hardware. Under SaaS the same job is a preview endpoint in the cloud rendering `simulate()` output — which already lives in `packages/escpos/src/simulate.ts` and is the one interpretation.
- **The duplicated layout gate in `apps/pass-kds/src/layout-gate/` (907 LOC)**
  - *why:* A second copy of a rail that already exists at 3,573 LOC in `apps/pos-electron`. Two walks over one property is exactly the defect `packages/escpos/CLAUDE.md` names about `simulate.ts` ("two walks diverge, and then a document looks right in the snapshot suite and wrong in the app"). Whatever the SaaS gate becomes, it is one implementation parameterised by surface, not one per app.
- **`packages/escpos`'s `cols_font_b` capability field and the Font B branch of `capability.ts`**
  - *why:* Measured: zero production readers, and the encoder physically cannot emit Font B (no `ESC M` in `encoder.ts`, one face in `simulate.ts`, `ESC M` not in K-2's oracle allowlist). It is a declared capability that nothing can use and that a future session will mistake for a working option — the dormant form of this repo's own named recurring defect. `DEC-HW-001` (1) already ruled Font B out on legibility. Delete the field or mark it unreachable-by-design with the ruling cited.

### Open questions

- Does commandment 4 survive SaaS? `00 §5.1` ("no in-branch feature may require WAN") and `01-F2` (durable local persist before UI ack) are the reason there is an Electron app at all. If they hold, the SaaS client is a PWA with an OPFS/Worker store plus an on-prem edge agent — not "open a browser and log in", and the founder should know that before the rewrite is scoped. If they are relaxed to "degrade to read-only when offline", roughly 12,000 LOC of device store, mesh, outbox and hub-election become unnecessary at the client. This is the single highest-leverage ruling in this dimension.
- Which runtime is the POS? Browser (keeps all 22 `packages/ui` components, 5,363 LOC of renderer, and the whole happy-dom suite; loses printing, LAN hub and physical PPI), or React Native (matches `specs/00:15` and the PKR-25k Android tablet in `00 §4`, keeps native printing via Expo Modules, but rewrites ~4,100 LOC of components and 3,501 LOC of their tests, and `pos-rn` is currently 2 lines). Do not build both.
- Does the product still print? If yes, someone must own the last hop, and the only options are a per-branch installed agent (contradicts "just a browser"), network-only printers (excludes the USB installed base the corpus itself names), or a cloud relay over a tunnel. If no — a screen-only kitchen — then `apps/pass-kds` moves from optional to load-bearing, `packages/escpos`'s 16,120 LOC becomes optional, and `DEC-HW-001`'s open sub-question (2) about a T0 tier below T1 must finally be ruled.
- Does the physical-millimetre design language survive? `27-F8` targets are specified in millimetres of glass and `27-F68` makes a dp a physical size; both require panel PPI, which only the OS can supply. In a browser the honest answer is `source: "assumed"` on every device, which `panel-density.ts:53` documents as rendering every target at ~45% size with nothing looking broken. Options: keep mm and require the agent, restate targets in CSS px and accept the loss on tablets, or ask the operator once at setup. Do not let it default silently to "assumed".
- Does `apps/manager` (Expo RN, 3,746 LOC) stay an app, or become a page in the back office? `05-F28` says a cloud web console cannot legally emit any doc-05 event because `01-F62` requires a device to stamp a branch-scoped envelope — so the answer is currently "app", but it rests on an event-model rule, not a product rule. Under SaaS that rule is worth re-examining, and until it is, the manager app is a read-only surface with no PIN session and no writer.

### Adversarial verdicts

- **CONFIRMED** — There is no packaged artifact. The product is not installed — it is a git checkout that rebuilds from source at every boot. No installer, no updater, no signing.
  - Re-ran the search with -a across all json/yml/yaml outside node_modules: zero hits for electron-builder/electron-updater/nsis/squirrel. No electron-builder*/forge.config* file exists anywhere in the tree. apps/pos-electron/package.json:11 is exactly `"start": "electron-vite build && electron out/main/index.js"`, and its devDependencies list electron/electron-vite/@electron/rebuild with no packager. specs/18-engineering-handbook.md:182 does list `electron-builder` on the Electron allowlist, uninstalled. Line-number nits only, substance untouched: RESTOS_REPO=C:\restos is ops/startup/restos-counter.bat:29 (not :28), `call pnpm -C apps\pos-electron start` is :169 (not :189), the manual `pnpm rebuild:native` warning is :13 (not :12). ops/startup/restos-kitchen.bat:117 does the same for the pass, so the checkout-as-install shape is both apps, not one.
- **CONFIRMED** — PRINTING: there is no USB or serial driver anywhere in the repo. All three shipped 'transports' reduce to a TCP socket or a filesystem write. `serialport`, `usb` and `ble-plx` are absent from every package.json.
  - grep -a for "serialport"/"usb"/ble-plx across every package.json in apps/, packages/, services/ returns zero. printer-link.ts:71-72 imports exactly `writeFile` from node:fs/promises and `createConnection` from node:net and nothing else native. The quoted admissions are real, one line off: "**No `serialport` and no `usb` dependency** — both are on `18 §14`'s allowlist and neither is installed" is at :427 (claim said :428); "`device://` and `windows://` are WRITE-ONLY, so `03-F40` is unreachable on them" is at :62 as claimed, restated at :583 in the shipped boot line, and ops/env/counter.env.example:110-113 repeats it to the operator. One framing caveat that does not change the verdict: USB printing IS reachable on both platforms through the OS (a shared queue / a character device), so the defect is precisely the missing back-channel — paper-out reads as printed — not an inability to print over USB. specs/03:230 (b) states the same consequence in the corpus.
- **CONFIRMED** — K-8 (a physical printer pass) has NEVER been done — verified in 12 places across shipped code, specs and ops docs.
  - Checked eight of the twelve citations verbatim and all eight say what is claimed at the cited line: printer-link.ts:14 "IT DOES NOT CLOSE K-8. NO PRINTER HAS BEEN ATTACHED TO ANY OF THIS."; packages/escpos/src/index.ts:8 "K-8 is the physical pass — NO PRINTER HAS EVER BEEN ATTACHED"; plans/wave-1/kot-printing.md:175 "| **K-8** | **Physical verification on real hardware — OWED, not done** |"; ops/README.md:652 "No print head has ever received a byte from this code (K-8)"; receipt-printing.test.ts:32 "NOT EVIDENCE FOR: any physical printer (K-8 is owed in full…)"; specs/03:193, :219, :230 each carry a K-8 clause; ops/env/counter.env.example:117 "NO PRINTER HAS EVER BEEN TESTED AGAINST THIS CODE (K-8)". Independent corroboration the finding did not cite: apps/pos-electron/src/main/job-store.ts:30 says the same of the durable spool ("every 'power cut' in the tests is close()"). Nothing contradicts it anywhere I looked.
- **AMENDED** — LOCAL SQLITE is already portable: `better-sqlite3` is constructed in exactly ONE file behind a 6-method `StorageAdapter` port, and a second driver (RN op-sqlite) already ships.
  - The port and the second driver are real — storage.ts declares prepare/exec/pragma/transaction/close plus statement run/get/all (five adapter methods, not six), property 1 at :29-31 reads exactly as quoted, storage-op-sqlite.ts is 207 LOC behind rn.ts. But `exactly ONE file` drops a scope the source itself keeps: storage-node.ts:9 asserts it is the one module *in the package*, and a symbol-precise grep for `from "better-sqlite3"` in non-test shipping code returns TWO hits — storage-node.ts:18 and apps/pos-electron/src/main/job-store.ts:35, which constructs `new Database` for 03-F4's print spool with no adapter, no port and its own SCHEMA string. Corrected claim: SQLite is behind a 5-method port for the ledger only; a browser or RN target has two porting jobs, and the second one (the durable print spool that K-6 mutation-proved is load-bearing) has no port at all. The synchronous-durability argument stands as quoted and applies to both.
- **AMENDED** — AUTHORIZATION is enforced on the customer's own till; commandment 8's "server-side" is satisfied by an Electron main process on premises, which under multi-tenant SaaS is a client.
  - The substance holds and I confirmed it beyond the citation: main/authorize.ts:29 says "the MAIN process is what \"server-side\" means" verbatim, and the sync-gateway does not re-check the permission matrix on ingest — grep for can(/canPayOut across services/sync-gateway/src outside tests returns only three comment mentions, and gateway.ts:784-786 checks org/branch/device identity only. So a person's write authority is decided on the customer's machine and never re-decided in the cloud. Two citations are wrong. (a) The repeated sentence is at authorize.ts:671, not index.ts:671 — index.ts:671 is the LAN mesh notifyChanged funnel; the sentence appears twice in ONE file. (b) "the two files importing the matrix" is stale: a comment-stripped, multi-line-safe import scan finds THREE shipping importers of matrix symbols — apps/pos-electron/src/main/authorize.ts (can, canPayOut, reportScope, ROLES), services/api/src/trpc.ts (can) and services/api/src/summary-router.ts (reportScope). That is one on-device and two in cloud, which strengthens rather than weakens the finding.
- **CONFIRMED** — The physical-millimetre design language requires shelling out to PowerShell WMI / xrandr for the panel's real size; the fallback renders every touch target at ~45% on a 10.1" tablet with nothing looking broken.
  - index.ts:1 is `import { execFileSync } from "node:child_process"`; :298-315 is screen.getPrimaryDisplay() feeding resolvePanelDensity with physicalWidthMm from measurePhysicalWidthMm(process.platform, execFileSync). panel-density.ts:115-121 is the `Get-CimInstance -Namespace root\wmi -ClassName WmiMonitorBasicDisplayParams` probe, :125-131 the `xrandr --query` probe, :133 returns null for darwin and everything else. The :17 quote ("and no physical size") and the :53 quote (~45% of ergonomic size, "nothing on the screen looks wrong") are verbatim and the header itself titles the section "THE FALLBACK IS A DEFECT". One qualifier worth carrying: index.ts:306 also reads a `RESTOS_PANEL_PPI` env override, so a browser (or any platform with no probe) is not without a path — it is without an AUTOMATIC one, and the 45% failure is what happens when nobody sets it.
- **CONFIRMED** — packages/ui's 22 components are an ELECTRON-TILL library, not a shared design system. The RN app imports zero components; the web app imports exactly one.
  - Counted independently: 22 non-story/non-test .tsx files under packages/ui/src/components and components/index.ts is 22 lines, all exports (AGENTS.md's "18" is indeed stale). grep -a for react-native across packages/ui/src returns zero. Consumer scan of `from "@restos/ui` across apps/ and services/: apps/manager takes tokens only (App.tsx:26, probe.ts:20); apps/backoffice takes tokens/fonts plus exactly one component, MoneyValue at owner-summary.tsx:52; every other component import is apps/pos-electron or apps/pass-kds, both Electron. apps/manager/src/App.tsx:12-13 corroborates from the other side in the repo's own words: "`packages/ui` ships no RN components … there is no compliant way to put a pixel on an Android screen in this repo today." Only the decorative primitive counts differ by scope — non-story component sources give 31 div / 39 span / 13 button / 2 input; including stories, 54/41/13/5. Neither matches the quoted 51/41/13/5, and neither changes the conclusion.
- **AMENDED** — THE POS/ANDROID GAP IS TOTAL AND SELF-CONTRADICTORY — the Electron layout gate ships `tablet-10.1` as ships:true, a panel class that only exists on Android tablets, for an app that cannot run on Android.
  - The gap half is exact: apps/pos-rn/src/index.ts is `// Scaffold stub…` + `export {};` (2 lines) and its package.json has no expo/react-native dependency at all; specs/00:15 row 02 reads "Windows (Electron), Android (RN) | 1"; specs/18:31 "pos-rn/ # Android counter (Expo)"; specs/00:141 names the PKR ~25k Android tablet as reference hardware; layout-gate/main.ts:181 is the tablet-10.1 ships:true row and :160-166 is the quoted comment. The self-contradiction half does not survive its own file. `tablet-10.1` is a GEOMETRY (1366×768 on 10.1" glass), not an Android-only class: the very next row, main.ts:198 `netbook-1024`, is the same 10.1" physical surface, also ships:true, and :183 describes it as the bring-your-own **netbook** — a Windows machine Electron runs on; the file's own summary table at :82 labels tablet-10.1 the "`27 §1a` waiter tablet". So the gate is measuring hardware the Electron app can actually meet, and no contradiction is demonstrated. Corrected claim: module 02's Android/RN half is entirely unbuilt (2 LOC) against a spec that names it at wave 1 — a real gap, major rather than blocker — but the layout gate's 10.1" rows are not evidence of it.
- **AMENDED** — `apps/manager` is the only real RN app and it is read-only by construction: no PIN session, identity from the BUILD (`EXPO_PUBLIC_*`), single-tenant baked into a binary.
  - Everything downstream of the first clause checks out: package.json is expo 54.0.36 / react-native 0.81.5 / @restos/sync-client; branch.ts:63 closes a real runtime `from "@restos/sync-client/rn"` import (home.ts:41 is `import type`, erased, so the live door is branch.ts alone); branch.ts:31 "Identity and the device credential come from EXPO_PUBLIC_*, i.e. from the BUILD", :79-83 declares the five EXPO_PUBLIC_RESTOS_* keys and :144-146 reads them; branch.ts:47 "**No PIN session.** 01-F26's unlock is not wired, so this device READS the branch and cannot author"; rn.ts:28 "**Nothing in this repository executes `open()`**". "The only real RN app" is refuted by the app's own first line — App.tsx:1 "⚠ THIS IS A FEASIBILITY PROBE, NOT THE MANAGER CONSOLE, AND IT MUST NOT BECOME ONE" — and the screen renders four probe rows plus an alarm list, declaring at :151 "05-F29 feasibility probes, not product surfaces". Corrected claim: there is no shipped RN product surface at all; apps/manager is a diagnostics probe that is read-only, and pos-rn/waiter are stubs. That is worse than the finding says, not better.
- **AMENDED** — Per-restaurant configuration is a hand-edited env file per physical machine with plaintext staff PINs, hand-copied UUIDs that nothing cross-checks, and three immutable hardcoded users.
  - Every quotation is verbatim at the cited place: counter.env.example:2 "One file per PHYSICAL machine", :15 the section header "identity — the values nothing anywhere cross-checks", :17-18 the BOOTSTRAP_ORG_ID clause ending "No error.", :146-150 "THIS IS A DEV SEED BEING USED IN PRODUCTION, KNOWINGLY, BECAUSE THERE IS NO OTHER OPTION … you cannot add, rename or remove them", :178-180 the three RESTOS_DEV_PIN* keys; dev-staff.ts:90-97 is the three-row DEV_STAFF const with UUIDs …0004/0005/0006 and roles cashier/cashier/branch_manager. The one clause needing amendment is "nothing cross-checks": services/sync-gateway/src/gateway.ts:783-789 quarantines every pushed envelope as `org_mismatch`/`branch_mismatch` when it disagrees with the registry-derived session identity, so a wrong RESTOS_ORG_ID/RESTOS_BRANCH_ID IS caught on the write path (loudly, at push). What genuinely nothing cross-checks is the READ path — services/api's BOOTSTRAP_ORG_ID and ENABLED_BRANCHES against the device's ids — which is exactly the silent no-menu failure the env comment describes. Substance and blocker severity stand.

## doc-corpus

**Verdict.** The founder's "deviated" verdict is TRUE OF THE CODE AND LARGELY FALSE OF THE PROSE, and that inversion should drive the rewrite decision. Measured: "one restaurant"/"single restaurant" appears 0 times across all 28 specs + restaurant-os.md; "org" appears 500+ times; specs/00 §5.4 already says "org data isolation absolute", §7 already defines a three-layer config model whose layer 1 is "Platform admin (vendor): org provisioning, feature flags/tier enablement, own-channel take-rate %"; restaurant-os.md §7 already prices the product at PKR 8,000/branch/month with an admin-settable per-org take-rate; and specs/15 is a 28-FR multi-tenant control-plane spec covering org lifecycle, suspension, fleet health and provisioning. The deviation lives in the code: 36 distinct process env knobs, of which at least six are settings specs/00 §7 assigns to layer 2 (ENABLED_BRANCHES and ENABLED_CHANNELS read from process env in services/api/src/server.ts, plus RESTOS_STATION_ROUTES, RESTOS_HARDWARE_TIER, RESTOS_READY_SIGNAL_OWNER, RESTOS_AGING_THRESHOLDS), which pins the cloud plane to one org by construction. HEAD (8659587, today) already began the correction inside the existing system — eight new FRs (01-F68..F71, 11-F20, 15-F25..F27, 21-F15) added org/branch/device/person records, tenant isolation as a tested property, and the law that "every provisioned record has a declared step, never SQL and never an env var" — with docs:lint still clean. So the numbering system demonstrably absorbs a SaaS pivot additively. What genuinely must be redone is not the requirements but the corpus's SHAPE and its GOVERNANCE. The corpus is ~485,000 tokens (363,778 words: specs 175k, plans 184k, package guides 89k, root docs 37k). Its own size law, 23-F3, caps a doc at ~4.5k tokens and 23-F8 promises a token-count rail — but scripts/docs-lint.mjs C5 counts LINES only, so 13 documents sit over the token cap at up to 4.2× (specs/03 = 18.9k tokens in 298 "lines"; AGENTS.md = 16.7k tokens in 89 lines, one of which is 5,013 words). That is this repo's own named defect — a proxy accepted as the evidence — sitting inside its own governance rail, and it is the mechanical reason the corpus grew past readability. AGENTS.md is 83% archaeology (10,339 of 12,516 words narrate specific defects in specific files) and is measurably stale TODAY: of eight re-measurable claims re-run 2026-08-16, five are wrong, including three separate assertions that the LAN mesh is "hosted by nothing" and that "RestOS is T1-only today" when apps/pos-electron/src/main/mesh.ts and apps/pass-kds/src/main/mesh.ts have hosted it since 2026-08-13. Two genuinely SaaS-hostile items exist and both are small and surgical: design law 5's second clause ("cloud is the exhaust"), which is the root cause of a control plane that had no orgs table for months, and 15-F7's "in-branch POS billing never stops — 00 §5.1 is not a business lever", which leaves a subscription business with no way to withhold its product from a non-payer. Everything else survives. The single most valuable asset is the FR-ID system: 805 definitions, 19,803 citations across 601 of 660 TypeScript source files (91%), and ZERO dangling references in code, plans or package guides. Renumbering from scratch destroys 19,803 anchors and buys nothing — the pivot at HEAD proves extension works.

### Findings

- **The size-cap rail enforces the wrong unit. 23-F3 caps a doc at "~4.5k tokens (~350 lines)" and 23-F8 explicitly promises "per-doc token count under the 23-F3 cap" — but scripts/docs-lint.mjs C5 checks LINES ONLY. 13 documents exceed the token cap, up to 4.2×, and docs:lint reports clean. This is the corpus's own named "proxy accepted as the evidence" defect instantiated inside its own governance rail, and it is the mechanical cause of the corpus's size.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* specs/23-ai-context.md:17 (23-F3), :50 (23-F8 "per-doc token count"); scripts/docs-lint.mjs:71-77 (C5: `read(...).split("\n").length > 360`, no token count anywhere in the 219-line file). Measured words×4/3: specs/03=18,909 tok (4.2×), specs/DECISIONS.md=17,186 (3.8×), AGENTS.md=16,688 (3.7×), specs/01=16,666 (3.7×), specs/02=13,992 (3.1×), specs/27=10,810 (2.4×), README.md=9,730 (2.1×), specs/14=8,785, specs/25=7,714, specs/05=7,026, specs/15=5,129, restaurant-os.md=4,973, specs/18=4,594. `node scripts/docs-lint.mjs` → "docs-lint: clean — 28 specs, 805 FR definitions", exit 0.
- **FR-ID traceability is near-perfect and is the corpus's single most valuable asset. 805 FR/N definitions across 28 specs; 601 of 660 TS/TSX source files (91%) cite at least one FR ID; 19,803 total citation occurrences; 366 distinct FRs reached from code. ZERO dangling citations anywhere — in code, in plans/, or in the 27 per-package CLAUDE.md files — even though docs-lint C3 only walks AGENTS.md + restaurant-os.md + specs/. Renumbering from scratch destroys 19,803 anchors in 601 files.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* `grep -arlE '\b[0-9]{2}-F[0-9]+' --include=*.ts --include=*.tsx apps packages services | grep -v node_modules | wc -l` → 601; total source files 660; occurrence count 19,803; distinct cited 366 of 708 F-IDs. comm against the 805-entry definition set from `^- NN-F/N` yields 0 dangling for code, 0 for plans/, 0 for package CLAUDE.md. docs-lint C3 corpus array: scripts/docs-lint.mjs:49-52 (AGENTS.md, restaurant-os.md, specs/* only).
- **The specs are NOT written for a single restaurant. "one restaurant"/"single restaurant" = 0 hits across all 28 specs and restaurant-os.md. "technician" = 5 hits, every one in a legitimate layer-3 device-configuration context; "installer" = 2, one of which is the NSIS packager. The multi-tenant model is stated in the authority docs, not merely implied.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* specs/00-platform-overview.md:147 (§5.4 "org data isolation absolute (customer phone numbers never cross orgs)"); :165-171 (§7 layer 1 = "Platform admin (vendor): org provisioning, feature flags/tier enablement, own-channel take-rate %, rollout channels", layer 2 = Organization/back office, layer 3 = Branch/device); restaurant-os.md:127-131 (§7: "PKR 8,000 / branch / month base subscription… Own-channel take-rate up to 5% — admin-settable per restaurant"); specs/15-platform-admin.md:18-60 (28 FRs of org lifecycle, provisioning, suspension, fleet health); specs/18-engineering-handbook.md:37 (`storefront/ # Customer ordering (Next.js, multi-tenant)`). Word counts: "org"/"orgs" 500+ across specs; "the restaurant" 1–3 per doc.
- **The corpus deliberately does not specify billing, subscriptions, plans, quotas or metering-to-invoice — the revenue mechanics of the SaaS the founder says this is. This is stated verbatim as an open question, not an oversight, and topic greps confirm it: "dunning" 0 docs, "uptime" 0 docs, "usage-based" 0 docs, "provisioning automation" 0 docs.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* specs/15-platform-admin.md:182 §9.7 — "Related and deliberately NOT specified anywhere in this corpus: billing, subscriptions, plans, quotas and metering-to-invoice. 15-F5a fixes plan *shape* and 15-F24 states plainly that metering is measurement only; no FR anywhere makes a payment state gate a service, and none should be written until §9.4's invoicing handoff has an owner." 15-F5a at :32 fixes only `{ base_subscription, take_rate (0–5%), add_ons }`.
- **The one genuinely SaaS-hostile LAW in the corpus: 15-F7 forbids the product from ever being withheld for non-payment. A subscription business with no enforcement lever is a business decision the corpus has already made against the founder, twice, in normative text.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* specs/15-platform-admin.md:34 — "15-F7 Suspension: reversible; gates cloud services… — **in-branch POS billing never stops** (00 §5.1 is not a business lever)"; restated at :101 for the `suspended` lifecycle value. Upstream law: specs/00-platform-overview.md:145 §5.1 (offline-first, every in-branch function works with WAN down indefinitely) and AGENTS.md:40 commandment 4 ("A sale is never blocked — not by inventory math, sync, or approval timeouts").
- **Design law 5's SECOND clause — "cloud is the exhaust" — is the source of the deviation; its first clause (LAN-first offline operation) is not. The metaphor makes the cloud a downstream reporting sink, and that is exactly the shape the code took: no orgs table, no code path creating an org, org_id arriving at the gateway as free text. The corpus itself contradicts the metaphor in practice, since the catalog path runs cloud-DOWN (API → gateway → device).** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* restaurant-os.md:35 law 5 — "LAN-first real-time… cloud is the exhaust and cross-branch path." specs/01-kernel-sync.md 01-F68 — "measured August 2026, `org_id` arrives at the gateway as free text, no migration creates an `orgs` table, no code path anywhere creates an org." Cloud-down catalog path documented in AGENTS.md:5 ("an authored menu now travels API → gateway → device read path").
- **Six settings that specs/00 §7 assigns to layer 2 (organization / back office, i.e. per-tenant DATA) are implemented as PROCESS environment variables, which makes each running cloud process single-tenant by construction. BOOTSTRAP_ORG_ID has 30 occurrences; 36 distinct env knobs exist.** [BLOCKER]
  - *evidence:* services/api/src/server.ts reads ENABLED_BRANCHES and ENABLED_CHANNELS from process env (00 §7 layer 2: "channels enabled"); apps/pos-electron/src/main/station-routing.ts reads RESTOS_STATION_ROUTES (00 §7 layer 2: "station fulfilment routes"); apps/pos-electron/src/main/hardware-tier.ts reads RESTOS_HARDWARE_TIER (layer 2: "hardware tier T1/T2/T3"); apps/pass-kds/src/main/ready-signal.ts reads RESTOS_READY_SIGNAL_OWNER (layer 2: "signal ownership", also restaurant-os.md:31 design law 3); packages/device-config/src/aging.ts reads RESTOS_AGING_THRESHOLDS (layer 2: "alert thresholds"). Occurrence counts by grep -arhoE over apps/services/packages: BOOTSTRAP_ORG_ID 30, ENABLED_BRANCHES 21, ENABLED_CHANNELS 12; 36 distinct RESTOS_/BOOTSTRAP_/ENABLED_/NEXT_PUBLIC_ names.
- **AGENTS.md is 83% codebase archaeology by word count. Seven paragraphs narrate the status of specific defects in specific files; the durable content is ~2,177 words. The file also defeats its own 120-line cap by writing 5,013-word lines.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* AGENTS.md: 89 lines, 12,516 words, 80,774 bytes. Archaeology paragraphs L5 (5,013 words), L7 (540), L8 (674), L10 (562), L12 (986), L14 (840), L16 (1,724) = 10,339 words = 82.6%. Durable: L18–L33 traps + three standing laws (1,273 w), L37–L46 commandments (248 w), L50–L80 routing table (~330 w), L84–L89 working rules (~195 w). Longest line 5,013 words; 7 lines over 500 words. 34 ⚠ markers, 29 self-correction admissions ("this line said", "was WRONG", "stopped being true").
- **AGENTS.md is measurably stale RIGHT NOW, and its worst error is a load-bearing product claim. Of eight re-measurable assertions re-run 2026-08-16, five are wrong. It asserts three times that the LAN mesh is "hosted by nothing" and that "RestOS is T1-only today" — both false since 2026-08-13.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* CORRECT: 26 turbo test tasks (26 package.json files with a "test" script); specs/27 has 77 FRs; docs-lint clean. STALE: (1) AGENTS.md claims "29 @unreached-by-design" — actual 17; (2) claims "34 @unreached-owed across 19 files" — actual 39 across 22 files; (3) claims PERMISSION_ACTIONS = 23 — packages/domain/src/permissions.ts:109-153 holds 24; (4) claims packages/ui = 18 components by its own line-count convention — packages/ui/src/components/index.ts is 22 lines; (5) claims `pnpm verify` is "six steps in that order" — package.json shows SEVEN (strings:check joined at HEAD), and that sentence is itself the correction of an earlier stale verify list. WORST: AGENTS.md contains "hosted by nothing" ×2, "NO HOST RUNS THE LAN MESH YET" ×2, "RestOS is T1-only today" ×1 — while apps/pos-electron/src/main/mesh.ts and apps/pass-kds/src/main/mesh.ts exist (both mtime 2026-08-13) and packages/sync-client/src/mesh-session.ts:17-21 reads "HOSTED since August 2026 (01-F12/F13/F15)… the oldest open item in the product — is deleted with that work."
- **KERNEL.md is wholly false and actively misleading to a new reader — it describes a pre-merge branch state and asserts nothing outside the kernel is built.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* KERNEL.md:5 — "Everything here is on branch `feat/t-01-16-transport` (not yet merged to `main`). Nothing else in `packages/`/`services/` is built — this is the kernel only." Against: 26 packages carry a test script, 11 apps and 8 services exist with CLAUDE.md guides, and HEAD is 8659587 on main.
- **Build coverage per spec doc, measured from FR citations in code — the corpus is four documents deep and twenty-four documents wide. 342 of 708 F-IDs (48%) are never cited from any TypeScript file.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* Per doc, defined→cited: 01 71→70 (98%), 03 59→55 (93%), 02 57→52 (91%), 27 77→70 (90%), 05 30→19 (63%), 14 38→24 (63%), 15 28→13 (46%), 12 30→12 (40%), 21 15→6 (40%), 08 19→7 (36%), 06 30→9 (30%), 24 25→7 (28%), 09 21→4 (19%), 16 26→5 (19%), 13 33→6 (18%), 04 20→2 (10%), 11 20→2 (10%), 23 15→1 (6%), 17 20→1 (5%), 07 25→1 (4%), 10 27→0 (0%), 22 22→0 (0%).
- **specs/22 — the entire SaaS operating obligation set (RPO/RTO, PITR, retention matrix, PII erasure over an append-only ledger, legal hold, owner export, vendor exit, WABA/LLM/Postgres-vendor failure modes) — is 22 well-formed FRs with ZERO code citations. Alongside it, specs/10 (inventory) is also 0%. These are the two largest fully-unbuilt bodies of requirement in the corpus.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* specs/22-operations-recovery.md 22-F1..22-F22 (RPO ≤5 min, PITR ≥30 days, quarterly restore drill 22-F8, retention matrix 22-F13, PII erasure 22-F14, legal hold 22-F15, org export 22-F16, vendor exit 22-F17). `comm -12` of defined vs code-cited yields 0 for prefix `22-F`. Same for `10-F`.
- **The specs have silently become part-changelog. 241 retro-narrative markers ("measured August 2026", "Amended August 2026", "this said", "was WRONG") sit inside normative documents, and their density correlates ~9× with implementation contact — the built docs absorbed AGENTS.md's disease.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* Markers per 1,000 words: specs/01 43/12,500 = 3.4 (98% built); specs/27 26/8,108 = 3.2 (90% built); specs/02 28/10,494 = 2.7 (91%); specs/03 34/14,182 = 2.4 (93%). Versus specs/22 1/2,734 = 0.37 (0% built); specs/17 1/2,206 = 0.45 (5%); specs/10 2/2,561 = 0.78 (0%). Total across specs/: 241.
- **DECISIONS.md's table format has structurally broken down. 47 decisions live in 56 lines and 12,890 words; single table cells run past 2,600 characters and the Status column has been repurposed to hold rulings.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* specs/DECISIONS.md — 47 unique DEC-* IDs, 56 lines, 12,890 words (~17,186 tokens, 3.8× the 23-F3 cap). Status distribution: 30 accepted/RATIFIED/RULED, 13 proposed, 1 superseded (DEC-SYNC-004 → DEC-SYNC-009), 1 blocked (DEC-CHAN-001, eval gate), 1 closed by founder risk acceptance (DEC-TAX-001). DEC-MONEY-009's Status cell contains "**RULED (founder, August 2026): REFUSE the second settlement at the till.** Observed on a running system, not derived"; DEC-ARCH-001's row exceeds 2,600 characters.
- **plans/ violates its own governance rule and is 57% orphaned. plans/README.md declares plans are "deleted after their wave"; all 17 wave-0 task files survive. 28 of 49 plan files are referenced from nowhere in AGENTS.md, specs/, or any package CLAUDE.md — 9,228 lines and ~184k tokens in total, larger than the entire specs/ corpus.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* plans/README.md:3 — "living task lists produced by planning sessions, reviewed like code, **deleted after their wave** (specs are permanent… plans are working memory)". Reference count by basename grep across AGENTS.md + specs/*.md + all package CLAUDE.md: 21 files referenced ≥1, 28 files referenced 0 — including all of plans/wave-0/t-01-*.md (10 files), plans/wave-0/kernel-tasks.md (496 lines), plans/wave-0/recorded-rulings.md, all 7 plans/wave-1/research/*.md, and plans/wave-1/decorative-surface-audit.md (584 lines). plans/ totals 9,228 lines / 137,829 words.
- **The SaaS pivot has ALREADY been executed once inside the existing numbering system, additively, with the rail staying green. This is direct evidence that renumbering is unnecessary and that the corpus's grammar can carry the change.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* HEAD = 8659587 "Multi-tenant SaaS foundations, and the back office written for the owner" (2026-08-16). Eight FRs added: 01-F68 (org is a first-class named record, registry is a directory never a gate on ingest), 01-F69 (branch is a named record under exactly one org), 01-F70 (device carries a required human name), 01-F71 (tenant isolation is a tested property with named enforcement points), 11-F20 (a person is a named record), 15-F25 (org lifecycle is active⇄suspended, no third state), 15-F26 (provisioning creates the first named owner; no self-service signup), 15-F27 (every provisioned record is created by an invokable declared step — never hand-written SQL and never an environment variable), 21-F15 (naming law: no surface renders a machine identifier). `node scripts/docs-lint.mjs` → clean, 805 definitions. Migrations 0010/0011 add kernel.orgs, kernel.branches, kernel.users.
- **01-F62 plus 05-F28 form a real structural constraint the SaaS control plane will hit: the event envelope has exactly two scopes, the org-scoped set is fixed, and a branch-scoped envelope must be stamped by a DEVICE — so a cloud-plane web surface cannot legally emit most audited facts. Any SaaS admin console that must write an audited action runs into this wall on day one.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* specs/01-kernel-sync.md 01-F62 — "The `01 §4` catalog holds TWO SCOPES, and an org-scoped event carries no branch fields… `EventEnvelope` requires `branch_id`, `branch_created_at` and `time_basis` because 01-F43..01-F46 make time branch-consensus and stamped at append by an originating **device**." specs/05-manager-console.md 05-F28 — "a cloud-plane surface cannot legally emit any event this module owns (measured August 2026)… the web option cannot deliver 05-F6 at all." Corroborated by AGENTS.md's note that `revoke-device` "deliberately emits no event — a shell on the service host has no authenticated user."
- **README.md is not a product front door and is not covered by any rail. At 711 lines it is the corpus's longest file and the only one failing the 360-line spec cap, yet docs-lint C5 only walks specs/. Its content is a local four-process laptop runbook.** [MINOR]
  - *evidence:* README.md: 711 lines, 7,298 words (~9,730 tokens). scripts/docs-lint.mjs:71-74 iterates `specFiles` only; README.md, AGENTS.md-adjacent root docs and plans/ are outside C5 except the AGENTS.md 120-line check at :78. Content headings: "Quickstart", "1. install", "2. Postgres + schema", "2b. the tenant", "3. secrets", "4. sync gateway (port 8080)", "5. the owner, then the API (port 3001)", "6. back office (port 3000)", "7a. native addon".
- **Per-package CLAUDE.md files have replicated AGENTS.md's failure mode at package scope. 66,911 words (~89k tokens) across 27 files, of which one file is larger than any spec and 11 are sub-100-word stubs — a bimodal distribution with no middle.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* apps/pos-electron/CLAUDE.md = 26,078 words (~34.8k tokens, larger than specs/03 at 18.9k); services/sync-gateway 9,933; services/api 7,013; apps/backoffice 5,901; apps/pass-kds 4,298. 11 of 27 under 100 words (apps/platform-admin 6 lines/48 words, apps/storefront 6 lines/47 words). No FR-ID references in these files dangle (0 dangling), so they are accurate — just unbounded.
- **A single routine task loads ~47,000 tokens of prose before a line of code is read, which is why sessions read past staleness. AGENTS.md's own working rule ("load only the routed docs, a task needs ~2–4 specs") does not bound the cost because the docs are 3–4× their stated cap.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* AGENTS.md 16,688 tok (mandatory, loaded via CLAUDE.md:3 `@AGENTS.md`) + specs/01 16,666 + specs/02 13,992 = 47,346 tokens for a POS/kernel task. Add apps/pos-electron/CLAUDE.md (34,800) for the counter app = 82,146. Corpus total 363,778 words ≈ 485,037 tokens.

### Keep

- **The FR-ID addressing system and scripts/docs-lint.mjs rules C1–C4 and C6 (routing completeness, unique prefix-matched definitions, universal reference resolution, byte-identical authority blocks, event-catalog absorption)**
  - *why:* It is the only mechanism in the entire repo that binds 200k LOC to intent, and it works: 19,803 citations, 0 dangling, across code the rail does not even walk. It is what makes commandments 2 and 9 mechanically true rather than aspirational — an invented policy fails because its ID resolves to nothing. Nothing else in the corpus has this property and nothing cheap replaces it.
  - *size:* scripts/docs-lint.mjs 219 lines; 805 FR/N definitions across 28 specs; 601 of 660 TS/TSX files (91%) cite ≥1 FR; 19,803 occurrences; 366 distinct FRs reached
- **specs/00-platform-overview.md §7 — the three-layer configuration model (platform admin / organization / branch-device)**
  - *why:* This is already the correct SaaS tenancy model and it is stated normatively. It says layer 1 is vendor-owned org provisioning, feature flags and take-rate; layer 2 is per-org back-office data; layer 3 is per-device. The code violates it in six measured places; the prose does not need one word changed. Rewriting it would be rewriting the answer.
  - *size:* specs/00-platform-overview.md:163-171, 9 lines
- **specs/01-kernel-sync.md — the event catalog, envelope, and the three standing laws (per-fold merge semantics / branch-consensus time / integer-paisa money)**
  - *why:* 71 FRs at 98% code coverage, the highest in the corpus, and the laws are tested by bijective id-relabel and clock-injection invariance rather than asserted. The money law in particular (BigInt accumulation, basis points, splitPaisa, overflow contributes zero and raises money_overflow) is hard-won and multi-tenancy does not touch it. Losing this is losing the product.
  - *size:* specs/01-kernel-sync.md 233 lines, 12,500 words, 71 FRs, 70 cited from code
- **specs/27-design-language.md and specs/21-ux-system.md**
  - *why:* 77 FRs at 90% coverage, and unusually the claims are hardware-measured rather than argued — 27-F68's dp-as-physical-size, 27-F26's bundled face with load() assertions, the eleven-panel sweep spanning 202-782 mm of glass. Tenancy is invisible to all of it. specs/21 adds the stable-layout and icon+number laws that carry the low-literacy requirement.
  - *size:* specs/27 286 lines/8,108 words/77 FRs (70 cited); specs/21 97 lines/15 FRs
- **specs/15-platform-admin.md — the vendor control-plane spec**
  - *why:* This is already a multi-tenant SaaS control plane: org provisioning (15-F4), plan and feature-flag enablement (15-F5/F5a), per-org take-rate (15-F6), reversible suspension (15-F7), fleet health (15-F11), and the new 15-F25/F26/F27. Only 46% is built. It is the document the rewrite should EXECUTE, not replace — and its §9 open questions are the founder's actual agenda.
  - *size:* specs/15-platform-admin.md 182 lines, 3,847 words, 28 FRs (13 cited from code)
- **specs/22-operations-recovery.md — backup/DR, retention, PII erasure, legal hold, export, vendor exit**
  - *why:* 22 well-formed FRs covering every SaaS operating obligation the product will be judged on (RPO ≤5 min, PITR ≥30 days, quarterly restore drill, retention matrix, crypto-shredding over an append-only ledger, owner-triggered full export, documented vendor exit). It is 0% built, which means it is a to-do list, not dead weight. Rewriting it from scratch would reproduce it.
  - *size:* specs/22-operations-recovery.md 124 lines, 2,734 words, 22 FRs, 0 cited
- **specs/18 §14 dependency allowlist + §15 addition process; specs/20 testing taxonomy; specs/24 build-task loop (§3 separate test-author rule, §3b craft rules)**
  - *why:* These are the engineering standards that produced 4,000+ tests and the seams/layout/tokens/strings rails. The separate-test-author rule and the mutation requirement are the reason the codebase's defects are findable at all. They are stack-shaped, not tenancy-shaped, and survive verbatim.
  - *size:* specs/18 199 lines/3,446 w; specs/20 113 lines/2,003 w; specs/24 104 lines/2,766 w
- **specs/26-merge-semantics.md + specs/19-sync-engine-decision.md + DEC-PERF-001**
  - *why:* The convergence-without-total-order design and the recorded reasoning for building a custom sync engine. This is the technical moat and the hardest thinking in the corpus. SYNC-ORDERING-PROBLEM.md is its superseded draft and can go; these two are the settled answer.
  - *size:* specs/26 137 lines/2,469 w; specs/19 66 lines/1,444 w
- **The transferable lessons buried in AGENTS.md's 83% archaeology — extract before deleting**
  - *why:* These are genuinely durable and were each paid for with real time. In priority order: (1) A MENTION IS NOT AN IMPORT — strip comments and match the import, or say you counted mentions. (2) ALWAYS `grep -a`; under a C locale a UTF-8 source file prints "Binary file X matches" instead of its matching lines, and the failure is a QUIETER answer, not an error. (3) A REPORTED EXIT CODE IS NOT EVIDENCE — `cmd > log; echo exit=$?` launders a SIGKILL, and the background-task notification path does the same; write a REAL_EXIT marker INSIDE the log. (4) MUTATE THE SEAM, NOT THE LOGIC — delete the call site and see whether anything reddens; a durability suite alone blesses a subsystem nothing calls, and a seam test alone blesses a decorative object. You need both. (5) READING A SUITE DOES NOT DETECT A VACUOUS TEST — only mutation does; build a plausible implementation out-of-tree, take the suite green, break the one thing each assertion claims to own, and use a one-branch CONTROL or your kill count proves nothing. (6) A TEST THAT STAYS RED UNDER A CORRECT IMPLEMENTATION is as damaging as a vacuous one. (7) DATE EVERY MEASUREMENT AND RE-RUN IT BEFORE QUOTING IT — a claim stated as a measurement reads as durable even when its shelf life was minutes. (8) A COMMENT PROMISING A PROTECTION THAT DOES NOT EXIST IS WORSE THAN NO COMMENT — it retires the assertion the next reader would have written; when you close a defect, state the class you closed and name the neighbouring case you did not. (9) A STALE CLAIM PROPAGATES FASTEST THROUGH THE PERSON FIXING A DIFFERENT STALE CLAIM IN THE SAME PARAGRAPH. (10) MUTATION-TEST SECURITY CONSTANTS OUT-OF-TREE — an agent killed between weaken and revert leaves live weakened crypto with every test green. (11) WHEN A RULING LANDS, GREP THE SUITES THAT ENCODE THE OLD RULE THE SAME DAY — a green test defended an overruled rule for three weeks. (12) NEVER `git add -A` while parallel agents share a worktree. (13) A single RED run is exactly as untrustworthy as a single green one; re-run in isolation before reporting a regression. (14) THE RECURRING DEFECT ITSELF: a correct subsystem with no seam to the product — fifteen instances, all with green suites; grep for the production caller before calling anything done.
  - *size:* ~2,177 durable words of AGENTS.md's 12,516 (17%); the list above compresses to roughly 900 words
- **plans/wave-1/running-the-stack.md and plans/wave-1/wave-1-scope-reconciliation.md**
  - *why:* The only two plan documents with real ongoing value. running-the-stack.md (758 lines, 6 inbound references) is the four-process-from-clean-checkout runbook and names what is still seeded versus owed — under SaaS it becomes the seed of a deployment doc. wave-1-scope-reconciliation.md is the record of how a wave got tracked from derived documents instead of re-derived from the authority, which is the same root cause as the current pivot.
  - *size:* 758 + 186 = 944 lines of the 9,228 in plans/
- **The eight SaaS FRs landed at HEAD (01-F68..F71, 11-F20, 15-F25..F27, 21-F15) and the two design rulings in that commit message**
  - *why:* They are the pivot already done correctly. Two rulings deserve promotion into the corpus proper because they are not recorded in DECISIONS.md: (a) NO LEDGER TABLE MAY CARRY A FOREIGN KEY TO THE ORG REGISTRY — events already exist under org ids no registry row names, and a constraint would refuse ingest for exactly those orgs, refusing a sale a till has already rung (01-F17); an org with events and no record is UNNAMED, not invalid. (b) NO "closed" TENANT STATE — indistinguishable in every enforcement path from suspension plus device revocation; closure is a procedure. Both are exactly the kind of reasoning that gets re-derived wrongly if lost.
  - *size:* 8 FR definitions across 4 spec files; migrations 0010/0011; 4 declared commands (create-org, create-branch, create-owner, list-tenancy)

### Redo

- **THE SHAPE OF THE NEW CORPUS — concrete proposal**
