  - *why:* The current corpus is 485k tokens across ~90 documents in five overlapping systems (specs/, plans/, AGENTS.md, per-package CLAUDE.md, root docs) with no cost bound, and a single task loads 47k–82k tokens of prose. The requirements are mostly right; the packaging is what failed. The rewrite is a RE-SHELVING plus two law amendments, not a re-authoring.
  - *shape:* THREE TIERS, HARD TOKEN BUDGETS, ~32 DOCUMENTS.

TIER 0 — THE ROUTER (one file, hard cap 2,000 tokens, CI-enforced on TOKENS). Replaces AGENTS.md. Contains only: the ten commandments as one-liners each with its FR pointer; the three standing laws as one-liners with pointers; the routing table; the working rules; the authority order. It explains WHERE truth lives and never WHAT the truth is — 23-F2's own rule, finally enforced. Everything currently in AGENTS.md's seven archaeology paragraphs moves out or dies.

TIER 0.5 — LESSONS.md (hard cap 3,000 tokens, no status claims permitted, CI-banned tokens: any file path, any test count, any date-stamped 'measured'). Carries the 14 transferable lessons listed under KEEP. A lesson is admitted only if it is true independent of the current tree state. This is the file that makes the archaeology deletable.

TIER 1 — THE SPECS (~26 numbered docs, hard cap 4,500 TOKENS each, CI-enforced, split by ownership boundary when exceeded):
  00 Platform overview + cross-cutting laws + the three-layer config model (KEEP as-is)
  01a Kernel: event envelope, catalog, scopes, money, time  [split from today's 16.7k-token 01]
  01b Kernel: sync, mesh, catch-up, device admission/revocation
  02a POS: order lifecycle and the counter loop  [split from today's 14k-token 02]
  02b POS: settlement, shifts, cash, escalation
  03a Kitchen: states, routing, aging, pass/KDS  [split from today's 18.9k-token 03]
  03b Printing: document model, ESC/POS, spooler, physical
  04 Waiter · 05 Manager console · 06 Storefront · 07 WhatsApp · 08 Aggregators · 09 Riders · 10 Inventory · 11 Staff · 12 Owner · 13 Intelligence (all KEEP, all already under cap)
  14 Back office (layer-2 config)
  15 Platform admin (layer-1 control plane)
  16 Tax · 17 Marketing (KEEP)
  18 Engineering handbook · 20 Testing · 21 UX · 22 Operations & recovery · 23 Context governance · 24 Build harness · 25 Fold performance · 26 Merge semantics · 27 Design language (all KEEP)
  **28 — NEW: Tenancy, entitlement & billing.** The document that does not exist. Owns: tenant identity and isolation as a tested property (absorbing 01-F71); plan/entitlement resolution as DATA not env; metering→invoice; dunning and what a non-paying tenant actually experiences; self-service signup or its refusal; per-tenant data residency; the tenant onboarding pipeline as an automated flow rather than 15-F10's human runbook.
  **29 — NEW: Deployment & the cloud plane.** Also does not exist. Owns: environments, regions, migration/rollout across N tenants, per-tenant observability and SLOs, incident response, on-call, cost per tenant. Today `18 §13` gives this four lines and `18 §16` defers hosting decisions to 'Wave 2'.

TIER 2 — PACKAGE GUIDES (one per package, hard cap 1,500 tokens, CI-enforced). Same rule as LESSONS: no status claims, no test counts, no dates. What the package IS and what its seams are. Today apps/pos-electron/CLAUDE.md is 34,800 tokens.

PLANS: recreated per wave, DELETED at wave end as plans/README.md already says, and CI-enforced by refusing a plans/wave-N directory once wave N+1 opens.

AUTHORITY ORDER (unchanged in structure, one clause added):
  (1) restaurant-os.md Part I for vision, waves and settled product laws
  (2) specs/00 §5 + specs/21 for cross-cutting UX/offline/performance
  (2b) NEW: specs/28 for anything tenancy-, entitlement- or billing-shaped — it wins over a module spec, because the deviation happened when no document owned this and each module answered it locally
  (3) the owning module spec for its normative behaviour
  (4) restaurant-os.md Part II appendices are seeds the specs refine
Still byte-identical across restaurant-os.md and specs/00, still C4-enforced.

MIGRATES vs WRITTEN FRESH: ~24 of 28 existing spec documents migrate substantially unchanged (all of 04–13, 16–27 and 00 §5–§7); 3 documents are split rather than rewritten (01, 02, 03); 2 documents are new (28, 29); 2 documents are amended in one clause each (restaurant-os.md §2 law 5, and the 15-F7 / 00 §5.1 entitlement conflict); AGENTS.md, KERNEL.md, SYNC-ORDERING-PROBLEM.md and 28 orphaned plan files are retired. Estimated new corpus: ~130k tokens against today's 485k, a 73% reduction with essentially no requirement lost.
- **The 23-F3 size rail — change the unit from lines to tokens**
  - *why:* 23-F3 and 23-F8 both specify a TOKEN cap; docs-lint.mjs C5 counts lines. Thirteen documents are over the token cap at up to 4.2× and the rail reports clean. This single defect is why the corpus reached 485k tokens: the constraint the authors believed they were under was never applied. It is also the repo's own named 'proxy accepted as the evidence' error, sitting inside the governance rail.
  - *shape:* Replace `read(f).split("\n").length > 360` with a token estimate (`words × 4/3`, or a real tokenizer) asserted against 4,500 for specs, 2,000 for the T0 router, 3,000 for LESSONS.md, 1,500 for a package guide. Apply it to specs/, root docs and per-package CLAUDE.md, not specs/ alone. Mutation-prove it: put a 5,000-word line back into any doc and confirm the rail reddens by name — today it does not.
- **AGENTS.md**
  - *why:* 83% archaeology (10,339 of 12,516 words), five of eight re-measurable claims stale on 2026-08-16, and three false assertions about a product-shape fact (the LAN mesh is hosted; it says it is not). It is loaded on every single session via CLAUDE.md's `@AGENTS.md` and is the single largest source of wrong beliefs in the repo. It cannot be edited into health because its failure mode is structural: it is a status board written in prose, and prose status boards rot faster than anyone edits them.
  - *shape:* Split into (a) a ≤2,000-token router that contains no status claim of any kind, (b) LESSONS.md carrying the 14 durable lessons with all file paths, counts and dates stripped, and (c) nothing else. Every status fact currently in it either becomes a rail that prints the number on demand (seams:check already does this; AGENTS.md hand-copies its output and gets it wrong) or is deleted. Enforce by CI: the router file fails on any line containing a digit-plus-'tests', a date, or a path under apps/|packages|services.
- **restaurant-os.md §2 design law 5 — 'LAN-first real-time. In-branch coordination works with the internet dead; cloud is the exhaust and cross-branch path.'**
  - *why:* The first clause is a genuine market requirement (Pakistani power and connectivity) and a real moat; it survives intact and every serious POS SaaS does it. The SECOND clause is the source of the deviation. 'Exhaust' is a one-directional metaphor that makes the cloud a downstream reporting sink, and that is precisely the shape the code took — 01-F68 measured that org_id arrived at the gateway as free text with no orgs table and no code path creating an org. The corpus already violates the metaphor in practice: the catalog travels API → gateway → device, which is cloud-DOWN.
  - *shape:* Amend to a bidirectional statement, e.g.: 'LAN-first real-time. In-branch coordination (sub-second state propagation) works with the internet dead, indefinitely. The cloud is the CONTROL PLANE and the cross-branch path: it owns tenant identity, entitlement, configuration authority and the fleet, and it publishes DOWN to branches as well as receiving UP from them. A branch may run without the cloud; a tenant may not exist without it.' One clause changed; law 5 keeps its veto power and stops producing a single-tenant architecture.
- **The 15-F7 / 00 §5.1 / commandment-4 entitlement conflict**
  - *why:* specs/15-platform-admin.md:34 states 'in-branch POS billing never stops (00 §5.1 is not a business lever)'. That is a defensible product ethic and an impossible subscription business — the vendor has no way to withhold the product from a non-payer. It is the one place where a corpus law is genuinely hostile to the declared SaaS model, and it must be RULED rather than quietly softened, because softening it silently would break the offline-first law that every branch depends on.
  - *shape:* A founder ruling recorded as a DEC-* entry and promoted into new spec 28, choosing explicitly among: (a) keep 15-F7 verbatim — never gate the till, enforce commercially only (honest, and means churn is the only lever); (b) gate only CLOUD services on non-payment and let the till run forever, which is what 15-F7 already half-says (storefront down, sync accepted, cloud orders blocked) — the till degrades to an island; (c) time-boxed grace: the till keeps selling for N days after entitlement lapses and then refuses to OPEN A NEW DAY (never mid-service, never mid-order, never mid-settlement) with the refusal stated on the honesty strip. Whichever is chosen, 00 §5.1 must gain the sentence that names the exception, or the two documents contradict.
- **specs/DECISIONS.md**
  - *why:* 47 decisions compressed into a 56-line markdown table holding 12,890 words. Cells run past 2,600 characters and the Status column has been repurposed to carry entire rulings ('**RULED (founder, August 2026): REFUSE the second settlement at the till.** Observed on a running system, not derived'). The format has been fully defeated; it is now unreadable as a table and unsearchable as prose.
  - *shape:* An ADR directory: specs/decisions/DEC-MONEY-009.md, one file per decision, each ≤800 tokens with a fixed front-matter header (id, status, authority FRs, date ruled, superseded-by) and the argument in the body. A generated INDEX.md gives the one-line table the current file was trying to be. docs-lint gains a rule that every DEC-* referenced anywhere resolves to a file, exactly as C3 does for FR IDs — today DEC IDs have no resolution rail at all.
- **specs/01, 02 and 03 — split by ownership boundary as 23-F3 already requires**
  - *why:* These three are the corpus's most valuable and most-cited documents (01 at 98% code coverage, 03 at 93%, 02 at 91%) and also its three largest at 16.7k, 14.0k and 18.9k tokens — 3.1× to 4.2× their own cap. They are unreadable in one sitting, which is why sessions read past their contradictions, and they carry the highest density of changelog contamination (43, 28 and 34 retro-narrative markers).
  - *shape:* Split at the seams the docs already have: 01 → 01a (envelope, catalog, scopes, money, time) + 01b (sync, mesh, catch-up, device lifecycle); 02 → 02a (order lifecycle, counter loop) + 02b (settlement, shifts, cash, escalation); 03 → 03a (kitchen states, routing, aging, pass/KDS) + 03b (printing: document model, ESC/POS, spooler). CRITICAL: split does NOT renumber. FR IDs keep their two-digit prefix and the docs-lint C2 'prefix matches owning doc' check becomes prefix-matches-owning-FAMILY. 19,803 code citations survive untouched.
- **The layer-2 settings currently implemented as process environment variables**
  - *why:* specs/00 §7 is right and the code is wrong, so this is a code fix with a corpus consequence: the corpus never stated the enforcement, which is exactly how it drifted. 15-F27 has now stated it for provisioning ('never by hand-written SQL and never by an environment variable') — that law needs to cover CONFIGURATION, not just provisioning.
  - *shape:* Extend 15-F27's prohibition into a general law in spec 28: no layer-1 or layer-2 setting may be read from `process.env` in any shipping code path; the enforcement is a CI rail in the same family as seams:check that walks for env reads and cross-references the 00 §7 layer assignment. The six known violations (ENABLED_BRANCHES and ENABLED_CHANNELS in services/api/src/server.ts, RESTOS_STATION_ROUTES, RESTOS_HARDWARE_TIER, RESTOS_READY_SIGNAL_OWNER, RESTOS_AGING_THRESHOLDS) become the rail's first mutation fixtures. Layer 3 (printer assignment, station identity, panel_ppi) legitimately stays device-local.
- **Per-package CLAUDE.md files**
  - *why:* 66,911 words across 27 files, bimodally distributed: apps/pos-electron/CLAUDE.md alone is 34,800 tokens — larger than any spec in the corpus and twice AGENTS.md — while 11 files are sub-100-word stubs. The large ones have become second AGENTS.md files carrying the same status-board rot, and they load automatically when an agent works in that directory.
  - *shape:* Hard 1,500-token cap, CI-enforced, with the same content ban as the router: no test counts, no dates, no 'measured' claims, no defect status. A package guide states what the package is, what its public seams are, and what it must never do. Anything a package guide currently says about the STATE of the work moves to a plan (disposable) or a rail (self-measuring).
- **README.md**
  - *why:* 711 lines and ~9,730 tokens of local four-process laptop runbook, presented as the repository front door, covered by no rail (docs-lint C5 walks specs/ only), and the only document that would fail the line cap if it were. It also encodes the single-install shape: 'the three IDs', BOOTSTRAP_ORG_ID, one owner, one branch.
  - *shape:* Split into a genuine ~150-line README (what RestOS is, the three-tier doc map, how to get a dev environment, where the authority lives) and docs/running-locally.md carrying the runbook. The runbook's tenant section (create-org / create-branch / create-owner / list-tenancy, added at HEAD) is the seed of spec 29's deployment content and should be promoted there rather than living in a quickstart.

### Delete

- **KERNEL.md (97 lines, 1,525 words)**
  - *why:* Wholly false. KERNEL.md:5 states 'Everything here is on branch `feat/t-01-16-transport` (not yet merged to `main`). Nothing else in `packages/`/`services/` is built — this is the kernel only, the foundation the product apps (specs 02–17) will sit on.' There are 26 test-bearing packages, 11 apps and 8 services on main at HEAD. It is a reviewer's map for a code review that finished months ago and it actively misleads anyone who opens it first, which is precisely what it instructs them to do.
- **SYNC-ORDERING-PROBLEM.md (202 lines, 2,719 words)**
  - *why:* A self-contained July 2026 request for outside opinion on an ordering problem that has since been answered — DEC-PERF-001 is accepted, specs/26 is the settled design, and 01-F34's per-fold merge semantics ship with bijective-relabel and clock-injection invariance tests. It is a superseded draft preserved beside its own answer, and under SaaS it invites a reader to re-open a closed question. If the reasoning is wanted, it belongs in specs/decisions/DEC-PERF-001.md, not at the repo root.
- **All of plans/wave-0/ (17 files, ~2,800 lines)**
  - *why:* plans/README.md:3 declares plans are 'deleted after their wave (specs are permanent, conformance matrices are derived, plans are working memory)'. Wave 0 is merged. Every one of the 17 files is referenced from nowhere in AGENTS.md, specs/ or any package CLAUDE.md — I checked all 49 plan files by basename. The one file that looked load-bearing, plans/wave-0/recorded-rulings.md, states in its own header that both rulings were 'promoted into the FRs (01-F31 for R1, 01-F29 + DEC-SYNC-007 for R2)' — verified present in DECISIONS.md. Nothing is lost.
- **The 11 other orphaned plans/wave-1 files, including all 7 of plans/wave-1/research/ and decorative-surface-audit.md (584 lines)**
  - *why:* 28 of 49 plan files have zero inbound references. plans/ totals 9,228 lines and ~184,000 tokens — larger than the entire specs/ corpus (175k) — and it is a wave-scoped disposable layer by its own charter. The research files (thermal-kot, colour-typography, low-literacy-iconography, fiscal-printing-pakistan, print-customisation, pos-kds-patterns, token-architecture) were inputs to specs/27 and specs/03; their conclusions are in those specs, which are 90% and 93% built. Keeping the inputs beside the ratified outputs is how a reader ends up implementing against a draft.
- **AGENTS.md's seven archaeology paragraphs (L5, L7, L8, L10, L12, L14, L16 — 10,339 words, 82.6% of the file)**
  - *why:* They narrate the status of specific defects in specific files, and status written in prose rots faster than anyone edits it: five of eight re-measurable claims are wrong today, and the file contains three false assertions that the LAN mesh is 'hosted by nothing' when apps/pos-electron/src/main/mesh.ts and apps/pass-kds/src/main/mesh.ts have hosted it since 2026-08-13. Its own text admits this 29 times ('this line said', 'was WRONG', 'stopped being true'). Extract the 14 durable lessons into LESSONS.md first — they are the genuine value and they must not be lost — then delete the narration. Every remaining fact in those paragraphs is either printed by a rail on demand (seams:check prints the owed register; AGENTS.md hand-copies it and gets it wrong) or is history.
- **The 241 retro-narrative markers inside specs/ ('measured August 2026', 'Amended August 2026', 'this said', 'was WRONG', 'as written')**
  - *why:* They turn normative documents into changelogs, and their density correlates ~9× with implementation contact — specs/01 at 3.4 markers per 1,000 words (98% built) versus specs/22 at 0.37 (0% built). A spec must state what is true, not the history of what it used to say; the history belongs in git and in the ADR files. This is the same disease as AGENTS.md and it has already spread to the four most valuable documents in the corpus (01, 02, 03, 27 carry 131 of the 241 markers between them).
- **The `restaurant-os.md` Part II hardware-tier ladder as a normative structure (T1/T2/T3)**
  - *why:* DEC-HW-003 and the amended restaurant-os.md §4.1 already demote it: 'Hardware is a capability set, not a ladder… No code may branch on the tier to decide whether a piece of hardware exists', and 02-F31's own detection rule is a pure function from device classes to a rung. The ladder survives as a SALES label and should be stated once as that, in spec 28 alongside the plan shape — not carried through four documents (restaurant-os.md:47, 00 §7, 15-F10, 03 §7) as if it were a structural fact. Under SaaS, where a tenant declares its own hardware at onboarding, a rung is a pricing artefact, and 15-F10's history proves the cost of confusing the two: the go-live gate REQUIRED a printer and a cash drawer, so a restaurant that owned neither could not complete onboarding at all.

### Open questions

- ENTITLEMENT vs OFFLINE-FIRST — the sharpest fork, and it must be ruled before spec 28 can be written. 15-F7 says 'in-branch POS billing never stops (00 §5.1 is not a business lever)'. Does a non-paying tenant's till (a) keep selling forever, (b) lose only cloud services, or (c) refuse to OPEN A NEW DAY after a grace window, never mid-service? Option (a) is the current corpus law and means churn is the only lever. There is no correct answer from the specs; commandment 4 and the subscription model point opposite ways.
- RENUMBER OR EXTEND the FR system? Measured cost of renumbering: 19,803 citations in 601 of 660 source files (91%), all currently resolving with ZERO dangling. Measured evidence that extending works: HEAD added eight SaaS FRs (01-F68..F71, 11-F20, 15-F25..F27, 21-F15) and docs:lint stayed clean at 805 definitions. My recommendation is extend and split-without-renumbering, but the founder may want a clean numbering that matches a new document set — and that is a real trade, not an obvious one.
- SELF-SERVICE SIGNUP — 15-F26 rules it out, explicitly on scope grounds rather than merit ('not on a judgement that it is wrong'), and 15 §9.7 says it 'must be decided as one rather than arriving as the shortest path to a login'. A SaaS restaurants 'sign up for' either has it or does not, and the answer changes spec 15, spec 28, apps/backoffice's entry, and whether restaurant-os.md §7's done-for-you onboarding survives as the only path.
- THE CLOUD CONTROL PLANE'S RIGHT TO WRITE. 01-F62 fixes the event envelope at two scopes and requires a DEVICE to stamp a branch-scoped envelope; 05-F28 concludes a cloud-plane web surface 'cannot legally emit any event this module owns'; the revoke-device command already ships emitting NO event because a shell has no authenticated user, leaving revocations with no ledger record and no attribution. Does the kernel gain a third PLATFORM scope, does the control plane write outside the ledger to an audit store, or does every admin action require a device? This is a kernel-law change and cannot be decided inside spec 28.
- DOES LAN-FIRST REMAIN A WAVE-0 OBLIGATION, or become a tier-gated capability sold to tenants who need it? It is named as the technical moat (restaurant-os.md:35) and it is now hosted. But it is also the single largest source of complexity in the codebase (mesh-session, hub-election, transport-ws, split-brain heal, DEC-SYNC-004/009), and a SaaS with a cloud control plane could ship T1 tenants without it. Not a question the corpus can answer.
- WHO OWNS BILLING — a new spec 28, or an extension of specs/15? 15 §9.7 leaves it unowned and names an unresolved '§9.4 invoicing handoff' with no owner. My proposal creates spec 28 because tenancy/entitlement/billing cut across 15, 14, 06 and 01, but that is an architecture call the founder should make explicitly rather than inherit from an auditor.
- DATA RESIDENCY AND PII ERASURE remain proposed with 'legal verify' as the blocking dependency, and both are SaaS launch blockers rather than engineering tasks. DEC-DATA-002 ('Pakistan data-residency posture — Region near PK; residency law unverified', target Pre-pilot) and DEC-DATA-001 ('crypto-shredding default — Legal verify + build spike', Pre-pilot). Neither has moved and 22-F12's vendor data-flow map is 0% built.
- IS THE 'GIGANTIC TOOL, NOT AN MVP' STRATEGY (restaurant-os.md:23, 'Public launch is the full suite') still the founder's position under a SaaS framing? The measured build state is four documents deep and twenty-four wide — 01/02/03/27 at 90–98% and 04/07/10/11/17/22 at 0–10%, with 342 of 708 FRs never cited from code. A SaaS that restaurants sign up for usually launches narrow and grows; the current corpus is scoped for a single simultaneous launch of everything, and the reconciliation document already records that reading plans/ as scope caused one wave to be tracked wrongly.

### Adversarial verdicts

- **CONFIRMED** — The size-cap rail enforces the wrong unit — 23-F3/23-F8 promise a token cap, scripts/docs-lint.mjs C5 checks lines only; 13 docs exceed the token cap and docs:lint reports clean.
  - All citations exact. specs/23-ai-context.md:17 = 23-F3 "~4.5k tokens (~350 lines)"; :50 = 23-F8 "per-doc token count under the 23-F3 cap". scripts/docs-lint.mjs:73-77 is C5 and reads `read(...).split("\n").length > 360`; `grep -an token` over the 219-line file returns exactly one hit (line 47, "NN-Fxx / NN-Nxx token", lexical, not counting). `node scripts/docs-lint.mjs` → "clean — 28 specs, 805 FR definitions", REAL_EXIT=0. Word counts reproduce to the digit (specs/03 14,182 w; DECISIONS 12,890; AGENTS 12,516; specs/01 12,500). Two qualifications that do not change the verdict: 23-F3's cap governs *specs*, and 3 of the 13 listed docs (AGENTS.md, README.md, restaurant-os.md) are not specs — though AGENTS.md is 11× its own stricter T0 cap (~1.5k tok), so the substance is worse, not better. And words×4/3 is conservative: chars/4 puts specs/01 at ~20.3k and AGENTS.md at ~20.2k.
- **CONFIRMED** — The corpus deliberately does not specify billing, subscriptions, plans, quotas or metering-to-invoice.
  - specs/15-platform-admin.md:182 carries the quoted sentence verbatim, including "deliberately NOT specified anywhere in this corpus" and the §9.4-owner gate. 15-F5a at :32 fixes only `{ base_subscription, take_rate (0–5%), add_ons: [tax, …] }` — "one plan, no tier SKUs". Nothing elsewhere contradicts it.
- **AMENDED** — 15-F7 forbids the product from ever being withheld for non-payment — a subscription business with no enforcement lever.
  - The quotes are exact (15-F7 at :34, restated at :101) but the conclusion overstates. 15-F7's full text gives suspension real teeth: "gates cloud services (storefront down with an honest notice, sync still accepted, new cloud-originated orders blocked)" plus a device status banner. So the lever exists and is substantial — what is protected is only the in-branch POS. Two further over-reads: the corpus never ties suspension to non-payment at all (per finding 2 billing is unspecified), so 15-F7 is not a rule about payment; and ":101 restated" is the same rule restated as a lifecycle status value ("15-F7 unchanged and restated here only as a status value"), not a second normative statement. Corrected claim: the corpus protects in-branch POS from ever being gated (00 §5.1, commandment 4) while explicitly permitting cloud-service suspension as the lever.
- **REFUTED** — Design law 5's "cloud is the exhaust" clause is the source of the deviation, and the code took exactly that shape: no orgs table, no code path creating an org, org_id arriving as free text.
  - Two failures. (1) Citation: law 5 is restaurant-os.md:27, not :35. (2) The load-bearing code claim is stale — the auditor quoted 01-F68's own dated *motivation* clause ("measured August 2026…") as if it described the tree. It does not: `services/sync-gateway/drizzle/0010_tenancy_records.sql:48` runs `CREATE TABLE "kernel"."orgs"`, and `services/sync-gateway/src/create-org.ts` is a declared command (`"create-org": "tsx src/create-org.ts"` in package.json:10) whose header states it closed exactly this gap, alongside create-branch.ts, create-owner.ts and list-tenancy.ts. HEAD is 8659587 "Multi-tenant SaaS foundations". 01-F68 is the FR that *fixes* the deviation, not evidence it persists. The causal attribution to the metaphor is in any case an unfalsifiable reading.
- **AMENDED** — Six layer-2 settings (00 §7) are implemented as process environment variables, making each running cloud process single-tenant by construction. BOOTSTRAP_ORG_ID ×30, 36 distinct env knobs.
  - The mapping and all counts reproduce exactly: 00 §7:169 lists hardware tier, channels enabled, signal ownership, station fulfilment routes and alert thresholds under layer 2; server.ts:401-402 reads ENABLED_BRANCHES/ENABLED_CHANNELS from env; station-routing.ts:72, hardware-tier.ts:211, ready-signal.ts:87, aging.ts:76 each export their RESTOS_* env name. My own `grep -arhoE` gives BOOTSTRAP_ORG_ID = 30 and 36 distinct names. services/api/src/server.ts:391 concedes it in code: "`00 §7`'s layer-2 config plane does not exist, so this is where … actually lands." The amendment is the conclusion: four of the six named settings (station routes, hardware tier, ready-signal owner, aging thresholds) live in DEVICE processes (pos-electron, pass-kds, device-config), where per-process config does not imply cloud single-tenancy; only ENABLED_BRANCHES/ENABLED_CHANNELS plus BOOTSTRAP_ORG_ID support that clause. And BOOTSTRAP_* is already a boot-refused stopgap superseded by the 15-F26/15-F27 provisioning commands (server.ts:230-246). Corrected: two layer-2 settings make services/api single-tenant; the other four are a layer-2/layer-3 config-plane gap on devices.
- **CONFIRMED** — AGENTS.md is 83% codebase archaeology by word count; it defeats its own 120-line cap by writing 5,013-word lines.
  - Reproduced to the digit. `wc -l -w -c AGENTS.md` = 89 / 12,516 / 80,774. Per-line word counts via awk: L5=5013, L16=1724, L12=986, L14=840, L8=674, L10=562, L7=540 — the exact seven cited, summing to 10,339 = 82.6%; durable remainder 12,516−10,339 = 2,177. Exactly 7 lines exceed 500 words (next is 192). `grep -ao "⚠" | wc -l` = 34. The self-correction count (29) is the one figure I could not reproduce from a stated pattern — a broad pattern sweep gives 21 — but it is decorative to the claim.
- **AMENDED** — AGENTS.md is measurably stale now; of eight re-measurable assertions five are wrong, worst being the LAN-mesh/T1-only claims.
  - The conclusion holds and the worst item is CONFIRMED — apps/pos-electron/src/main/mesh.ts and apps/pass-kds/src/main/mesh.ts both exist (mtime 2026-08-13) and packages/sync-client/src/mesh-session.ts:18-21 reads "HOSTED since August 2026 (01-F12/F13/F15) … the oldest open item in the product — is deleted with that work", while AGENTS.md still says "hosted by nothing" and "RestOS is T1-only today". Items (4) and (5) also confirm: packages/ui/src/components/index.ts is 22 lines vs AGENTS.md's 18, and package.json:17 shows seven verify steps (strings:check added). But items (1) and (2) are REFUTED and refuted in the misleading direction: the auditor counted raw marker TOKENS with grep over .ts files (17 / 39-across-22) instead of reading the rail, which counts marked EXPORTS — a file-header marker covers every export in a module, as create-org.ts states outright. `node scripts/check-seams.mjs` (exit 0) prints "34 marked @unreached-by-design, 21 marked @unreached-owed" across 14 files. So the owed register went DOWN to 21, not up to 39, and by-design is 34, not 17. Item (3) is off by one: PERMISSION_ACTIONS holds 25 entries (counted programmatically), not 24 (AGENTS.md's 23 is still stale). This is the audit reproducing the very "proxy accepted as the evidence" defect it charges AGENTS.md with.
- **AMENDED** — KERNEL.md is wholly false and actively misleading — it describes a pre-merge branch state and asserts nothing outside the kernel is built.
  - KERNEL.md:5 is verbatim as quoted and is genuinely false and misleading — HEAD is 8659587 on main, 26 packages carry a test script, and the branch named has long since merged. But "wholly false" is inflated: §1 onward is an accurate description of the kernel that AGENTS.md still routes readers to ("KERNEL.md §'Not yet done at all' agrees"). The supporting counts are also wrong: `ls apps services` gives 10 apps and 7 services, not the claimed 11 and 8. Corrected: KERNEL.md's fifth line (2 sentences) is false and stale by ~4 months; the document's body is not.
- **CONFIRMED** — specs/22 (22 FRs) and specs/10 have zero code citations — the two largest fully-unbuilt bodies of requirement.
  - Independently measured across all specs: defined FR IDs vs IDs cited anywhere in apps/services/packages .ts/.tsx. specs/22 = 22 defined / 0 cited; specs/10 = 27 defined / 0 cited. They are the ONLY two FR-bearing specs at zero (next lowest: 07 at 1/25, 17 at 1/20, 04 at 2/20, 11 at 2/20). 22-F1..22-F22 cover RPO/PITR, restore drills, retention matrix, PII erasure, legal hold, org export and vendor exit as claimed.
- **CONFIRMED** — 241 retro-narrative markers sit inside normative specs, with ~9× density correlation to implementation contact.
  - Reproducible within ~7% using date-mention counting: specs/01 = 40 (claimed 43), specs/02 = 27 (28), specs/03 = 32 (34), specs/27 = 26 (26), specs/22 = 1 (1), specs/17 = 1 (1), specs/10 = 2 (2); total "2026" mentions across specs/ = 258 vs the claimed 241, so the auditor's set is slightly narrower than mine, not looser. Sampled context confirms these are genuine amendment narrative, not header dates alone — e.g. specs/03 carries "**Amended July 2026 (`03-F50`)**", "**Amended August 2026 (`03-F51`)**", "`stalled` added July 2026", "verified July 2026 against the code-pa…". The density gap between built and unbuilt docs holds in every pair.
- **CONFIRMED** — DECISIONS.md's table format has structurally broken down — 47 decisions in 56 lines / 12,890 words, cells past 2,600 chars, Status column repurposed to hold rulings.
  - `grep -aoE "DEC-[A-Z]+-[0-9]+" | sort -u | wc -l` = 47; `wc -l -w` = 56 / 12,890. DEC-ARCH-001's row is line 54 at 6,384 characters — 2.5× the claimed threshold, so the finding understates. Longest row is line 49 at 12,403 chars. The Status-cell repurposing is verbatim at line 12: "**RULED (founder, August 2026): REFUSE the second settlement at the till.** Observed on a running system, not derived".
- **CONFIRMED** — plans/ violates its own governance rule and is 57% orphaned — all wave-0 task files survive; 28 of 49 plan files referenced from nowhere.
  - plans/README.md:3 verbatim, including "**deleted after their wave**". My independent basename sweep across AGENTS.md + specs/*.md + every package CLAUDE.md reproduces the split exactly: 21 referenced, 28 orphaned of 49 files, including all ten plans/wave-0/t-01-*.md, kernel-tasks.md, recorded-rulings.md, all seven plans/wave-1/research/*.md and decorative-surface-audit.md. `wc -l` = 9,228 and `wc -w` = 137,830 across plans/, both as claimed. One trivial correction: plans/wave-0 holds 18 files, not 17.
- **CONFIRMED** — 01-F62 plus 05-F28 form a structural constraint: a cloud-plane web surface cannot legally emit most audited facts.
  - Both quotes verbatim. specs/01-kernel-sync.md:130 = 01-F62, "an org-scoped event carries no branch fields", envelope requires branch_id/branch_created_at/time_basis "stamped at append by an originating **device**". specs/05-manager-console.md:87 = 05-F28, "a cloud-plane surface cannot legally emit any event this module owns (measured August 2026)… **the web option cannot deliver `05-F6` at all**". Independently corroborated at specs/01:122, which says a cloud-plane emitter "would need a branch-scoped envelope it cannot legally stamp (`01-F62`), which is `05-F28`'s finding one event over".
- **CONFIRMED** — Per-package CLAUDE.md files replicate AGENTS.md's failure mode — 66,911 words across 27 files, bimodal with 11 sub-100-word stubs.
  - Under apps/services/packages I count 26 files totalling 66,908 words with 10 under 100 words; adding the root CLAUDE.md (3 words, `# RestOS` + `@AGENTS.md`) reconciles the auditor's figures exactly at 27 / 66,911 / 11. The top of the distribution matches to the word: pos-electron 26,078 (160,621 bytes — larger than any spec), sync-gateway 9,933, api 7,013, backoffice 5,901, pass-kds 4,298.
- **CONFIRMED** — A routine task loads ~47,000 tokens of prose before a line of code is read.
  - Confirmed and understated. CLAUDE.md:3 is `@AGENTS.md`, so the router is mandatory. The three files sum to 228,884 bytes; at the more realistic chars/4 that is ~57,200 tokens rather than 47,346, and pos-electron/CLAUDE.md adds another 160,621 bytes (~40k). Corpus-wide markdown outside node_modules measures 386,078 words, above the claimed 363,778. The bound-failure argument follows directly from finding 1, which I confirmed.

## assets

**Verdict.** The deviation is real but it is almost entirely in the HOSTING layer, not in the substance. Re-measured today, the repo is 4,642 green tests across 25 turbo tasks (4,284 without the gateway + 358 in `services/sync-gateway`, both read from vitest's own summary lines with `REAL_EXIT=0` markers — AGENTS.md's "4279"/"4375" are stale). The single-tenant assumption lives in environment wiring — `BOOTSTRAP_ORG_ID` in `services/api`, `ENABLED_BRANCHES`, `DEV_IDENTITY` in `packages/device-config/src/device-identity.ts:45` — and in the device-plane apps. It does NOT live in `packages/domain`, which has been org-scoped since Wave 0: `EventEnvelope` carries `org_id` (`packages/domain/src/envelope.ts:34`), `can()` and `reportScope` refuse cross-org before any action reasoning (`permissions.ts:485`, `:507`), and `tenancy.ts` (377 LOC, written 2026-08-16) is a complete four-record tenant directory with a stated refusal of FKs from ledger tables. The last commit (`8659587`) already stood up two tenants against the live tRPC host, found a real cross-tenant write (`catalog.runDayEnd` sweeping every org), and left a 1,189-line two-tenant attack suite behind. So the crown jewel is not merely salvageable — parts of it are already the SaaS work. What is genuinely at risk of being sunk is the offline-first device plane: `packages/sync-client` (7,781 prod LOC, 743 tests) and `apps/pos-electron` (1,188 tests) are excellent and exist to solve a problem — LAN-mesh, no-WAN, per-fold convergence without a total order — that a cloud-first SaaS may simply not have. That is ~40% of the codebase whose quality is high and whose SaaS-independence is low, and the honest answer is that the DESIGN (specs/26, the merge algebra, the money laws) survives while much of the CODE may not.

### Findings

- **packages/domain is the crown jewel and it is genuinely SaaS-independent: 3,183 production LOC, 8,720 test LOC, 543 passing tests across 28 acceptance files, and its only runtime dependencies are zod, uuidv7 and @noble/hashes.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* `wc -l packages/domain/src/*.ts` = 3183; `find … __acceptance__` = 8720; `pnpm -C packages/domain test` → `Test Files 28 passed (28) / Tests 543 passed (543)`, REAL_EXIT=0. `packages/domain/package.json` dependencies block has exactly three entries. 69 non-test files across 13 workspaces import from `@restos/domain` (grep -arl, comments/tests stripped).
- **The permission matrix's org arm is a real multi-tenant enforcement point, and it is the only one in the product that is mutation-proven at the function level. AGENTS.md's "89 tests" is accurate (re-measured).** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* `packages/domain/src/__acceptance__/permission-matrix.test.ts` → `Tests 89 passed (89)`. `permissions.ts:507` `if (subject.org_id !== scope.org_id) return { outcome: "deny", action }` in `can()`; `:485` the same in `reportScope`; `:581` in `canPayOut`. `packages/domain/src/__acceptance__/tenant-isolation-matrix.test.ts:36-52` records an OUT-OF-TREE mutation matrix — M-a1 kills 4, M-a2 kills 2, M-a3 kills 1, and M-neg (operands swapped, behaviour-preserving) kills 0 as the attribution control. It also measures that all 287 `services/api` tests stay green with either org arm deleted, i.e. the guard was previously unasserted anywhere.
- **packages/domain/src/tenancy.ts IS the SaaS tenant model and it already exists — 377 LOC of Zod records (OrgRecord, BranchRecord, DeviceRecord, PersonRecord) with org lifecycle states, plus migrations 0010/0011 and four declared provisioning commands.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* `packages/domain/src/tenancy.ts` — `ORG_STATUSES = ["active","suspended"]` (:~200), `OrgRecord`/`BranchRecord`/`DeviceRecord`/`PersonRecord`. `services/sync-gateway/drizzle/0010_tenancy_records.sql`, `0011_tenancy_users.sql`. `services/sync-gateway/src/{create-org,create-branch,create-owner,list-tenancy}.ts`. Commit `8659587` message: "Eight FRs (01-F68..01-F71, 11-F20, 15-F25..15-F27, 21-F15)".
- **The two-tenant attack suite in services/api is the highest-value SaaS artifact in the repo and it is only days old. It found a live cross-tenant WRITE that 13 read-side attacks and every prior suite missed.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* `services/api/src/__acceptance__/tenant-isolation.test.ts` — 1,189 LOC; header rules: "ONE HOST, TWO TENANTS, ONE SET OF STORES" and stores deliberately non-defensive so the service is the enforcement point. Commit `8659587`: "catalog.runDayEnd invoked by A's owner advanced B's published catalog v1->v2 … runDue() swept EVERY org and the procedure filtered only the ANSWER." Measured kill count in the header: making `catalog.published` read org_id from input kills 3 tests here, 230 pre-existing stay green.
- **packages/escpos is the most portable substantial asset in the repo: 3,702 production LOC, 12,418 test LOC, 368 tests, and its entire dependency surface is @restos/domain + qrcode.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* `wc -l packages/escpos/src/*.ts` = 3702; `__acceptance__` = 12418; `pnpm -C packages/escpos test` → `Tests 368 passed (368)`. `packages/escpos/package.json` dependencies = `{@restos/domain, qrcode}`. Contains a pure `render()` over `DocumentSpec`/`DocumentProfile`, an encoder with typed refusals (`EncodeRefusalReason`), a durable spooler, and `simulate.ts` (557 LOC) which walks bytes→pages so one interpretation exists rather than two.
- **seams:check is a genuinely novel, architecture-independent CI rail and it is currently clean with a printed debt register — but the register's numbers in AGENTS.md are wrong by 40-70%.** [MINOR]
  - *evidence:* `node scripts/check-seams.mjs` → exit 0, "Scanned 240 production modules (136 shipping), 621 value exports … 18 optional seams … 34 marked @unreached-by-design, 21 marked @unreached-owed". AGENTS.md claims 173/93/432/10 and 29/34. The rail's own two rules are in `scripts/check-seams.mjs:16-27`; the marker-on-a-reached-symbol failure mode ("the register cannot rot") is at `:55-70`.
- **layout:check is novel and roughly 25% of it is directly portable to any Chromium driver; the rest is Electron-bound. Its measurement core (probe.ts, mode-contract.ts) contains zero Electron references.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* `apps/pos-electron/src/layout-gate/` = main.ts 2057, mode-contract.ts 461, preload.ts 604, probe.ts 451 (3,573 LOC); `apps/pass-kds/src/layout-gate/` = 907 LOC. `grep -acn "electron|BrowserWindow" probe.ts` = 2 (both in a doc comment about executeJavaScript), `mode-contract.ts` = 0. `main.ts:1-9` imports `COUNTER_WINDOW_OPTIONS` from the shipped app rather than retyping it; `main.ts:35-45` states why Playwright is structurally blind to the frame-vs-content defect — an argument that only holds for Electron and would not survive a web POS.
- **packages/auditor is real, is REACHED by production code, and is already multi-tenant: services/jobs iterates every org in the kernel and runs it per org on a BullMQ repeatable.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* `packages/auditor/src/auditor.ts` 567 LOC, `runAuditor` at :150, five legs (lamport_gap, conservation, state_legality, readmodel_diff, audit_chain) plus `unparseable_merged_event`. Production callers: `services/jobs/src/index.ts:79` `import { runAuditor } from "@restos/auditor"`, `:134` `select org_id from kernel.org_sequences`, `:146-147` `for (const org_id of await everyOrg(db)) { await runAuditor({ db, org_id }) }`. Test coverage lives in `services/sync-gateway/src/__acceptance__/auditor-*.test.ts` — 9 files, 2,448 LOC, ~45 `it()` blocks, all inside the 358 green gateway tests.
- **The auditor has ONE unsupplied leg and it is the one specs/20 §4.2 calls its headline: the read-model diff. Nothing in the cloud maintains a projection to diff against.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* `packages/auditor/src/auditor.ts:99-110` — `read_model?: ReadModelInput` carries an `@unreached-owed` marker: "the scheduled host runs five legs and NOT this one, because there is nothing in the cloud to diff against … Supplying a snapshot this process refolded itself would diff a refold against a refold and always pass." This is a direct consequence of the device-side projection architecture; a cloud-first SaaS would make this leg trivially supplied.
- **packages/ui is 5,765 production LOC + 1,738 LOC of stories + 392 tests, 22 exported components, and its only workspace dependency is @restos/domain — but only its TOKEN layer is actually shared across planes. The component vocabulary is counter/touch-specific.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* `packages/ui/src/components/index.ts` = 22 export lines (AGENTS.md says 18 — stale). `packages/ui/package.json` dependencies = `{@restos/domain}`. Four workspaces depend on it (`apps/{pos-electron,pass-kds,manager,backoffice}`), but `apps/backoffice` imports only `@restos/ui/tokens`, `@restos/ui/fonts` and one component: `owner-summary.tsx:52 import { MoneyValue }`. Everything else in backoffice is Radix + Tailwind.
- **The physical-units layer (dp = 1/160 inch of glass, converted once through panel PPI) is a real, evidence-backed invention that survives any client technology — and it caught a 29% ergonomic breach that a pixel-based system could not see.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* `packages/ui/src/physical.tsx` 244 LOC — `cssPxPerDp(panelPpi, devicePixelRatio) = panelPpi / devicePixelRatio / DP_PER_INCH` at :66-76, with a worked table showing 126 dp = 79 px on a 100.5 PPI panel and 111 px on a 141.2 PPI panel (both 15.6″). `specs/27-design-language.md` 27-F68: a pinned 79 px constant is "14.2 mm on the 141-PPI panel §1a also lists — below 27-F8's floor, on the product's highest-consequence entry surface". Three IBM Plex Sans weights are bundled at `packages/ui/src/fonts/*.woff2`.
- **The design research is a standalone knowledge asset with primary-source citations, independent of every line of code — including one finding with direct founder/legal consequence.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* `plans/wave-1/research/` — 7 files, 68 KB. `low-literacy-iconography.md:1-40`: Pakistan literacy 58.86%, 74.1% urban vs 51.6% rural; CHI'13 n=60 deep-hierarchy success 91% high-literacy vs 40% low-literacy; INTERACT'13 flat paged 25 s/100% vs 4-level 65.5 s/80%; Urdu numerals are `latn` per CLDR with measured codepoint counts (express.pk 1,371 Latin / 0 Eastern). `fiscal-printing-pakistan.md:9-19`: Sindh Finance Act 2026 s.43 entry 2AA — up to Rs 1,000,000 penalty on "any person who designs, develops, customizes or supplies invoicing software" issuing non-conforming invoices, flagged UNVERIFIED and needing a Pakistani tax lawyer; and SRB's mandated 7×7 mm QR being physically unscannable at 203 dpi for its own ~95-char payload.
- **The spec corpus is 5,116 lines / 708 FRs / 47 recorded decisions, machine-linted for FR uniqueness, reference resolution and routing completeness — and traceable FR→test via a derived conformance matrix.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* `wc -l specs/*.md restaurant-os.md` = 5116; `grep -aho "^- [0-9][0-9]-F…" specs/*.md | wc -l` = 708; `grep -ac "^| DEC-" specs/DECISIONS.md` = 47. `scripts/docs-lint.mjs:18-80` — C1 routing completeness, C2 FR definition uniqueness + file-prefix match, C5 size caps. `conformance/01.yml` is 1,257 lines, header `# DERIVED by scripts/verify-module.mjs — do not hand-edit (24-F5)`, `derived_from: 4 suite run(s)`, each FR mapped to named test titles.
- **DEC-MONEY-007 (the khata double-count) is the single most expensive-to-rediscover piece of domain knowledge in the corpus: a Pakistani credit-tab repayment made every screen read "OVERPAID — refund to close", on the happy path, converged identically on every device.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* `specs/DECISIONS.md:10` — "A khata regular paying their ₨1,850 tab makes every screen read 'OVERPAID ₨1,850 — refund to close'. 01-F30's receivable is meanwhile structurally always 0. The fold cannot discriminate: payment.recorded.method is z.string() (registry.ts:50) while payment.refunded.method IS a closed enum (:56)." Resolution ratified as a `purpose: settles_order | repays_receivable` discriminator. The same shape then generated DEC-MONEY-004's tip modelling.
- **The money contract is enforced by three independent mechanisms, not one — branded types, BigInt accumulation, and a GritQL lint plugin whose own limits and false-positive history are documented in the rule file.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* `packages/domain/src/money.ts` — `totalPaisaOrNull` accumulates in BigInt (:51-67) with the stated reason that float `+` is non-associative near 2^53 so "delivery order decides a money outcome"; `splitPaisa` largest-remainder (:86); `applyRateBps` round-half-up in BigInt (:110). `packages/config/biome-plugins/no-raw-money-arithmetic.grit`, wired at `biome.json:3`, with a measured record of two false-negative rounds (the `rupees` field escaping the `paisa`-only match; five false positives on the blessed BigInt path from an unbalanced-paren receiver) and an explicit "HONEST LIMIT" section conceding renames defeat it. 59 tests across five money acceptance files.
- **packages/sync-client is high-quality and low SaaS-independence — 7,781 production LOC, 21,656 test LOC, 743 tests, all of it in service of an offline-first LAN mesh that is hosted by nothing today.** [MAJOR]
  - *evidence:* `packages/sync-client` prod 7781 / test 21656, `Tests 743 passed (743)`. `src/folds/merge.ts` is 1,521 LOC of per-fold merge algebra. `specs/26-merge-semantics.md` §1: folds need "deterministic merge semantics … not a universal total order". Against that: `seams:check` output and AGENTS.md both record `mesh-session.ts` carrying `@unreached-owed NO HOST RUNS THE LAN MESH YET`. If the SaaS client is a browser talking to a cloud, most of this package's reason for existing evaporates while the ALGEBRA (documented in specs/26, prototyped 13/13 money, 6/6 tables, 13/13 lines) remains a design asset.
- **Ten workspace packages are two-line `export {}` scaffolds that inflate every count of the system and cost real graph/install time.** [MINOR]
  - *evidence:* `apps/{owner,platform-admin,pos-rn,rider,storefront,waiter}/src/index.ts` and `services/{foodpanda,intelligence,tax,whatsapp}/src/index.ts` are each exactly `// Scaffold stub — implementation arrives via plans/ tasks (24 §9). Spec: specs/NN-….md` + `export {};` — 2 LOC each, 20 LOC total across 10 workspaces.
- **AGENTS.md's own size cap is unenforceable as written: the rail measures LINES and the file is 89 lines of 80,774 bytes.** [MINOR]
  - *evidence:* `scripts/docs-lint.mjs:73` — `// C5 — size caps (23-F3): specs ≤ 360 lines; AGENTS.md ≤ 120`; `:80` errs if line count > 120. `wc -l AGENTS.md` = 89, `wc -c AGENTS.md` = 80774 — i.e. ~900 bytes per line, single paragraphs of 8,000+ characters. The cap passes while the document is 80 KB, which is why so many of its numbers are stale: nothing forces it to be re-read.
- **packages/escpos's own barrel header is stale in the direction that matters — it says K-7 is owed when K-7 landed and the package is at 368 tests.** [MINOR]
  - *evidence:* `packages/escpos/src/index.ts:8` — "Owed: K-7 wires `order.confirmed` → spooler and raises 03-F5's S1 on the counter." AGENTS.md records K-1..K-7 all landed; `packages/escpos/src/__acceptance__/spooler*.test.ts` and the shipped `apps/pos-electron/src/main/printing.ts` contradict the header. K-8 (a physical printer) genuinely is still owed.
- **The deterministic simulation harness exists and is small, seeded and self-describing — the piece specs/20 calls "the crown jewel" of the testing taxonomy.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* `packages/testing/src/sim.ts` 275 LOC + `sim-cloud.ts` 372 + `virtual-printer.ts` 158 = 826 LOC, 75 tests. `sim.ts:1-6`: "single-threaded virtual time; timers and deliveries execute in (time, schedule-seq) order; every randomness draw comes from the seeded RNG only — no Math.random, no Date.now; same seed + same script => deep-equal trace(). Every delivery round-trips encodeMessage/decodeMessage." It carries an inverted `@unreached-by-design` marker: shipping code reaching it would be the bug.
- **The strings:check rail (added in the last commit) is directly SaaS-relevant — it is the only mechanical defence against internal identifiers reaching a paying customer's screen, and it is currently reporting 93 findings.** [INFO]
  - *evidence:* `scripts/check-strings.mjs` 984 LOC, wired into `pnpm verify` at `package.json:verify`. Header: the founder read "Leave blank to inherit from the category above (03-F50)" on a live help line, and a string telling an owner to "Set ENABLED_BRANCHES and ENABLED_CHANNELS on the RestOS service". Four banned classes: fr-id, env-key, repo-path, spec-ref, with comments stripped first. Current run: "93 string(s) OUTSIDE a catalog carry internal jargon, across 23 file(s) — counted, not failed", 1 file excluded as unwalkable JSX.

### Keep

- **packages/domain — money, permissions, event registry, tenancy, business-day, PIN/Argon2id, invariants, audit chain**
  - *why:* Org-scoped from the ground up; three runtime deps; no I/O, no framework, no host assumption. `can()`/`reportScope`/`canPayOut` already refuse cross-tenant and are the only mutation-proven isolation point in the product. `tenancy.ts` is literally the SaaS tenant directory and was written last week. The event registry (~41 Zod payload schemas) is the encoded business model of a Pakistani restaurant and is what a rewrite would otherwise spend a quarter re-deriving. Port it byte-for-byte.
  - *size:* 3,183 prod LOC / 8,720 test LOC / 543 tests / 28 acceptance files. `packages/domain/src/{registry.ts 1077, permissions.ts 600, tenancy.ts 377, money.ts 185, business-day.ts 179, pin.ts 156, invariants.ts 136}`
- **The tenant-isolation register: the two-tenant attack suite + the matrix-level org-arm suite**
  - *why:* This is the SaaS security work, already done, and it was written to a discipline no rewrite will reproduce cheaply: one host, two tenants, one set of deliberately non-defensive stores, plus an out-of-tree mutation matrix WITH a negative control. It already caught a live cross-tenant write. Whatever the new architecture is, these are the assertions to port first — they are the spec of what multi-tenancy must mean here.
  - *size:* `services/api/src/__acceptance__/tenant-isolation.test.ts` 1,189 LOC; `packages/domain/src/__acceptance__/tenant-isolation-matrix.test.ts` 351 LOC (10 tests, kills 4/2/1 with a 0-kill control)
- **packages/escpos — ESC/POS encoder, document model, renderer, spooler, byte→page simulator**
  - *why:* Zero coupling to tenancy, hosting, sync or UI. Thermal printing is a solved problem here and it is a problem every restaurant SaaS still has to solve. The typed-refusal design (a non-Latin user_text part REFUSES with `raster_font_unavailable` rather than silently printing garbage) is the kind of decision that only gets made once someone has thought hard about Urdu on a 203-dpi head. Move it to a package and never touch it again until K-8 (physical hardware) is run.
  - *size:* 3,702 prod LOC / 12,418 test LOC / 368 tests / 13 acceptance files. Deps: @restos/domain + qrcode only
- **The domain-knowledge corpus: specs/ (708 FRs) + specs/DECISIONS.md (47 rulings) + plans/wave-1/research/**
  - *why:* Completely code-independent and the single most expensive asset to re-acquire. Specific items a rewrite must not lose: the khata double-count (DEC-MONEY-007 — a credit-tab repayment reads as an overpayment on the happy path); the advances/baqaya ledger (restaurant-os.md:71, specs/11 §Advances — "the deeply Pakistani gap no competitor touches"); COD rider settlement and khata deliveries collecting zero (09-F10); the 05:00 Asia/Karachi business day as platform law with only the hour configurable (01-F46); FBR/PRA/SRB fiscalization incl. the vendor-liability finding and the unscannable-QR finding; aggregator receivables (01-F32) and foodpanda commission economics; low-literacy design derived from census + CHI/INTERACT studies rather than taste.
  - *size:* specs/ 5,116 lines, 708 FR definitions, 47 DEC- rows; plans/wave-1/research/ 7 files / 68 KB with primary-source citations; plans/wave-0/ 18 task+ruling records
- **The static CI rails: seams:check, strings:check, docs-lint, tokens:check, the GritQL money ban, verify-module.mjs + conformance/**
  - *why:* Pure Node, no framework coupling, and they encode failure modes this team actually hit. seams:check answers a question no off-the-shelf tool answers ("does SHIPPING code reach this?" with a barrel re-export explicitly not counting as a use) and its exception markers fail when stale, so the debt register cannot rot. strings:check is the only mechanical defence against leaking internal identifiers to a paying customer — which is exactly the complaint that triggered this whole review. The conformance matrix gives FR→test traceability that a SaaS with fiscal-compliance obligations will want anyway. All of it transfers verbatim.
  - *size:* scripts/ 2,562 LOC (check-seams 1167, check-strings 984, docs-lint 219, verify-module 131, check-tokens 61) + no-raw-money-arithmetic.grit + conformance/01.yml 1,257 derived lines
- **packages/ui's token + physical-units + font layer (NOT the whole component set)**
  - *why:* `tokens/` and `physical.tsx` are the parts already proven to cross planes — the Next.js back office consumes `@restos/ui/tokens` and `@restos/ui/fonts` today. The dp→millimetre conversion (a dp is 1/160 inch of glass, applied once at the token boundary through the panel's own PPI) is a genuine invention: it caught a 20 mm ergonomic floor rendering at 14.2 mm on a panel the spec itself lists. The bundled IBM Plex Sans is load-bearing — without it every OS picked a different fallback and 44 fatal violations appeared on Linux against 0 on macOS from one repo state.
  - *size:* `packages/ui/src/tokens/` 2,205 LOC incl. 4 oracle suites; `physical.tsx` 244; `surface-mode.tsx` 187; 3 bundled woff2 weights
- **packages/auditor + the services/jobs per-org scheduler**
  - *why:* Read-only, five-leg correctness audit over one org's kernel tables — conservation, state legality, lamport-gap coverage, audit-chain integrity. It is already multi-tenant in shape: `services/jobs` selects every org from `kernel.org_sequences` and runs it per org on a BullMQ repeatable. specs/20 §4.2 calls it "the single highest-value correctness artifact we build" and it is the thing that would catch a permanently doubled cash figure in a tenant nobody is watching. Conditional on the event-sourced kernel surviving.
  - *size:* `packages/auditor/src/auditor.ts` 567 LOC; test coverage 2,448 LOC across 9 gateway suites (~45 `it()` blocks) inside 358 green gateway tests; caller at `services/jobs/src/index.ts:79,146`
- **packages/testing's deterministic simulation scheduler**
  - *why:* Seeded virtual time, no Math.random, no Date.now, same seed ⇒ deep-equal trace, and every delivery round-trips the wire codec. Small, self-contained, and the only way to test a distributed system's failure modes reproducibly. Keep it even if the mesh goes — a cloud SaaS still has reconnects, retries and partial writes to simulate.
  - *size:* 826 LOC / 75 tests. `packages/testing/src/{sim.ts 275, sim-cloud.ts 372, virtual-printer.ts 158}`
- **specs/20 + specs/24 as a working method, not as documents**
  - *why:* The disciplines that actually produced the quality here: acceptance tests authored by a DIFFERENT session from spec text and committed red first; the round-3 mutation law (build a plausible implementation out-of-tree, take the suite green, break exactly one branch, confirm THAT assertion fails, and use a behaviour-preserving control so kill counts mean something); security-parameter mutation happening out-of-tree only. These are why 543 domain tests are worth more than most 5,000-test suites. They are process assets and cost nothing to carry forward.
  - *size:* specs/20 113 lines, specs/24 104 lines; the discipline is visible in every acceptance-file header, e.g. tenant-isolation-matrix.test.ts:36-52

### Redo

- **packages/ui's component vocabulary (22 components, 5,765 LOC, 392 tests)**
  - *why:* It is a COUNTER vocabulary — Cart, TenderPanel, NumericKeypad, ItemGrid, TicketCard, AlarmBand — designed for a touch till at 76 dp tiles and 126 dp keys. Under SaaS the surfaces multiply (signup, billing, tenant admin, owner web analytics, a support console) and none of them is a till. The back office already proved this by importing exactly one component and otherwise using Radix + Tailwind. Keeping the components as-is would either constrain the web surfaces or fork the design system.
  - *shape:* Split into three: (1) `@restos/tokens` — colours, type, dp/mm conversion, fonts — shared by every surface, unchanged; (2) `@restos/counter-ui` — the 22 touch components, kept intact for whatever the till becomes; (3) a NEW web/admin vocabulary built on the same tokens for tenant-facing SaaS screens. The token layer is the contract between them, and it already works that way in `apps/backoffice/src/lib/theme-css.ts`.
- **layout:check**
  - *why:* Its highest-value half (the ancestor-clipping walk, the composition/fit verdicts, the mm-of-glass touch-target check across an 11-panel sweep) is technology-neutral and lives in files with zero Electron references. Its harness half constructs a real Electron BrowserWindow from the shipped app's own options — which is correct today and meaningless if the POS becomes a browser tab. Its central anti-Playwright argument ("a headless browser sets the viewport, and the bug is that the app does not GET that viewport") is specific to Electron frame-vs-content sizing.
  - *shape:* Extract `probe.ts` (451) + `mode-contract.ts` (461) into a framework-free `@restos/layout-probe` package that takes a driver interface. Give it two drivers: Electron (if a desktop till survives) and Playwright with `deviceScaleFactor`/physical-size emulation for web surfaces. Keep the panel sweep table and the `PANEL_FLOOR_MM` concept verbatim — a floor stated in millimetres of glass is right regardless of client.
- **The auditor's read-model diff leg and its host placement**
  - *why:* The one leg specs/20 §4.2 puts in its headline sentence is unsupplied, and the recorded reason is architectural: the cloud maintains no incrementally-maintained projection to diff against, because projections are built device-side. A SaaS with a server-side read model makes this leg trivially supplied — and it is the leg that catches the class the other five cannot see. Separately, `services/jobs` currently reports findings to a log line; a multi-tenant operator needs findings routed per tenant.
  - *shape:* Keep `runAuditor`'s five legs and its read-only posture (zero writes, only SELECTs) unchanged. Supply `read_model` from the server-side projection the SaaS will have anyway. Add per-tenant finding persistence + alerting rather than a log line at `services/jobs/src/index.ts:155,163`.
- **The permission matrix's role set**
  - *why:* `ROLES = [cashier, branch_manager, storekeeper, owner]` and `reportScope` tops out at `"org"`. There is no platform-operator, support-agent or billing role, and no cross-org reach of any kind — which is correct as a default and insufficient for a SaaS that must support its tenants. specs/15 (platform admin) exists but `apps/platform-admin` is a two-line stub.
  - *shape:* Keep the matrix engine, the `AuthDecision`/`satisfied_by` shape, the fail-closed defaults and the org arm exactly as they are — they are right. Add a SEPARATE control-plane authority (platform roles, impersonation with audit, support scoping) that composes with `can()` rather than widening `ROLES`, so a tenant-plane role can never accidentally acquire cross-org reach. The corpus's own precedent for adding an action without touching Appendix A (14-F30, 02-F46, 02-F47) is the pattern to follow.
- **The single-tenant bootstrap wiring**
  - *why:* This is the actual deviation and it is small and localised: an org assembled at boot from `BOOTSTRAP_ORG_ID`/`BOOTSTRAP_OWNER_*` into a process-local Map that dies with the process, `ENABLED_BRANCHES` as an env-declared branch set, and `DEV_IDENTITY` as a hardcoded device triple. The kernel underneath was never single-tenant.
  - *shape:* Delete the env-var tenancy path entirely; the replacement already exists — `kernel.orgs`/`kernel.branches`/`kernel.users` (migrations 0010/0011) plus `create-org`/`create-branch`/`create-owner`. What is missing is self-service signup and a device pairing flow that does not require shell access on the service host (01-F25's back-office pairing code, deliberately never invented). `packages/device-config/src/device-identity.ts:45`'s `DEV_IDENTITY` fallback must become a hard failure, not a default.
- **packages/sync-client and apps/pos-electron as the till architecture**
  - *why:* 743 + 1,188 tests of genuinely excellent work aimed at a problem statement — LAN mesh, no WAN dependency, per-fold convergence without a total order, hub election — that a cloud-first SaaS may not have. The mesh is built and hosted by nothing today, so the product is already T1-only (one till per branch) in practice. Rewriting the till as a thin cloud client would discard most of this; keeping offline-first would preserve it at the cost of every SaaS-shaped simplification.
  - *shape:* This is a founder fork, not an engineering call (see open questions). If offline-first survives: keep both, they are the asset. If not: keep specs/26's merge algebra as a DESIGN record, keep the event registry and the money laws (already in domain), keep the local-durability property (01-F2: persisted before UI ack) as a requirement on whatever replaces it, and accept ~29,000 LOC of sync-client tests as sunk. Do not attempt a partial port — the value of merge.ts is that it is complete and mutation-proven.

### Delete

- **Ten two-line scaffold workspaces: apps/{owner,platform-admin,pos-rn,rider,storefront,waiter}, services/{foodpanda,intelligence,tax,whatsapp}**
  - *why:* Each is `export {};` with a comment. 20 LOC total across 10 workspaces. They make the repo present as "10 apps, 7 services" when 4 apps and 3 services actually run, they cost install and turbo-graph time, and they let a reader (or an agent) believe a module exists. Their specs are the real placeholder and those stay. Deleting them costs nothing and makes every subsequent count honest.
- **AGENTS.md in its current form (80,774 bytes across 89 lines)**
  - *why:* It is the repo's largest liability disguised as its memory. Its own size rail passes because the cap is measured in LINES and the paragraphs are 8,000 characters. Re-measured today it is stale on: total test count (says 4279/4375, actual 4,642), ui component count (says 18, actual 22), seams register (says 173/93/432/10 and 29/34, actual 240/136/621/18 and 34/21), and it documents its own staleness repeatedly instead of being fixed. The KNOWLEDGE in it is real and much of it is recorded nowhere else — extract the recurring-defect taxonomy, the measurement traps (grep -a under C locale, laundered exit codes, turbo --force/--continue) and the mutation discipline into short owned documents, then delete the narrative.
- **The claim that layout:check's Electron harness is the general solution**
  - *why:* Not a file to delete but an idea to drop. `apps/pos-electron/src/layout-gate/main.ts:35-45` argues Playwright is the wrong tool because a headless browser cannot see the frame-vs-content sizing defect. That is true and it is an Electron-specific defect. Carrying that argument into a web SaaS would block the right tool for the wrong reason.
- **The env-var-as-tenancy pattern (BOOTSTRAP_ORG_ID, ENABLED_BRANCHES, ENABLED_CHANNELS, DEV_IDENTITY)**
  - *why:* This IS the deviation, mechanically. It is a join key with three ends and no error message: the API's org, the back office's enabled set and the device's branch_id must all agree or all four processes report success and no till ever sees a menu. Under multi-tenancy it is not merely wrong, it is unrepresentable. The database-backed replacement already exists and shipped last week.

### Open questions

- Does offline-first survive? This is the single fork that decides whether ~29,000 LOC of sync-client + pos-electron test work is an asset or sunk cost. The corpus makes it platform law (`00 §5.1-5.2`, commandment 4: no in-branch feature may require WAN; confirmed = locally persisted before UI ack) and the Pakistani connectivity reality is the stated reason. A cloud-first SaaS is dramatically simpler and would discard the best-tested part of the repo. Do not let this be decided implicitly by whoever writes the first new client.
- Does the till stay a desktop app? packages/ui's 22 components, the whole layout:check harness, `PANEL_FLOOR_MM`, and the Electron single-instance/device-binding work all assume installed software on a restaurant's own hardware. A browser-based till changes what is salvageable in `packages/ui` and `apps/pos-electron` more than any other decision.
- Where does the control plane live and who may cross a tenant boundary? The permission matrix has no platform/support role and `reportScope` stops at `org`. Support impersonation, billing access and platform admin are unbuilt (apps/platform-admin is a stub) and specs/15 §9's open question about whether managers get a scoped back-office slice is still open. Answering the SaaS half by widening `ROLES` would silently answer the tenant half too.
- Is the Sindh Finance Act 2026 vendor-liability finding real? `plans/wave-1/research/fiscal-printing-pakistan.md:9-19` reads a penalty of up to Rs 1,000,000 on the party that "designs, develops, customizes or supplies invoicing software" issuing non-conforming invoices, plus a rule requiring the PoS vendor to have its place of business in Sindh. It is OCR'd from an image scan, single-source, self-flagged UNVERIFIED. Under one-restaurant-installed-software this is the restaurant's problem; under SaaS the vendor is the supplier for every tenant. This needs a Pakistani tax lawyer before a Karachi tenant is signed, and it is a business-model input, not an engineering ticket.
- Do tenants share one Postgres or get one per tenant? The kernel is org-scoped by column with a deliberate architectural refusal — no ledger table may carry a foreign key to the org registry, because events exist under org ids no registry row names and a constraint would refuse ingest for exactly those orgs (refusing a sale already rung). That ruling is sound and it also means row-level isolation is the ONLY isolation. `services/api/src/__acceptance__/tenant-isolation.test.ts` is currently the entire defence, and it already found one cross-tenant write. Whether that is sufficient is a founder/risk call.
- Is DEC-MONEY-004 (tips) still ratified, and does it land before or after the money model is ported? It is ratified at FULL tips and forbids a tip field until `tip.pooled`/`tip.paid_out` enter the 01 §4 catalog — but a tip is drawer cash outside `billed_total`, so the shift-cash fold is being built in front of a ratified change to its own arithmetic. A rewrite is the cheap moment to do this; doing it later is another DEC-MONEY-007.
- What happens to packages/escpos's K-8? No printer has ever been attached. 368 tests and 12,418 lines of test code describe emitted bytes and a software page, and the package's own header says nothing in it is evidence about a kitchen. `RESTOS_PRINT_TO_FILE` renders PDFs from our own encoder's interpretation, which is not independent evidence. Its first simulated document already produced an unresolved finding (quantity column at 48 dots vs a 24-dot line feed — either real heads expand the line or the layout is wrong, and only hardware separates them). A SaaS selling to restaurants must resolve this before the first paying tenant prints.

### Adversarial verdicts

- **CONFIRMED** — The auditor has ONE unsupplied leg and it is the one specs/20 §4.2 calls its headline: the read-model diff. Nothing in the cloud maintains a projection to diff against.
  - Verified at the cited lines and beyond the cited lines. packages/auditor/src/auditor.ts:99-110 is exactly the @unreached-owed JSDoc block and :111 is `read_model?: ReadModelInput` — the quoted text is verbatim, including "runs five legs and NOT this one" and the refold-against-a-refold sentence. Supply check by property, not by one symbol: the ONLY non-test call of runAuditor( in the repo is services/jobs/src/index.ts:147, `runAuditor({ db, org_id })` — no read_model; every other supply site is under __acceptance__ (auditor-fix-round, auditor-report-contract, auditor-readmodel-diff). services/jobs/src/index.ts:70-73 independently states the diff leg is not driven and that the other five legs run on every pass, matching the six-member AuditorCheck union at auditor.ts:64-73. specs/20-testing §4.2's bolded headline sentence is indeed "refold the entire ledger from raw events with the current fold code and diff against the incrementally-maintained read models and last-reported device states", so "headline" is accurate. Cloud-projection check: services/sync-gateway/src/day-ledger.ts is a raw kernel.events window read whose header says the service "SERVES; it does not interpret" and that no fold lives there; the only maintained gateway projection is the catalog (catalog.ts / publish-http.ts). `pnpm seams:check` runs clean (exit 0) and its owed register lists packages/auditor/src/auditor.ts (1) — exactly one owed marker, confirming ONE unsupplied leg. Only nuance, not enough to amend: the cloud does maintain a catalog projection, but nothing in OpenOrderRow/KitchenQueueRow shape, so "nothing to diff against" stands for this leg. Severity 'major' is fair — the FR calls the Auditor the single highest-value correctness artifact and dates it Wave 0.
- **REFUTED** — packages/sync-client is high-quality and low SaaS-independence — 7,781 production LOC, 21,656 test LOC, 743 tests, all of it in service of an offline-first LAN mesh that is hosted by nothing today.
  - The metrics are right and the load-bearing claim is false. Numbers re-measured and confirmed: prod 7781 LOC (non-test .ts under src), test 21656 LOC (*.test.ts), src/folds/merge.ts 1521 LOC, and `pnpm -C packages/sync-client test` gave `Test Files 66 passed (66) / Tests 743 passed (743)`, REAL_EXIT=0. But (1) THE MARKER DOES NOT EXIST: `grep -an unreached` over src/mesh-session.ts, src/hub-election.ts, src/transport-ws.ts returns no @unreached-owed at all — only @unreached-by-design at mesh-session.ts:39 (01-F13 re-election ASSERTION BUDGET) and transport-ws.ts:227 (DEFAULT_RECONNECT_MS tuning default), different markers about different things. (2) THE MESH IS HOSTED, BY TWO PRODUCTION APPS: apps/pos-electron/src/main/mesh.ts:147 and apps/pass-kds/src/main/mesh.ts:141 both call createMeshSession(...), and pos-electron wires it in shipping code at src/main/index.ts:69 (`import { createLanMesh } from "./mesh"`), :667 (`const mesh = createLanMesh({ store, lan, onChanged: notifyRenderer })`), :668 will-quit → mesh.stop(), :678 mesh.notifyAppended(), :825 reachability; mesh.ts's own header says it "is the host packages/sync-client/src/mesh-session.ts had been owed since Wave 0". (3) THE CITED seams:check EVIDENCE IS FALSE AGAINST THIS TREE: a clean run (exit 0, 240 modules / 621 value exports / 18 optional seams) lists 21 @unreached-owed markers across 14 files and packages/sync-client appears NOWHERE in it. The only surviving sources of the quoted string are AGENTS.md:5 and two plans/wave-1/ docs — stale prose the finding mistook for a measurement. (4) The composition characterisation is wrong independently of hosting: mesh-session + hub-election + transport-ws are 994 of 7781 prod LOC (~13%); the bulk — device-store.ts, cloud-session.ts, folds/, pin-session.ts, catalog*.ts, storage*.ts — serves the local durable store, Argon2id PIN sessions and the CLOUD session, all running in production today. Corrected claim: packages/sync-client is 7781 prod / 21656 test LOC with 743 passing tests; its LAN mesh (~13% of the code) is now hosted by both Electron apps, and the majority of the package backs the offline-first local ledger and the live cloud path rather than the mesh.
